The only dynamic Linux binary is roc-ray, which has only a glibc target: `libc.so.6`, `libm.so.6`, `libX11.so.6`, with no symbol versions. No platform links OpenSSL. macOS binaries link only system libraries. Windows targets list only Windows system libraries. A tool records its libraries (R-T8). |
| Q17 | Can Talon build roc from a GitHub archive with no `.git`? | Yes. Tested on `2664a27` (nightly 10-12) with Zig 0.17.0: `zig build roc -Doptimize=ReleaseFast -Dcompiler-version=git-2664a278` passes. `roc version` prints `git-2664a278`, and `roc check` works. Without the flag, the code (`getCompilerVersionGit`) gives `release-fast-no-git` (not tested). So git is not a build dependency (CLI 6.6, R-R3). |
| Q19 | Split a tool-neutral `deps.toml` out of `talon.toml`, so that package authors who do not use Talon can adopt it? | No. (1) The tool-neutral manifest already exists: the header. roc reads it, and any tool can read it. (2) Talon reads no `talon.toml` of a dependency (R-I1). Its ranges are the intent of one project (4.3), and roc MVS ignores ranges (section 2). So a neutral file gives other projects and other tools nothing to use. (3) A package author already has nothing to adopt: `talon.toml` is optional (G2), and a package with only a header works with Talon. (4) A second file adds a merge rule (which file wins for a key) and one more name to learn. (5) A neutral name with no agreement from the Roc project is a Talon file with another name. If the Roc project defines a manifest later, Talon reads it. The `[dependencies]` and `[toolchain]` tables have no Talon-only syntax, so that move is mechanical. |
| Q18 | Can 3 `roc build` runs share one compiler cache at the same time? | Yes. 100 iterations of 3 different apps, 50 on an empty cache and 50 on a warm cache, 2 runs: 600 of 600 builds pass, with the same output and the same bytes as a build that ran alone (`conformance/build-share/`). The build weight of 4 slots stays (Resolution 4). Found on the way: a warm build gives other bytes than a cold build, with the same output (R-T4). |
| Q20 | Before the registry exists, where does the sha256 of a compiler download come from, and how does Talon read a GitHub release as a package candidate? | The GitHub REST API. Checked on 2026-10-15 with `gh api repos/roc-lang/nightlies/releases`: each asset of the nightlies has a `digest` field (`sha256:<hex>`), and the asset names follow `roc_nightly-<target>-<date>-<sha7>` (CLI 6.2, R-CV1 to R-CV5). Package releases (`roc-lang/basic-cli`, `lukewilliamboswell/roc-parser`) have one `<hash>.tar.zst` asset next to other assets, and basic-cli marks its `0.26.0-rc1` and `0.26.0-rc2` tags as `prerelease`. roc reads the version as the one `X.Y.Z` run anywhere in the URL, and the text around it is part of the group (`roc:src/base/url.zig:152-203`). So R-DI3 to R-DI5 (CLI 7.3) select the asset, skip prereleases, and get the group from the tag. |
| Q22 | How does Talon find the project file, and how does a user name another one? | The walk of CLI 1, "The project" (R-PD1 to R-PD8): the nearest `main.roc` or workspace member, up to the repository root (`.git`), the home directory or `/`. The global flag `--file <path>` names another file. It replaces `--member`. The proxy keeps the target file of roc (R-PD7). `talon/projects.json` records each project that a command or the proxy uses (R-PD9 to R-PD13). |
| Q23 | One repository for all generic packages, or one for each? | One for each (R-GP2). The name `owner/repo` (4.1), the default alias, the short name and the `direct` index take one package for each repository. roc alone would accept a tag prefix (`research/roc-http-fetch/`). |
| Q24 | Does roc fetch a package from an `http://` URL? (`TESTING.md` TQ4) | Only from a loopback host: `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`. Any other `http://` URL is the error "insecure package url", with no connection. Tested on `nightly-2026-10-09-258ab27` (`research/roc-http-fetch/`), and in `roc:src/unbundle/download.zig`. So a test with a `github.com` URL pre-fills `packages/<hash>/` (CLI 1, "Packages in tests"). |
| Q32 | Which exit code does `tln publish` give when it has no OIDC token (Registry 4, R-AP27)? | 2 (`usage`, CLI 3, R-EC2). The command runs where it cannot work, as `tln probe --share` with no registry (Resolution 4, R-PB6). The fix is in the workflow file (`permissions: id-token: write`), and the message names it. `tln yank` outside a GitHub Actions job (Registry 7.2, R-YK3), and `tln publish` and `tln yank` with no registry, also give 2. Not 18 (`environment`): it covers a program that is not on `PATH`, and a new meaning would change its kind (R-J6). Not a new code: this is a usage failure, not a new kind (R-E5). |
| Q33 | What does `tln` do with a header hash that the closure answer lists in `unknown` (Registry 4, R-AP17)? | Registry 4.1, R-AP28. When the answer has the label with another hash, Talon warns (the case of R-PU19). Else a `github.com` release URL gets one ingest (R-AP9), and Talon asks again once, as for a full name (Q34, CLI 2, R-AD35). Any other URL, a failed ingest, or `--snapshot log:N` (R-AD36): Talon reads the facts from the archive, as in `direct` mode (CLI 7.3, R-DI7), and the entry is an anchor when the registry has no release of its group (as R-DI11). The header value always stays in its domain (Resolution 2, R-C9). Not stop: a hand edit (Design 4.1, R-ID7) must still work. A URL on a private host is never sent. |
| Q34 | What does `tln add owner/repo` (a full name) do when `GET /v1/packages/{owner}/{repo}/releases` answers 404 (Registry 4, R-AP1), and the repository has releases on GitHub that the registry did not index? | Talon reads the releases from GitHub, sends an ingest (R-AP9) for each candidate, and asks the registry again (CLI 2, R-AD35). `tln tool install` and `tln x` with a full name do the same. A private repository, a name that GitHub does not know, or no candidate gives exit code 2. With `--snapshot log:N`, Talon sends no ingest and stops with exit code 2 (R-AD36). Reason: one command uses one index (CLI 7.3, R-DI14), so the log snapshot, the matrix and the compat bot cover the new releases. Go modules have the same model: the module proxy fetches a module on its first request. Rejected: a GitHub read for that group only, as `direct` (a second index in one resolve, with no log index for `--snapshot log:N`); stop and name an ingest (Talon has no ingest command, and the user would need the asset URLs). |
| Q35 | One release comes in by the webhook and by `tln publish`. What happens when the asset comes after the release is published, and when the webhook indexes first? | GitHub sends no event for an asset upload (the `release` actions only, checked on 2026-10-16). So the webhook reads the release again after 1, 5, 15 and 60 minutes (R-PU13). Each path is idempotent for a label (R-PU14). Provenance moves to an add-only `provenance` table with one row for each path (R-PU15), so a later publish adds its `oidc` row and its author facts with no change to the release row (R-PU16, R-RE6). A release that stops being a candidate on GitHub is withdrawn (R-PU17), so both modes see the same candidates (CLI 7.3, R-DI14). |
| Q36 | Who sets the workflows that can publish, and which OIDC claims does the Worker check? | No per-repository config in P2. A fixed rule: a token of a public repository, for the tag of the asset URL, from a `release`, `push` or `workflow_dispatch` run, with the registry as `aud` (R-TP1, R-TP3). The tag ref stops `pull_request_target` and `workflow_run`, and a fork pull request gets no token (R-TP4). The `repos` table binds a name to the GitHub repository id and owner id against repo-jacking (R-TP5), as PyPI does. |
| Q37 | Which packages does a short name match? | The rule of CLI 2, R-SC8, as an exact set from `GET /v1/packages?short=` (R-SN1): repo name, or repo name minus a leading `roc-`, ASCII case-insensitive, `github.com` only (R-SN3), yanked and withdrawn releases included (R-SN2). The ranked `?q=` results are for messages only (R-SN5, CLI 2, R-AD34). |
| Q38 | Does the registry list prebuilt binaries of other tools in P2? | No. P3 (CLI 6.4, R-T22). The package hash fixes the source, not a binary, and two builds of one build id can give other bytes (Q18). So a prebuilt binary needs a check that ties it to the release: a GitHub artifact attestation. Attestation checks are P3 (`cloudflare-workers/API.md` RN12). |
| Q39 | How does a yank work? | P3. `PUT` and `DELETE /v1/releases/{log_index}/yank` (R-YK1). The log index names a release on any host. Auth: a `workflow_dispatch` OIDC token of the repository, or the operator key, and the operator only for other hosts (R-YK2, R-TP8). `tln yank <tag>` (R-YK3). An add-only `yanks` history, and no log leaf (R-YK4). The current yank state applies also at a snapshot (R-YK5), as in Cargo. |
| Q40 | What happens to the binding of a name when its repository is renamed, transferred, deleted or made again, and how does an owner move a package to another repository? | Registry 7.3. The registry trusts the owner of the recorded repository: a write is accepted when the repository id or the owner id is equal to the binding, and refused (403, with the issues page of the Talon repository `roc-talon/talon`, the Worker setting `ISSUES_URL`) only when both are different (R-TP9, R-TP12). A rename gives a successor link at once (R-TP10). A deleted or private repository makes the name `missing`: no release is a candidate, the binding and the short name stay, so another account cannot take the name (R-TP11). In P3, an owner sets a successor with `PUT /v1/packages/{owner}/{repo}/successor` from a `workflow_dispatch` job of the new repository, or with an offer from the old one (R-AP31, R-TP15). The Worker checks the index, the kind and the module names (`exposes`), and the client checks the rest with the whole-app check (R-PU28). A move changes no URL: a person names the alias to follow it. A later takeover of the owner account is an accepted risk (Design 8, R-SE14). Rejected: the operator for each rebind (it treats the owner as an attacker), a symbol check in the Worker (it needs the compiler), and an automatic follow of a move (a takeover could then change headers). |
| Q30 | How is an ingest (`POST /v1/releases` with no token, Registry 4, R-AP9) kept from abuse? | No login: ordinary commands send an ingest, also in CI. A rate limit for each IP address, a limit of new names for each owner and day, the archive size limit of Q28, and the rules that exist (`github.com` only, public only, one release for each label). Registry 7, R-PU34. User hints have their own rate limit (Registry 6.1, R-CT11). |
| Q41 | Can `tln` share probe results by itself, and what does the registry keep? | Yes, when the user turns sharing on: opt-in at the first interactive run (prompt R-NI16, CLI 1, R-SH11), a Consent prompt that `--yes` does not answer (CLI 4, R-NM12), or with `tln share on`. The installers do not ask. Reads stay open for each user. `tln` shares unit checks (pass, fail, crash) and the builds of public tool apps, through the filters of CLI 1, R-SH3. It never shares the baseline check, the whole-app check, app builds, the project file or bundle files. A CI job and `DO_NOT_TRACK` stop it (R-SH2). The id is stable across off and on (R-SH5). The registry keeps only an HMAC of the id (R-CT3). It keeps one row for each contributor and pair until its compiler is 12 months old or a delete (R-CT6, R-CT15), and computes the totals from the rows and the totals of the trim (R-CT10). The write is one D1 batch with a trigger, tested on local D1 (R-CT4, `research/d1-contrib/`). A GitHub login (device flow, no scope, no revoke) gives a JWT and the tracker of the account, and moves the rows of the machine to the account (R-CT8 to R-CT10). With no login, the tracker is on the machine only (R-SH6). A user can delete their rows, but not the counts, which hold no id (R-CT7). Rate limit per IP address (R-CT11). |
| Q42 | Should `tln` build a fixed smoke-test app for each (platform hash, nightly, target) and share the result? | Yes, from the start of sharing (Resolution 4, R-SM1 to R-SM5). Talon makes the app from the platform header (R-SM2), and builds it once for each key on each machine, only with sharing on, after the command did its work (R-SM3, R-SM4). It holds no code of the user. Measured: the same result as a real app on 34 of 34 targets, under 0.5 s (`research/smoke-app/`). The bot also runs them for each target (Q43). |
| Q43 | Does the compat bot run the smoke build (Resolution 4, R-SM1) of each platform for each target? | Yes, for each target in the `targets` of the platform, on one Linux runner: roc links with the files of the platform only (`research/smoke-app/`). On each new nightly for the platforms of the bot set (Registry 6, R-MX11), and on the nightlies where the unit check of a new platform release passed (R-MX10). A `bot` smoke fail on the target of a build is a real fail of ladder step 3, so the solver needs no build to learn it. A pass does not replace the build of the app (Resolution 4, R-SM6). An error that comes from the build machine (`WindowsSDKNotFound`) gives no row (R-SM7). Smoke rows have no sweep. |
| Q44 | Which hashes does the compat bot check on each new nightly? | The bot set (Registry 6, R-MX11), not each indexed hash: the newest release of each series of each group, for the series with a release in the 18 months before the nightly, and always the newest series of a group. Packages and platforms only. The size follows the groups and their active series, not the releases (30 of 46 in `conformance/compat-sweep/`). A sweep covers the set at its bound (R-MX4, R-RE8), and the guard trigger and the work route `GET /v1/sweeps/{tag}` (R-AP39) use one SQL text. The registry counts the changed hashes of each pair of adjacent swept nightlies (`transitions`, R-MX12). With 1 changed hash as the threshold, the set finds the same 5 boundaries as all 46 hashes (R-EP3). The bot also checks the 200 pairs with the most user hints (R-MX13). A hash outside the set gets inferred facts, user probes and hints (R-MX14): it can cost a probe, never a wrong header (R-EP8). Test: `research/bot-set/` (13 cases on local D1 and SQLite). Rejected: each hash on each nightly (CI time and downloads grow with each release); a set from the closure queries (a write on each read, and a log of what people use). |
| Q45 | Does P2 keep contributor ids, the tracker and GitHub accounts, and for how long? | Yes, with one 64-bit id for each contributor (Registry 6.1, R-CT3): a machine is the HMAC of its random id with the top bit set (negative), and a GitHub account is the GitHub user id (positive), so the two kinds never collide. `contributors` is the index of the accounts, and `links` ties a machine to its account. A login moves the rows of the machine to the account (R-CT8). One `contributions` row for each contributor and pair is kept until its compiler is 12 months old, or the user deletes it (about 45 bytes a row, R-CT6). Then a daily trim adds the classes of the rows to `contributor_totals` and deletes the rows and their hints (R-CT15). The age is the `published_at` of the compiler: it needs no new column, and a report about an old compiler is old. So there is no fold: the tracker computes the totals from the rows when it is read, plus the totals of the trim (R-CT5, R-CT10). The daily limit bounds a flood (R-CT13). Any GitHub token of the account proves the account (Design 8, R-SE16). Test: `research/d1-contrib/` (22 cases, local D1 and SQLite). Rejected: anonymous counts only (no history of who sent what); a 30-day limit on the rows with no totals (the history is lost); the time of the first report of a pair as its age (a new column, and a new report about an old compiler would look new). |
| Q46 | With D1 read replicas, the closure that `tln` sends again after an ingest (R-AP28 case 2) can read a replica that does not have the ingest yet. How does the second read see the ingest? | Each write answer has the header `Talon-Bookmark` with the D1 bookmark, `tln` sends the largest one with its next read, and the Worker reads with `with_session!(After(bookmark))` (decided 2026-10-17, Registry R-AP41 row 5, R-AP28). |
| Q47 | Does one D1 batch of N statements count as 1 query or N toward the limit of queries for each invocation? | The limit counts D1 calls, not statements: one `batch()` or one `exec()` of any size is 1 call, and an invocation can make 1,000 D1 calls. `fetch()` subrequests have a separate limit. R-PU23 bounds the rows of a run by both limits; 500 rows is 501 D1 calls (decided 2026-10-17, measured on the edge, Registry section 8, "D1 call limit and placement"). |
| Q48 | RN16 with D1 rows runs archive work in a cron, but RN17 says that a cron does no archive work (`cloudflare-workers/API.md`). Which variant of the index retries? | The queue variant of RN16: Queues retry with a delay and send to a dead-letter queue by themselves (decided 2026-10-17, Registry R-PU35, R-PU36). |
| Q49 | Smart Placement or the default placement for the registry Worker? | The default placement, with D1 read replication on, and each read route in a read session (R-AP41 row 5). No Smart Placement and no `placement.region`. Reasons: read routes make 1 D1 call (R-AP41), and a remote placement adds one client hop (35 to 246 ms) and removes the replica gain (22 ms against 161 ms from `DFW`). A separate write Worker placed near the primary would save 100 to 190 ms on a far publish: not needed now (decided 2026-10-17, measured on the edge, Registry section 8, "D1 call limit and placement"). |
| Q14 | Values for the real registry app: the memory budget, the deadlines and `heapCheckEvery` | Deferred to the P2 load test (Registry 8). Until then the platform defaults apply: `isolateBytes` 128 MB, `jsReserveBytes` 48 MB (so 80 MB for wasm), `heapCheckEvery` 100, `resumeCheck` `"always"`, 254 tasks, and the deadlines of `cloudflare-workers/API.md` 10.2: 120 s for one wait of a `Fetch` task before the response, 15 min for a `Scheduled`, `Queue` or `Alarm` task, 30 s after the response (decided 2026-10-18). The load test runs on Workers Paid with `cpu_ms`, and step 12g sets `heapCheckEvery`. |
| Q25 | How does a publish make the edge caches drop the old version list? | No purge across colos. The Worker drops the entries of the group in its own colo, and other colos follow after `max-age` (60 s). A client that must see the newest state sends the standard request header `Cache-Control: no-cache` (and `Pragma: no-cache`), and the Worker then skips the Cache API. A 404 is not cached. `tln` sends it after an ingest and with each `Talon-Bookmark` (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP43). |
| Q26 | Who writes the tiles and the checkpoint, when two publishes run at once, or an R2 write fails after the D1 commit? | One idempotent step, after each publish and from the catch-up cron: tiles first, then the checkpoint with an R2 conditional `put` on the etag, only to a larger size (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-SG7). P3 (Q55). |
| Q27 | How does the publish batch insert the leaf only when it inserts the release, and stop when the label has another hash? | A `BEFORE INSERT` trigger with `RAISE(ABORT, 'label-conflict')`, `INSERT OR IGNORE` for the same label and hash, `log_index = max + 1`, and an `AFTER INSERT` trigger that writes the leaf (`research/d1-sweep/`). Changed by Q79: the Worker writes the leaf before the release, and the trigger only checks it. Tested on local D1, SQLite and remote D1 (2026-10-18, `research/d1-remote/`): the same results and the same error text, and a failed statement rolls back the whole batch. |
| Q28 | What is the largest archive that the registry indexes, and does it need the paid plan? | The registry runs on Workers Paid, and CPU does not limit the size. Memory does: the Worker reads the asset as a stream (BLAKE3, zstd, tar) and keeps only the header and the paths. Limits against abuse of the ingest: 100 MB packed and a zstd window of 32 MB (first guesses) (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-PU38). The window limit is 8 MiB from Q57. |
| Q29 | Should large reads (`POST /v1/closure`) be JSON that SQLite makes? | No. The closure answer is runs in a window of compilers (Q50): 9 KB for a held nightly and 49 KB for all compilers of 3 years, so typed Roc rows are cheap (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP41 row 6, R-AP42). |
| Q31 | Should `tln publish` or the registry run `roc bump`? | `tln publish` runs `roc bump --old <previous release> --expect <version>` and warns on a mismatch. It does not stop, because the asset is already public and other paths index it. The registry runs no compiler (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-PU39). |
| Q50 | The closure answer sent every matrix row of each hash on all compilers: 1.8 MB for 3 years of a closure of 26 hashes, on each resolve. What does the client need? | The facts of the window W of compilers that the command can choose (a held nightly: one compiler), made larger to the ends of the registry epochs that W touches, as runs, plus the nearest code result on each side, and the epochs of the registry in place of the `sweeps` and `transitions` rows. The client gets the same facts in W as from all rows: 400 random cases on local D1 and SQLite, and 4 mutations fail (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP16, R-AP17, R-AP42, `research/closure-runs/`). |
| Q51 | How can the registry API change, and how does the registry tell an old `tln` that it is too old? | A change that an older client can ignore stays in `/v1`: a new route, field or problem type, and a new optional request field when an answer that ignores it holds the same facts or more. A removed or renamed field, a changed meaning, a new required field or a new value in a closed set (`result`, `state`) needs `/v2`, and one Worker serves both for at least 12 months. `tln` ignores unknown answer fields and maps an unknown problem type by its status. `tln` sends `User-Agent: tln/<version>`, and 2 Worker settings refuse old versions on the write routes or on all routes with 400 `unsupported-client` (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP44, R-AP45). |
| Q52 | `GET /v1/toolchains` grows by about 365 KB a year and is read again on each new nightly. How does the client keep it? | Incremental: `?after=<log_index>`, pages of at most 1000 rows with `more`. Each row has its own log index and never changes, so `tln` keeps the rows in `talon/index/` and asks only for new ones (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP5, R-AP47). |
| Q53 | What are the size limits of a request? | A body of 256 KB, 300 `groups` and 1000 `hashes` in a closure, 50 `short` values, 500 hint rows (first guesses), so each JSON parameter stays under the 100 KB of one D1 statement. Over a limit: 413 `too-large` with `field` and `limit`, before any D1 read. `tln` gives exit code 1 (decided 2026-10-18, Registry R-AP46). |
| Q54 | Does the `worker/` header name the platform by its release URL, or by a path to the submodule `cloudflare-workers/` (Registry R-WH12)? | The release URL, from 0.0.2. The registry tests the real release path, and one header hash pins the host and the shim. A platform fix waits for a platform release (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-WH12). |
| Q55 | Are the log tiles and the signed checkpoint part of P2 (Registry R-SG7, R-PU1) or of P3 (section 7)? | P3, as section 7 says. P2 writes the log leaves in D1 only (R-SG2, R-SG3, R-SG5). The tile writer, the log routes, the tile catch-up job and the R2 step of R-PU1 are P3. The signing key, its algorithm and its setting name are given when P3 starts (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-SG4, R-SG7, R-AP11, R-PU1, R-PU35). |
| Q56 | The platform has no `Ctx.respond!`. How does the webhook do its work after the 202 (Registry R-PU11)? | The webhook checks the signature, sends one message for each event to the index queue (R-PU13), and answers 202. The queue consumer does the work, with 15 min. Reason: `Response.after` gives only 30 s after the answer (`cloudflare-workers/API.md` 10.2), and a download of 100 MB, BLAKE3, zstd and the D1 batch can take longer. The message holds GitHub ids, not a name (R-PU32), so the consumer reads the repository by its id (R-PU31). `Response.after` is the name of the work after the answer in the design (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-PU11, R-PU31, R-AP41, R-SG7). |
| Q57 | Which zstd window does the archive read allow (Registry R-PU38 row 3, Q28)? | 8 MiB, the hard limit of `Native.ZstdStream` in platform 0.0.2. Real bundles use 2 MiB (basic-cli 0.26.0 and 2 other large bundles, `zstd -lv`). A larger window gives 422 `not-a-candidate` (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-PU38). |
| Q58 | Platform 0.0.2 has no tar reader (6.2, R-GA2). Where is it? | Changed by Q78: the generic package `roc-tar` (R-PK8). zstd and BLAKE3 stay in the host (`Native`) (decided 2026-11-01, R-GA2, Registry R-PU38). |
| Q59 | Platform 0.0.2 is built and tested with `nightly-2026-10-31-f7bef6b`, and the CLI pins `nightly-2026-10-14-38a3029` (`TESTING.md` TW28). Which compiler builds `worker/`? | A second tag, `ROC_TAG_WORKER` = `nightly-2026-10-31-f7bef6b` in `scripts/env.sh` beside `ROC_TAG`, for `worker/` and its `roc:` pin. `packages/records` builds on both nightlies. Rejected: the whole repository on 10-31 (a move of the CLI for the Worker), the Worker on 10-14 (a nightly that the platform does not test) (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-WH13, R-PK7, `TESTING.md` TW28). |
| Q60 | The key of `provenance` is (release, via) in Registry R-RE9 and (group, version, via) in R-PU15. The key of `yanks` is (release, seq) in R-RE10 and (group, version, seq) in R-YK4. Which one? | The release log index in both: (release, via) and (release, seq). A release is named by its log index (R-IK6) (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-PU15, R-YK4, R-RE9, R-RE10). |
| Q61 | Is `transitions` a `WITHOUT ROWID` table (Registry R-IK8)? | Yes. It has a composite key and small rows, the reason of R-IK8, and `research/bot-set/` makes it so (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-IK8). |
| Q62 | Does an ingest answer 202 (Registry R-AP9)? | No. Q30 has no queue for an ingest, so an ingest answers as a publish does (R-AP8) (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-AP9). |
| Q63 | Which settings, secrets and bindings does the Worker have, and what is the origin of the JWT (Registry R-CT9)? | One table in Registry 8 (R-WH14). New names, as a proposal for the review: `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `GITHUB_APP_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_KEY`, the bindings `DB` (D1), `LOG` (R2, P3), `INDEX` and `INDEX_DLQ` (queues), `RL_INGEST`, `RL_HINTS`, `RL_LOGIN`. `AUDIENCE` is also the `iss` and `aud` of the JWT (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-WH14, R-CT9). |
| Q64 | Where does the clock of the Worker come from, so that a test can fix it (Registry R-WH4, `TESTING.md` TW2)? | The Worker reads the time only with `Time.now_ms!` (`Date.now()`). The test entry sets `Date.now` to the time of the case. The shim option `now` drives only the task deadlines and the memory budget, so it is not this clock (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-WH4). |
| Q65 | Which type does a time column in D1 have (Registry R-RE3, R-RE7, R-RE25)? | INTEGER, milliseconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC, for each time column. `hint_days.day` stays a count of days (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-RE26, R-RE2, R-RE3, R-RE5, R-RE7, R-RE25). |
| Q66 | Where is the compat bot workflow, and what does it run (Registry R-MX1, section 7 P2 exit)? | `.github/workflows/compat-bot.yml` in this repository, on `schedule` and `workflow_dispatch`, on one Linux runner. It runs `tln probe` (Resolution 4, R-PB) and a small bot script for the registry calls (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-MX15, R-MX1, R-TP7). |
| Q67 | With which token does the Worker read the tag list of `roc-talon/talon` (Registry R-CT12)? | The installation token of R-PU25. The App is installed on `roc-talon`, so the token reads that repository. A read with no token has 60 requests an hour for each IP address (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-CT12). |
| Q68 | Does the CLI move its `compat.db` code into `packages/records` (R-PK7) in P2? | Not in the first registry slices. `records` first has the API types and the records of the registry. A slice of the client track of P2 moves the `compat.db` rows when the CLI uses `records` (decided 2026-11-01, R-PK7). |
| Q69 | In which order does the Worker check a request, and what does a known path give for a method that it does not serve? | Path (404), method (405 `method-not-allowed` with `Allow`, RFC 9110), client version (400), size (413), media type only when there is a body (415, `application/json` with no charset or UTF-8), parse (400), then the route. No `crash` at run time (decided 2026-11-01, slice 101, Registry R-AP48, R-AP21, R-AP23). |
| Q70 | Which JSON members does a release, an artifact and a dep have on the wire (Registry R-RE1, R-RE2, R-RE19)? The design columns `exposes`, `platform` and `targets` are reserved words of Roc. | One table in Registry R-AP49. A release: `log_index`, `hash`, `group`, `series`, `version`, `url`, `tag`, `roc_version`, `repo`, `published_at`, `yanked`, `yank_reason`, `withdrawn` (the R-PU17 value as text, not a boolean). An artifact: `hash`, `kind`, `exposed`, `platform_hash`, `roc_pin`, `size`, `deps`, `target_names`. A dep: `alias`, `hash`, `group`, `series`, `version`, `url`. The names that differ from the columns need no rename in a Roc codec and are clearer. `null` stays on the wire where the table allows it (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-AP49, R-RE1, R-RE2, R-RE19). |
| Q71 | Which form does a time have in the API (Registry R-RE26)? | A JSON integer, milliseconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC, the value of the D1 column, for each time member (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-AP50, R-RE26). |
| Q72 | Which target names have the codes 11 to 20 (Registry R-IK7)? | `x64v1mac`, `x64v1win`, `x64v1mingw`, `x64v1freebsd`, `x64v1openbsd`, `x64v1netbsd`, `x64v1musl`, `x64v1glibc`, `x64v1linux`, `x64v1elf`, from `RocTarget` in `roc:src/target/mod.zig` (lines 419-428 on `258ab27`, 434-443 on `38a3029` and `f7bef6b`). The list of 35 is the same on the 3 nightlies (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-IK7). |
| Q73 | Which values does the `kind` of an artifact have (Registry R-RE1)? | `app`, `package`, `platform`, the header kinds of a project file (CLI 1, R-PD8). An app release is a bundle (section 2). A closed set (R-AP44), codes 0, 1, 2 (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-AP49, R-IK7, R-RE1). |
| Q74 | Which "sizes" does an artifact keep (Registry R-RE1)? | One size: the bytes of the `.tar.zst` asset, the count of R-PU38 row 2. It gives the total of the download progress (CLI 7.5, R-DA6). No unpacked size: no rule uses it (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-RE1, R-AP49, R-PU38, CLI R-DA6). |
| Q75 | Which tags have a nightly id (Registry R-IK4): a sha that is not 7 hex digits, an upper-case sha, a date that does not exist? | Only the form of the real tags: `nightly-`, 4-digit year, a 2-digit month or the English month name, a 2-digit day of a date that exists, exactly 7 lower-case hex digits, a date from 1970-01-01 to 3405-06-14. Each other text is `NotANightly`. All 348 tags of `roc-lang/nightlies` on 2026-11-01 have the form. roc reads more in a pin, but such a pin has no registry facts (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-IK4). |
| Q76 | A request or an answer misses a member that the design does not call optional. Is it an error or an empty value? | An error. The Worker answers 400 `bad-request`. `tln` does not use the answer and exits with 15 (CLI R-E6). A `null` where the rule does not allow it, a wrong type and an unknown closed-set value are the same. A missing optional member, or a `null` one, has no value. A member that a later Worker adds in `/v1` is optional for the reader (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-AP51, R-AP44, CLI R-E6). |
| Q77 | What does a client do with a problem `type` that it does not know (Registry R-AP44)? | The decoder keeps it, with its text, `status`, `title` and `detail`. `tln` maps it by the HTTP status (CLI R-E6) and gives the text in `error.details.type` (CLI R-J7) (decided 2026-11-01, slice 104, Registry R-AP52, R-AP21, R-AP44). |
| Q78 | Does R-GP1 limit the count of generic packages, and where is the tar reader of the Worker? | No fixed count: the generic packages are a list, not a limit (R-GP1). The tar reader is the generic package `roc-tar` (R-PK8) in `Karakatiza666/roc-tar`, used by release URL, when its measured cost in the Worker is immaterial against the budget of Registry R-PU38. zstd and BLAKE3 stay in `Native` (decided by the user 2026-11-01, R-GP1, R-PK8, R-GA2, Registry R-PU38). |
| Q79 | A leaf holds text forms (Registry R-IK1), but `releases` has only ids, and SQL cannot make the base58 of a hash. Who writes the leaf text, so that a leaf is still written only with a new release, in the same batch (Q27)? | The Worker computes the text and sends the leaf statement before the release statement, in the same batch: `INSERT INTO log ... SELECT (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(idx), 0) + 1 FROM log), ... WHERE NOT EXISTS` (the label). The release takes `(SELECT MAX(idx) FROM log)` as its `log_index`. An `AFTER INSERT` trigger on `releases` and on `toolchains` raises `leaf-missing` when the leaf of `NEW.log_index` is not there. The leaf `time` is the time of the write. Rejected: ids in the leaf (R-IK1), `WHERE changes() > 0` (no check that a release has its leaf), a staging table (decided 2026-11-01, slice 103, Registry R-SG8, R-RE5, R-PU9, `research/leaf-text/`). |
| Q80 | What is the length of "18 months" in the bot set (Registry R-MX11 row 3)? | 548 days, 47,347,200,000 ms, back from the `published_at` of the nightly. A release with `published_at >= t - 47347200000` is in the window, also at the edge and also after the nightly (decided 2026-11-01, slice 103, Registry R-MX11, R-MX16). |
| Q81 | Do all `toolchains` rows of one nightly have one `published_at`, and which time and order does SQL use for a nightly (Registry R-RE4, R-MX16)? | Yes: the rows of a tag come from one GitHub release in one transaction (R-PU8, R-PU9), and each row gets the `published_at` of the release. SQL takes `MIN(published_at)` of the rows of the nightly. The order is the date of the id, then that time, then the id (R-NS1). A nightly with no row has no window time (decided 2026-11-01, slice 103, Registry R-MX16, R-RE4). |
| Q82 | Which values do `first_time` and `last_time` of a `compat_hints` row take (Registry R-RE12)? | `first_time` is the `time` of the hint that made the row, and it never changes (R-RE6). `last_time` is `MAX(last_time, NEW.time)`, so a write with an older time does not move it back (decided 2026-11-01, slice 103, Registry R-RE12). |
| Q83 | A sweep upsert with a bound that is not larger than the row, after a yank changed the set at that bound: does the `BEFORE INSERT` trigger raise `sweep-incomplete` (Registry R-MX4, R-MX8)? | No. The `BEFORE INSERT` trigger checks nothing when the row of (nightly, target) has a bound at least the new bound. R-MX4 says that such a sweep changes nothing, with no error, and a bot that sends a request again must not fail after a yank. A raise of the bound gets all checks (decided 2026-11-01, slice 103, Registry R-MX8). |
| Q84 | With which conflict mode does the trigger of Registry R-MX12 write a `transitions` row that exists? | `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (nightly, target) DO UPDATE`. `INSERT OR REPLACE` in a trigger body fails when the sweep statement is the update part of an upsert, because the conflict mode of the outer statement applies (slice 103, F1). The Worker also never writes `sweeps` with `INSERT OR REPLACE` (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-MX12, R-MX8). |
| Q85 | When does the Worker read the GitHub JWKS again, and is `Bearer` case-insensitive? | Max age 1 h. An unknown `kid` reads it again only when the copy is older than 60 s, else 401. The copy keeps its fetch time, so the test clock can test it. `Bearer` is case-insensitive (RFC 9110) (decided 2026-11-01, slice 108, Registry R-TP16). |
| Q86 | Can the release and toolchains statements of R-SG8 use `INSERT OR IGNORE`? | No. `OR IGNORE` drops, with no error, a row whose `log_index` is already used (a missing leaf) and a row that fails a `CHECK`. The statements are `INSERT ... SELECT ... WHERE NOT EXISTS (the same label and artifact, or the same nightly and target)`, so a sent-again request is skipped and each fault is an error (found by the slice 103 test agent, F5; decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-SG8 rows 4 to 6). |
| Q87 | What is `checked` in the answers of `GET /v1/packages/{owner}/{repo}/releases` and `GET /v1/releases?group=` (Registry R-AP1, R-AP13)? | One item `{name, checked_at, stale}` (R-AP29), not a list, because one path or one group names one repository. `null` for a group on another host and for an answer with no release (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP53). |
| Q88 | Is the path `Roc-Lang/Unicode` the same repository as `roc-lang/unicode` (Registry R-AP1)? | Yes. GitHub names are case-insensitive. The Worker makes the ASCII letters lower case before the id of R-IK2. A `group` is compared as exact bytes (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP56). |
| Q89 | `GET /v1/releases?series=4` with no `group` (Registry R-AP13, R-AP14)? | 400 `bad-request` when there is also no `after`. With `after` and no `group`, `series` is ignored: the answer holds the same facts or more (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP56). |
| Q90 | Which request headers bypass the Cache API of the Worker (Registry R-AP43)? | Only the directive `no-cache` in `Cache-Control` or `Pragma` (a list, case-insensitive). `max-age=0`, `no-store` and other directives do not: a browser sends `max-age=0` at each reload (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP55). |
| Q91 | `GET /v1/artifacts/<text that is not base58 of 32 bytes>`: 404 or 400 (Registry R-AP2)? | 404 `not-found`, with the `detail` of an unknown hash, and no D1 call. The path names no resource (R-AP48 row 1) (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP56). |
| Q92 | What is the answer of `GET /v1/artifacts/{hash}` (Registry R-AP2)? | `{artifact, releases, repos}`, with the lists in the order of R-AP53 (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP2, R-AP53). |
| Q93 | How is `artifacts.exposes` stored (Registry R-RE1)? | TEXT: a JSON array of the module names, in the order of the header (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-RE1). |
| Q94 | What is the `group` of a dep whose URL has no version (`deps.form` NULL, Registry R-RE19, R-AP49)? | The URL with no scheme and no last segment (`<hash>.tar.zst`), with no `{v}`. The Worker makes it from `url`. `group` stays a required text. No release has such a group (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP49). |
| Q95 | Which texts does `after` take (Registry R-AP5, R-AP13, R-AP14)? | Decimal digits, no leading zero (only `0` itself), at most 2^63 - 1. Else 400 `bad-request`. One text form gives one cache key, and the value fits an SQLite INTEGER (decided 2026-11-01, slices 106 and 109, Registry R-AP56). |
| Q96 | What is the form of the ETag, and what do `If-None-Match`, a 304 and the Cache API do (Registry R-AP24, R-AP43)? | A strong tag: the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of the body as 32 lower-case hex digits, in double quotes. `If-None-Match` uses the weak comparison of RFC 9110 (`*`, a list, `W/` ignored). A 304 has no body, and has `ETag` and `Cache-Control`. A 304 after a miss puts the 200 answer in the Cache API. The key is the whole URL. The same for `GET /v1/toolchains` with `max-age=300` (decided 2026-11-01, slices 106 and 109, Registry R-AP54, R-AP55). |
| Q97 | In which order are the lists of the release reads, and what is an empty answer (Registry R-AP1, R-AP2, R-AP13, R-AP14)? | `releases` in rising `log_index`, `repos` sorted by `name` (bytes), so one state gives one ETag. A group with no release and a page after the last release are 200 with empty lists. `Talon-Bookmark` has its white space cut, and an empty value is no header (decided 2026-11-01, slice 106, Registry R-AP53, R-AP55). |
| Q98 | Does `PUT /v1/toolchains/{tag}` for a tag with rows read GitHub (Registry R-AP6, R-PU9, R-PU10)? | No. D1 is read first. A tag with rows gives 200 with no GitHub call (1 D1 call). A new tag gives 2 D1 calls. A recorded row never changes, so a GitHub read cannot change the answer (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q99 | What does `PUT /v1/toolchains/{tag}` answer for a tag with no nightly id (Registry R-IK4)? | 422 `not-a-candidate`, with no D1 call and no GitHub call (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q100 | Is a compiler release with `draft` or `prerelease` true a candidate (Registry R-PU8, CLI R-CV1)? | No: 422 `not-a-candidate` for the whole tag, as R-DI3 for the client (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q101 | An asset whose `digest` is missing, `null`, or not `sha256:` and 64 lower-case hex digits (Registry R-PU8)? | It is not an asset for its host. The other hosts stay. No asset left: 422 `not-a-candidate` (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q102 | Which members does a toolchain row have on the wire (Registry R-AP5, R-RE4)? | `tag`, `target`, `asset_name`, `sha256` (bare lower-case hex), `published_at` (ms), `log_index`. No id (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP49). |
| Q103 | Another request wrote the tag between the read and the batch of `PUT /v1/toolchains/{tag}`: 201 or 200? | 200. The answer is 201 only when a `toolchains` statement of the batch has `changes` above 0 (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q104 | `GET /v1/toolchains?target=` with an empty or unknown target (Registry R-AP5)? | 200 with no rows. The text is compared exactly. roc can add a target, and a 400 would refuse a newer `tln` (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP56). |
| Q105 | A GitHub `published_at` of a compiler release in another form than `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ` (Registry R-PU8)? | The answer is not a valid release: 502 `upstream`. GitHub sends this form (decided 2026-11-01, slice 109, Registry R-AP57). |
| Q106 | The limits and checks of the archive read with `roc-tar` | Unpacked total 256 MiB, 10,000 members, path 255 bytes, header file 1 MiB. The paths that `roc unbundle` refuses, links and devices, duplicates and the strict errors give 422. Any end form is accepted, because the hash finds a cut. The header file is `main.roc`. `roc-tar` pins the nightly of `worker/` (its only user, R-GP5). Measured: 1.4 s (Node) and 4.6 s (workerd) for basic-cli 0.26.0, mostly zstd (decided 2026-11-01, Registry R-PU38 rows 5 to 7, Design R-PK8). |
| Q107 | What is the query form of several `short` values, and the shape of `matches` (Registry R-SN1, R-AP7)? | The repeated parameter `short=a&short=b`. `matches` is a list of entries `{short, matches}`, one for each `short` parameter in the order of the URL, also for equal values. `short` is the value as sent, decoded. An item has `name`, `first_log_index`, `state` and, for a replaced name only, `moved_from` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN6). |
| Q108 | A request with `short` and `q`, an empty `short` value or an empty `q` (Registry R-AP7)? | 400 `bad-request` for each, after the checks 404, 405 and 413, and before any D1 call. An empty `short` value counts for the limit of 50 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN7). |
| Q109 | Where does the Worker stop a successor chain in a match set (Registry R-AP33, R-TP14)? | With the walk of the client: at most 4 links, and before a name that is already on the walk. The end is the last name reached, with its own `state`: `moved` shows a cut chain or a cycle (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN8, R-TP14). |
| Q110 | What is `first_log_index` of a replaced item (Registry R-SN4, R-AP33)? | The smallest log index of the releases of the end and of each name that it replaces in the entry, so it exists also when the end has no release. A move is not in the log, and the old name keeps the item in the set at `--snapshot log:N` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN9, R-SN4). |
| Q111 | `moved_from` when the end is also in the set, or when 2 old names give one end (Registry R-AP33)? | It stays when the end is in the set (the set has the end once). With 2 or more old names it is the smaller by bytes (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN9). |
| Q112 | What is the "text match on the name" of `?q=` (Registry R-SN5)? | A rule without fuzzy match: rank 1 exact (the name, the repo part or the part without `roc-` equals the text), rank 2 prefix, rank 3 substring of the name; ASCII case-insensitive; then the name by bytes; cut to 20. No other name is listed (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN10). |
| Q113 | Which members has an item of `packages` (Registry R-AP7, R-SN5)? | `name`, `first_log_index`, `state` of the name itself. No `moved_from`: a moved name is not replaced (decided 2026-11-02, slice 107, Registry R-SN6). |
| Q114 | How is a matrix row with no error digest in `runs` and `nearest` on the wire (Registry R-AP17, R-RE3)? | `error_digest` is text or null: `null` for no digest, not `""`. It is a required member. 2 rows with no digest have the same digest for a run (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP58). |
| Q115 | A text of `hashes` in the closure query that is not base58 of 32 bytes (Registry R-AP16)? | Not a 400. It goes in `unknown`, as sent, and not to D1. `unknown` has the order of `hashes`, each text once (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP59). |
| Q116 | A tag of `compilers` with no nightly id (Registry R-AP16, R-IK4)? | Not a 400. It is a missing end: the end of the domain on its side. A held `roc:` pin can be a compiler release, and a 400 would refuse a newer `tln` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP60). |
| Q117 | A `compilers` end with a nightly id and no `toolchains` row (Registry R-AP16, R-MX16)? | A missing end: the end of the domain on its side, not a place by its date. The answer then holds the same facts or more (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP60). |
| Q118 | `compilers.from` after `compilers.to` (Registry R-AP16)? | An empty W and an empty E: `epochs`, `runs`, `nearest` and `hints` are `[]`. The other members as usual (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP61). |
| Q119 | A closure body that does not decode and has more than 300 groups: 413 or 400 (Registry R-AP46, R-AP48)? | 400. The order: the body size (413), the decode (400), the counts of `groups` then `hashes` (413), the `series` and `range` values (400), then D1 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP62). |
| Q120 | A candidate that the key bounds give and `VersionReq.matches` drops: is its closure in `artifacts` (Registry R-AP41 row 4)? | Yes. The filter applies only to `releases`. A release whose hash is in `hashes` stays also when the filter drops it, because a header value stays in its domain (Resolution R-C9) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP63). |
| Q121 | `checked` of the closure answer: from which releases (Registry R-AP17, R-AP29)? | All releases of each group in `groups`, with no version bound. A group with no release or on another host gives no item. Sorted by `name` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP64). |
| Q122 | The closure answer has no release with the label of an unknown header value (another group, or a range drops it). How does the client see case 1 of Registry R-AP28? | From 2 sources: the label in the answer, or a 409 `label-conflict` to the ingest of case 2, which is case 1 for that URL (the R-PU19 warning, no R-E6 exit code). The closure is sent again only after an ingest gave 200 or 201. No new request member, no new round trip. With case 3 conditions, case 3 applies (decided 2026-11-02, slice 110, Registry R-AP65, R-AP28). |
| Q123 | Which problem for an asset whose hash is not the hash of its URL (Registry R-PU38 row 4)? | 422 `not-a-candidate`, and the `detail` names the 2 hashes. roc also refuses it (`HashMismatch`), so the bytes are not the release (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 8). |
| Q124 | A read with a wrong hash and also another refusal: which one is the answer (Registry R-PU38)? | The first of: the hash, the first refusal of the read in stream order, an error at the end of the tar, no header file, an error of the header file. A size over 100 MB stops the read at once and gives that refusal (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 9). |
| Q125 | The zstd errors other than the window: 422 (Registry R-PU38 row 3)? | Yes, 422 `not-a-candidate` for a damaged frame, a cut frame, a dictionary, and the output limit of one push. The Worker keeps the default limits of `Native.ZstdStream`. The output limit is a platform limit (first guess, slice 111 issue P2) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 10). |
| Q126 | A dir in `targets/` whose name does not match `[a-z0-9]{1,32}`, or a file directly in `targets/` (Registry R-RE20, R-IK7)? | Skipped, not a target, and the archive stays a candidate. roc looks only in the dirs of the declared targets, and the linker reads `targets/macos-sysroot/` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 11). |
| Q127 | Which tar member kinds does the archive read accept (Registry R-PU38 row 6)? | A file (type `0` or NUL) and a dir (type `5`). Each other type is refused, also a FIFO and a contiguous file, because roc refuses it (`TarUnsupportedHeader`). A symbolic link is refused by row 6 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 12). |
| Q128 | Is the platform entry of an app a `deps` row? Is `roc` a `deps` row (Registry R-RE1, R-RE19)? | No and no: the platform of an app is `platform_hash` only, and `roc` is `roc_pin` only. A platform entry in a package or platform header is a `deps` row, because `platform_hash` is `null` for these kinds (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 13). |
| Q129 | Which dep URLs does the archive read accept (Registry R-RE19)? | `https://`, `PackageUrl.parse` gives `Ok` or `NoVersion`, and the hash is base58 of 32 bytes. Each other entry (a path, `http://`, no hash, 2 versions, `0.0.0`, a bad hash) gives 422 `not-a-candidate`. roc refuses these on download, and a path has no hash for a `deps` row (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 14). Changes the proposal: the hash text is checked. |
| Q130 | Is 100 MB 100,000,000 bytes (Registry R-PU38 row 2)? | Yes: 10^8 bytes. 100,000,000 bytes pass, 100,000,001 are refused. The other limits are in MiB (2^20) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 15). |
| Q131 | `main.roc` that is not UTF-8, has no header, or is an app with no platform (Registry R-PU38 row 7)? | Not UTF-8, and no `app`, `package` or `platform` header: 422 `not-a-candidate` (roc refuses UTF-8 errors, and reads a file with no such header as a module). An app with no platform is accepted with `platform_hash` `null`, because roc gives it the Echo platform (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 16, R-AP49). Changes the proposal for the app with no platform. |
| Q132 | Duplicate paths: exact text, or also `a//b` and `a/b`, and a case-only difference (Registry R-PU38 row 6)? | A key: the path parts between `/` with no empty part, and ASCII case folded. One key 2 times is a duplicate (422). A member below a file member is also refused. The header file is the file with the key `main.roc` (case kept). roc writes `a//b` and `a/b` to one file, and a case-insensitive file system does the same with `main.roc` and `MAIN.ROC`, so the header could differ from the file roc reads (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 17). Changes the proposal. |
| Q136 | The platform URL of an app with no hash: a URL with no `<hash>.tar.zst`, or a path platform (Registry R-PU38 row 13)? | 422 `not-a-candidate`, with the checks of a dep URL (row 14) also for the platform: `https://`, a valid package URL, a hash of 32 bytes. roc refuses a URL with no hash and an absolute path. A relative path in the bundle gives no hash for `platform_hash` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 111, Registry R-PU38 row 18). |
| Q137 | The size limit of an OIDC token (Registry R-TP16, slice 108b)? | 8192 bytes, the default `max_token_bytes` of `roc-jwt`. A longer token gets 401 `unauthenticated` before any other check. A GitHub token is 1 to 2 KB, and long names, `job_workflow_ref` and custom claims add a few hundred bytes. 8192 is more than 4 times that. The limit of one Workers request header is 32 KB, so the header always fits. A small limit also bounds the work of a made-up token (decided 2026-11-02, slice 108b, Registry R-TP16). |
| Q138 | `aud` as a list (Registry R-TP3 row 2, slice 108b)? | Refused: 401 `unauthenticated`. R-TP3 row 2 says `aud` is equal to `AUDIENCE`, and GitHub sends one text, set by the `audience` parameter of `getIDToken` (R-TP2). A list needs a rule for the other values, and no GitHub token has one. No wire change from slice 108 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 108b, Registry R-TP16). |
| Q139 | A member `source` inside a row of `POST /v1/compat` (Registry R-MX5, R-AP10)? | 400 `bad-request`, as in the body. Any value, `null` too; a key with a JSON escape is the member; a text that holds the word is not. R-MX5 says the source comes from the auth, and a row is a part of the body (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-MX5, R-AP66). |
| Q140 | `error_digest` in a bot row (Registry R-AP10, R-AP58)? | A required member: text or `null`. `null` is stored as NULL, an empty text is a digest. A missing member is 400. The request has the form of the answer (R-AP58) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q141 | A row `hash` that is not base58 of 32 bytes (Registry R-AP10)? | 400 `bad-request` with no D1 call. A valid hash that is not indexed is 404. A body value in no valid form is 400 (R-AP51); the 404 of R-AP56 is for a path. The path rule of Q91 stays (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q142 | Which row of a bad `POST /v1/compat` gives the answer (Registry R-AP10, R-MX10)? | The first row of the body that fails. The static checks of all rows come before the facts of D1. In the facts check, the 404 of a row comes before its target 400 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q143 | The 500 row limit and the value checks (Registry R-AP46, R-AP48)? | The limit (413) comes first, after the decode (400). The same order as the closure body (Q119) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q144 | A body that fails R-AP48 rows 4 to 6 and a bad token: 413, 415, 400 or 401, 403 (Registry R-AP48, R-AP10)? | 413, 415, 400 first. The token check is part of row 7, the route. These checks cost no D1 call and no JWKS read. The media type is a header, read before the body, so a body that is too large and not JSON gets 415 (as `POST /v1/closure`) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP48, R-AP66). |
| Q145 | The `missing` list of a 422 `sweep-incomplete`: the state after the rollback, or without the rows of the request (Registry R-MX9)? | The state after the rollback, read in a third D1 call on the primary with the SQL text of R-AP39 `missing`. It holds the members whose rows were in the failed request, and it can be shorter or empty when another request wrote in between. The answer stays 422. Rejected: a list without the request rows needs a second SQL text and a parameter, and R-MX8 says that one SQL text serves the triggers and R-AP39 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-MX9, R-AP66). |
| Q146 | A `POST /v1/compat` with `Talon-Contributor` (Registry R-CT1, R-MX5)? | With an `Authorization` header the request is the bot path and the contributor header is ignored (R-CT1: a request with a token has no id). With no `Authorization` it is a user hint, which slice 116 writes. Until then 500 `internal`, so the route is not deployed before slice 116 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q147 | `GET /v1/sweeps/{tag}?target=` with a target other than `any` (Registry R-AP39, R-MX4)? | 400 `bad-request`, also for an empty value. A sweep exists only for `any` (R-MX4), so another target has no fact; this differs from R-AP5 `target` (Q104), which filters rows that exist for each target (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-AP67). |
| Q148 | `audit` of `GET /v1/compat_hints` (Registry R-AP40)? | Required. Decimal digits, no leading zero, as `after` (Q95). Any other text is 400 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-AP67). |
| Q149 | `audit` above 1000, and `audit=0` (Registry R-AP40)? | 1000 for any number above 1000, also past 2^63 - 1 (the text is compared). `audit=0` gives `{"pairs":[]}`. R-AP40 says "at most 1000". `after` is 400 above 2^63 - 1 (Q95) because it binds to a column; `audit` is a count with a limit in the rule (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-AP67). |
| Q150 | What does `members` count in `GET /v1/sweeps/{tag}` (Registry R-AP39, R-MX11)? | Distinct hashes. A member is a hash (R-MX11 row 5): a `bot` row is the fact of a hash, and `missing` lists hashes (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-MX11, R-AP67). |
| Q151 | The order of pairs with an equal total in `audit` (Registry R-AP40)? | Hash bytes, then nightly id (date order, R-IK4), then target code, each rising. 2 answers of one state must have one ETag (R-AP24) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-AP67). |
| Q152 | A malformed `{hash}` in `GET /v1/artifacts/{hash}/compat` (Registry R-AP4)? | 404 `not-found`, `no artifact <hash>`, no D1 call. The rule of R-AP2 (Q91); an unknown hash is 404 and is not put in the Cache API (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115b, Registry R-AP56, R-AP67). |
| Q153 | R-MX8 says `INSERT OR IGNORE` for the matrix rows, R-CT4 and Q86 say `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (Registry R-MX8, R-CT4)? | `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` (with `WHERE true` after the `SELECT`). `OR IGNORE` also skips a row that fails a `CHECK`, with no error. R-MX8 and the plan row 115 are changed to the text of Q86 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-MX8). |
| Q154 | Where are the request types of `POST /v1/compat`: `packages/records`, a public `Nullable`, or a copy in `worker/lib/` (Design R-PK7, R-GP1)? | `packages/records/Compat.roc`, in a records slice 104d before the code of 115a. They are API types (R-PK7), the CLI and the bot script send the same rows (R-SH4), and a copy would drift. `Nullable` stays private (as in 104b). `records` is a Talon package, so R-GP1 to R-GP7 do not apply; the Worker already uses it by relative path (decided 2026-11-02, slice 115a, Registry R-AP66). |
| Q155 | R-PU34 row 4 and Q30 say `github.com` only for an ingest. R-PU6, R-PU27, CLI R-DI11 say an ingest indexes another host (Registry R-PU34, R-PU40)? | `github.com` only. No P2 path indexes another host: there is no visibility read, no release rules, no reconcile, no ids and no abuse bound for it. The client sends no ingest for it (R-AP28 case 3). The read shapes for another host stay (`null` members, operator yank). A later path (for example an ingest with the operator key) can add it as a new path, so the ingest with no token keeps the rule (R-PH1). R-PU6, R-PU27 and CLI R-DI11 are changed (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-PU40). |
| Q156 | The order of the reads of an ingest (Registry R-AP68)? | D1 first (a known label answers 200 or 409 with no GitHub read), then the repository, the release, the asset, `.roc-version` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68). |
| Q157 | The label with the same hash and no `ingest` provenance row: which reads (Registry R-PU14, R-PU31)? | Only the visibility read, then one batch: the provenance row and the read. No release read, no download (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 7). |
| Q158 | R-PU15 says the release record carries all its provenance rows; R-AP49 has no such member (Registry R-PU15, R-AP49)? | A new member `provenance`: the list of the `via` texts, strongest first. Not in slice 112: one later slice adds it to each answer with releases at once, so that one state has one ETag. A new member is allowed in `/v1` (R-AP44) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP49). |
| Q159 | Which `url` is stored for an ingest, and is the case of `owner/repo` kept (Registry R-AP49, R-AP56)? | The request URL as sent. Only the `repo` name is lower case (R-IK2). `Acme/Lib` and `acme/lib` are 2 groups, as in roc (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68). |
| Q160 | The release read of an ingest gives 404 (Registry R-AP9)? | 422 `not-a-candidate`. A draft is a 404 to a reader with no token (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 8). |
| Q161 | `.roc-version` with a status other than 200 and 404, and an empty file (Registry R-AP49)? | 502 `upstream`. An empty file is the text `""` (the file exists, as `direct` reads it, CLI R-DI8) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 10). |
| Q162 | The asset read gives 404 after the release read listed the asset (Registry R-PU38)? | 502 `upstream`: a fault of the read, not a fact of the release (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 9). |
| Q163 | Does the first write of a name by an ingest add a `repo_events` row `bind` (Registry R-TP13, R-RE15)? | Yes, in slice 113 with R-TP9. Slice 112 writes none, and no Worker is deployed between the two slices (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112). |
| Q164 | The `label-conflict` of the trigger after a race: does the `detail` name the other hash (Registry R-AP41)? | No. The `detail` says that the label has another hash in the index. No more D1 call: R-AP41 gives it only to R-IK3 and R-MX9, and the client needs only the fact (R-AP65) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68). |
| Q165 | The session of an ingest, and the bookmark of a 200 with no batch (Registry R-AP41 row 5)? | One `with_session!(Primary)` for all D1 calls. Each 200 and 201 has the bookmark of the session after its last call, also a 200 from the read only. A problem answer has none (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68). |
| Q166 | Which cache keys does a write drop (Registry R-AP43, R-AP55)? | A fixed list of 4 keys: R-AP13 with no series, R-AP13 with the series, R-AP1 with the lower-case name, R-AP2. The query values are percent-encoded by one rule that `tln` uses too. `after` pages and other spellings keep their entry up to 60 s. A publish, an ingest that wrote, and a yank drop, after the answer (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP69). |
| Q167 | The owner count of R-PU34 row 2: by owner id or owner text, and where in the order (Registry R-PU34)? | The lower-case owner text of the URL, in D1 call 1, with a byte range on the `names` value. Only for a new name of a new label. 429 with `Retry-After` to 00:00 UTC. A soft limit: the write batch does not check it again (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 5). |
| Q168 | The IP limit of R-PU34 row 1: the key, the place in the order, and does a refused request count (Registry R-PU34, R-AP25)? | The key from `cf-connecting-ip` (IPv4, or the IPv6 /64 prefix, else `unknown`). After the body and URL checks, before the first D1 call. Each request that gets there counts. `Retry-After: 60`, the period of the binding (decided 2026-11-02, slice 112, Registry R-AP68 row 3). |
| Q169 | Which `gh` version do the prebuilt tool binary rules need, and how does Talon tell that `gh` is too old (CLI 6.4, R-PT20, R-PT23)? | Minimum `gh` 2.68.0: `gh attestation` is from 2.49.0, but `--source-ref` is from 2.68.0 (checked in the `cli/cli` source tags; the Ubuntu 2.46.0 has no `gh attestation`). Talon runs `gh attestation verify` with the flags of R-PT20 and `--help` once: exit code 0 is new enough. No version parsing. A `gh` that is missing, too old or not logged in builds from source with one `note:` line on stderr when the release has a binary. Q133 stays: no check without `gh`. The REST call `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/attestations/sha256:{digest}` gives the Sigstore bundle, not a verified result, so it does not remove the need for `gh` (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PT20, R-PT23). |
| Q170 | Which token reads the tag list of `roc-talon/talon` in slice 116a, before the token code of slice 119 (Registry R-CT12, Q67)? | Q67 stands: the installation token of R-PU25 (its P2 check passed). The token helper is a new slice 119a, with the RS256 JWT of the App, the installation token in `Memo`, and the change of all GitHub reads (slices 109, 112, 116a) to one function that adds the header. Before 119a, the read of 116a has no `Authorization` header, as the reads of slices 109 and 112 have. The fake GitHub of the tests does not see headers, so no test changes. Rejected: the helper in 116a. It is about 100 lines with new secrets and a new fake endpoint, and 116a is at the size limit (TW19). Cost of the wait: 60 requests an hour for each IP address. A failed read gives the key 0 (Q178), so no hint is lost, and the Worker is not deployed before 119a (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT12). |
| Q171 | The order of the checks of the hint path (Registry R-AP66, R-AP48, R-AP10)? | The order of R-AP66 for both paths, with the client version of R-AP48 row 3 after the method: path, method, client version, media type (415), body size (413), the id (400), the decode (400), the rows limit (413), the values (400), then the rate limit, then D1. The proposal had 413 before 415. R-AP66 and Q144 have 415 first (the media type is a header), so the two paths of one route must agree. New rule R-AP70 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-AP70). |
| Q172 | A hint body with `rows: []` (Registry R-AP10, R-AP70)? | 200 `{"written":0,"first":0,"skipped":[]}`, no D1 call and no GitHub read. `talon_version` is still required. The request uses the IP budget, as each request that passes the static checks does. Reason: there is nothing to write or to read, and a 400 would be a fault of `tln` only for a body that is valid (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-AP70 row 10). |
| Q173 | The target of a hint row for a platform and for an app (Registry R-CT2, R-MX10)? | A package takes `any` only. An app takes `any` or any target name that has a code (an app has no `artifact_targets` row). A platform takes `any` or a target of its `artifact_targets`, else 400, as the bot has. A target with no code is a skip (`unknown-target`). A hash that is not indexed is a skip before the target is read. Reason: a smoke build of a platform for a target that it does not build is a fault of the client, as for the bot (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT2). |
| Q174 | `old-nightly` of a hint row: how old, which edge, which `published_at` (Registry R-CT14, R-CT15)? | Older than 365 days (31,536,000,000 ms) before the request time. A compiler at exactly 365 days is not old. A nightly with many `toolchains` rows (one for each target) uses the smallest `published_at`. R-CT15 uses the same 365 days in the Worker, so a row that is written is not trimmed at once. Reason: the age of R-CT15 is "12 months (first guess)", and a month has no fixed length in ms (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT14). |
| Q175 | The message of the HMAC that makes the contributor id of a machine (Registry R-CT3)? | The 32 characters of the id of the header as UTF-8 bytes. The id is lower-case (R-CT1), so one id has one message. The form never changes: a change unlinks all machines. Rejected: the 16 bytes. They add a decode and give no more security (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT3). |
| Q176 | A hint request with `error_digest` in a row, `source`, or `sweep` (Registry R-CT2, R-MX5, R-AP10)? | 400 `bad-request` for each, with any value, `null` too, and the `detail` names the member. Reason: each one is a member that a client of this path never sends, and the rule "unknown members are ignored" (R-AP44) is for members that the Worker does not know (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT2, R-AP70 row 6). |
| Q177 | Is `talon_version` stored as the key, or as the key with the top bit flipped (Registry R-IK5, R-RE12)? | The key as a signed 64-bit integer (`to_i64_wrap`), with no flip. The column is compared for equality only, and `unknown` is 0 as R-IK5 says. The flip of `releases.version` is for the order of the column. `0.4.1` is 17180000255 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-IK5). |
| Q178 | The tag list read of R-CT12: URL, page, and a failed read (Registry R-CT12)? | `GET https://api.github.com/repos/roc-talon/talon/tags?per_page=100`, one page, `Memo` of 1 h in the isolate. A failed read (not 200, no answer, a body that is not a tag array) keeps nothing, the request is written with the key 0, and the next request reads again; the reads after a fault are bounded by R-CT11. More than 100 tags is a known limit: a version that is not on the page is `unknown`. Paging comes at about 80 tags (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT12). |
| Q179 | The `Retry-After` of `hint-quota` (Registry R-CT13)? | The seconds to 00:00:00.000 UTC of the next day, rounded up to a whole second. At 00:00:00.000 it is 86400 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT13). |
| Q180 | One pair twice in one hint request, with two results (Registry R-CT4)? | The first row of the body wins. The insert reads the rows in the order of their index and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` skips the second one (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT4). |
| Q181 | A row that is a duplicate for the contributor, and a request whose rows are all skipped (Registry R-CT4, R-AP10)? | A duplicate is not written and not in `skipped`. It is not a fault, and a request that `tln` sends again must give 200. A request with `written` 0 gives 200. `first` is read before the insert, so a request sent again has `first` 0 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116a, Registry R-CT4). |
| Q182 | Which routes are the write routes for `MIN_TLN_WRITE` (Registry R-AP45, R-AP41)? | The routes of the Write row of R-AP41, with their methods: `PUT /v1/toolchains/{tag}`, `POST /v1/releases`, `POST /v1/compat`, the move route, `DELETE /v1/contributors/me`, `POST /v1/tokens`, `DELETE /v1/tokens`, the yank routes. `POST /v1/closure` is a read. The webhook is not checked (the agent is GitHub) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116b, Registry R-AP45). |
| Q183 | A setting `MIN_TLN` or `MIN_TLN_WRITE` that is not a semver, and a `tln/<v>` that is not (Registry R-AP45)? | Not applied, and not checked: the request passes. Reasons: a typo in a setting must not stop all clients, and a build with a free version text cannot be ordered. The operator tests a new setting with one request (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116b, Registry R-AP45). |
| Q184 | A `User-Agent` of the form `tln/<v> (extra)` (Registry R-AP45)? | The first product is read: the text up to the first space. `tln/0.4.1 (linux)` is 0.4.1. A header that starts with another product is not checked. Reason: RFC 9110 10.1.5 allows more text after the product, and a client with an appended comment must not skip the minimum (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116b, Registry R-AP45). |
| Q185 | The rate key of hints: no address header, a mapped IPv6 address, the text of an IPv6 key, and the `Retry-After` (Registry R-CT11, R-AP68)? | The key and the function of Q168, one function for ingests and hints: the text of the key is `2001:db8:0:0::/64`, a mapped address is its IPv4 address, no header or an unreadable one is `unknown`. `Retry-After: 60`. Reason: one rule has one test, and the key is not visible to a client (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116b, Registry R-CT11, R-AP68). |
| Q186 | The place of the hint IP limit in the order of the checks (Registry R-CT11, R-AP70)? | After the id check, the decode, the rows limit and the values (rows 1 to 8 of R-AP70), before D1 call 1, as R-AP68 row 3 and Q168. A request refused before it does not use the budget. A request that gets there counts, whatever its answer, also a body with no row (decided 2026-11-02, slice 116b, Registry R-AP70 row 9). |
| Q190 | The `compat` member of the publish body: row form, hash, target, limit (Registry R-AP8, R-PU5, R-PU16; CLI plan G-C3)? | A list of facts `{nightly, target, result, error_digest}`: a bot row without `hash`. The facts are of the hash that the Worker reads from the asset, so a client cannot name another hash; a member `hash` or `source` is 400, as in Q176. `target` is `any` only in P2, because R-PB5 sends unit checks (smoke builds are bot rows or hints). `result` is `pass`, `fail`, `hang` or `crash`. At most 500 facts, 413 with `field` `compat`. The checks come before the token check. The first write of a key wins. The type is `Compat.Publish` of `records` (slice 104f). Rejected: a `hash` in each fact (it adds a mismatch case and no new fact), a wider `target` (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP71). |
| Q191 | The user hint body and its answers as `records` types (Registry R-AP10, R-AP70, R-CT2, R-CT14; CLI plan G-C4)? | New types in `records/Compat.roc` (slice 104f): `HintRow` `{hash, nightly, target, result}` with no `error_digest`, `HintPost` `{talon_version, rows}` (both required, Q172), `HintSkip` `{row, reason}`, `HintAnswer` `{written, first, skipped}` and `Written` `{written}` for the bot path. `Compat.Post` of 104d is not changed. The decoder of `HintPost` refuses the members of Q176, so a fake registry in a `tln` test refuses what the Worker refuses. The Worker keeps its `Hints.parse` and the 400 texts of slice 116a. `reason` is text, so a new reason does not break an older client. The encoder of `HintAnswer` writes the bytes of the Worker (`written`, `first`, `skipped`; an item `row`, `reason`). That order and `row` from 0 are proposals of 116a section 6 for the human review; if the review changes them, only the encoder changes (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP72). |
| Q192 | Which answer types go in `records`, and what is the scope of the slice (Registry R-AP1, R-AP2, R-AP5, R-AP7, R-AP13, R-AP14, R-AP35, R-AP37; Design R-PK7; CLI plan G-C5)? | Every answer that a client reads and that a route gives: releases, artifact, toolchains, short names (`short` and `q`), the tracker and the token. Slice 104e adds the modules `Index`, `Toolchain`, `Short` and `Account` with a type, a decoder and an encoder for each. The encoders give the bytes that the Worker gives now. Then `worker/lib/Reads.roc`, `Toolchains.roc` and `Shorts.roc` call them, and their own text builders go. No wire change: the W0 and W2 tests of 106, 107 and 109 stay as they are and are the proof. `Account` has no Worker code yet (slice 117 uses it). Not in 104e: R-AP4, R-AP39 and R-AP40, which only the compat bot reads (slice of the bot script, CLI plan G-C12). Rejected: each client writes its own decoder (two copies drift, the reason of Q154) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP73). |
| Q193 | The text of the OIDC `AUDIENCE` against the default registry URL of `tln` (Registry R-TP2, R-TP3 row 2, R-TP16; CLI plan G-C11)? | One text B for the setting `AUDIENCE`, the `audience` of the token request, the `aud` of the OIDC token and the `iss` and `aud` of the JWT. B is the base URL form of CLI R-RG7 row 3 (Q207): the origin, lower-case scheme and host, no path, no trailing `/`. The Worker compares byte for byte and does not change the text. Any default host fits if B of the constant equals `AUDIENCE` (R-RG8). Rejected: a list of `aud` values (Q138), a Worker that changes the text (the text is then not the one that the token has) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-TP17). |
| Q194 | Is a 4xx to `POST /v1/closure` exit 20 or 15 (CLI 3, R-E6; CLI plan G-C24)? | A read: not exit 20. The route answers 400 and 413 only, so exit 1 by R-E6. Any other 4xx is exit 15. "A write" in R-E6 means the Write row of Registry R-AP41. An error answer that is not problem details is exit 15 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP74 row 1). |
| Q195 | The message of `unsupported-client` when the answer has no `minimum` (CLI plan G-C25)? | Exit 1 as with `minimum`. Text (a proposal): `tln <version> is too old for <registry base URL>: update to the newest version` (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP74 row 2). |
| Q196 | How a variable with several ranges becomes the one `range` of a closure group (Registry R-AP16; CLI plan G-C26)? | The distinct ranges in the order of first appearance, joined with `, `. A `roc-semver` range is a comma list that a version must match in full, so the join is the intersection. `*` is dropped, and with no range left `range` is left out (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP74 row 3). |
| Q197 | What does the client do when no compiler is allowed (Registry R-AP61; CLI plan G-C27)? | A command that must choose a compiler sends no query and stops with exit code 10 (R-NS2). A report-only command sends the query with no `compilers`. Both ends missing is the whole domain, so it is never used to mean none. `tln` sends no empty window (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP74 row 4). |
| Q198 | `hashes` of the closure query: every header entry, or only the ones that no group gives (Registry R-AP16; CLI plan G-C28)? | Every header entry that has a hash, also when its group is in `groups`, each hash once, in the order of first appearance. A range or a series can drop the release of a group, and R-AP63 keeps the release of a hashes value (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP16, R-AP74 row 5). |
| Q199 | Does the registry list compiler releases or nightlies only (Registry R-AP57; CLI plan G-C29)? | Nightlies only in P2. The domain of releases stays empty (Resolution 1, R-NS2), and a held release pin is a missing end of `compilers` (R-AP60). A later phase can add release rows with no change of R-AP5 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP74 row 6). |
| Q133 | Trust policy of prebuilt project tool binaries: no `gh` (or not logged in), no attestation, or an attestation from a self-hosted runner, at lock time (CLI 6.4, R-PT20, R-PT24)? | The target gets no value in `bin` and builds from source, with a note (CLI R-PT23). With no `gh`, a new `url` gets no `bin` key, and a later `tln update` with `gh` adds the values (R-PT19). `--deny-self-hosted-runners` stays: a self-hosted runner is a machine of the owner. Rejected: the GitHub `digest` with a warning. The digest proves only the upload, so the binary has the trust of the machine of the owner and not of a public workflow, and the lock looks like an attested one (R-SE20). R-S2 accepts this for Talon itself because Talon has no source path before the user has `tln` (R-S1); a tool has one. The cost is small: the lock is in `talon.toml`, and the use needs no `gh` (R-PT21), so one contributor with `gh` locks the binaries for all machines and CI (report E8). Rejected: a check with no `gh`. The attestations API gives the Sigstore bundle with no token for a public repository, but a check needs ECDSA P-256, X.509 and chain checks, the Rekor proof and the Sigstore trust root with its updates (TUF). basic-cli has SHA-256 only (R-PL1), so this is new security code in Roc. `cosign` and the other verifiers are not more common than `gh`. The Worker needs the same parts in P3 (`cloudflare-workers/API.md` RN12); a later phase can add such a check as a new path (R-PH1) (decided 2026-11-02 (user delegated to the design agent), CLI R-PT20, R-PT23, R-SE20). |
| Q134 | An opt-out of prebuilt tool binaries, and at which level (CLI 6.4, R-PT19, R-PT21)? | Two levels (CLI R-PT25). Machine: `tool-binaries = false` in `config.toml` (default `true`): no binary download, no prebuilt entry, no check and no `bin` write on that machine; a `bin` table of a `url` that stays is kept. Project: `bin = false` in a `[tools]` entry: no machine uses or writes a binary of that tool. The project level is needed: without it, the next `tln update` of any contributor writes `bin` again. It is per entry because `bin` is per entry, and it is in `talon.toml` because the lock values of a project are there (G1, G2).
