A `bin` table never overrides `tool-binaries = false`, an exception to CLI R-LY4 as R-C14 is, so a cloned repository cannot turn binaries on (R-SE20). No flag and no environment variable (decided 2026-11-02 (user delegated to the design agent), CLI R-PT18, R-PT19, R-PT25, R-LY4). |
| Q135 | Phase of prebuilt project tool binaries (CLI 6.4, R-PT17 to R-PT25)? | P1, with the project tools (R-PH3), for project tools only. The CLI part needs no registry and no Sigstore code in Roc: `gh` checks at lock time, as R-S2 does in P1, and the use needs only SHA-256 (basic-cli `Crypto.SHA256`). R-PH1 allows it: an entry with no `bin` builds as before, so no result of the R-PT1 to R-PT16 tests changes, and no exit of P1 depends on it. Global tools (`tln tool install`, `tln x owner/app@<sel>`) stay P3 (CLI R-T22): they have no lock file that machines share, so each machine needs `gh` at each install, and `tools.json` needs a record of the check. The registry records no tool binaries (Q38 stays) (decided 2026-11-02 (user delegated to the design agent), R-PH3, CLI R-T22). |
| Q230 | G-C15: `tln add --tool owner/app` for a key whose entry has `bin = false` and the same app (CLI R-PT3, R-PT25)? | It acts as `tln update <key>`, and `bin = false` stays: no command writes, changes or removes `bin = false` (R-PT19). Accepts the proposal (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT3). |
| Q231 | G-C17: the order of the download with its digest and the `gh` check (CLI R-PT20)? With no `gh`, is a binary downloaded? Can exit code 16 come at lock time? | The `gh` probe and `gh auth status` come first (step 2), then the bundle check (step 3), then the downloads (step 4). With no usable `gh`, Talon downloads no binary: it could record nothing. Exit code 16 at lock time comes only from step 4: `gh` is usable, the bundle passed, and a download is not its GitHub `digest` (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT20). |
| Q232 | G-C16: the bundle verify fails. Are the binaries still verified? One note, or one note per target (CLI R-PT20, R-PT23)? | No. A binary passes only with the commit and workflow file of the bundle, so no binary can pass: Talon downloads and verifies no binary, and prints one note for the tool with the reason (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT20 step 3, R-PT23). |
| Q233 | G-C18: does `--dry-run` download binaries, run `gh`, or write temp files in `talon/x/` (CLI R-PT19)? | No. It runs only step 1 of R-PT20 (the release list). With `--json`, rows give `would_check`, the targets with an asset and no value (a proposal, R-OUT2). A dry run writes nothing, as the other `--dry-run` rules say (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT19). |
| Q234 | G-C19: a `url` that is not on `github.com` (CLI R-PT20)? | No check and no note: there is no release list and no GitHub attestation, as for a release with no binary asset (R-PT23 prints a note only when step 1 finds an asset). A new `url` gets no `bin` key, so a later move to a GitHub URL can check (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT20 step 1). |
| Q235 | G-C20: the targets pass with different commits. Which commit goes in the R-PT23 summary? Is a mix a failure (R-SE20)? | A mix cannot pass. The verified attestations of the bundle must name one commit and one workflow file, else no target passes (one note). A binary passes only with that commit and workflow file. So the summary has the one commit of the bundle. A re-run of the workflow at the same commit is allowed (R-PT24, R-SE20 say one workflow file and one commit, not one run) (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT20 steps 3 and 5). |
| Q236 | G-C21: a retry after a `gh` network or rate-limit error (CLI R-PT20)? | No retry. The failure is as any other failure of that file: no value, a note with the last stderr line, and a source build. The next `tln update` checks the target again (R-PT19). A retry needs a reading of the `gh` error text, which Talon does not do (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT20). |
| Q237 | G-C22: a source build entry and a prebuilt entry for the same bundle and target are both usable (CLI R-PT22). Which does step 1 of R-PT9 take? | The prebuilt entry when its sha256 is the `bin` value, else the source build entry. Reason: the lock names the prebuilt bytes, so each machine runs the same binary. Accepts the proposal (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT22). |
| Q238 | G-C23: `tln install --frozen` with exit code 16 on one tool. Do the other tools go on (CLI R-PT21)? | No. Exit code 16 stops the command, as a failed build does (R-T25). Running downloads and builds stop, their temp files go (R-CC2), and the `talon/x/` entries that are done stay (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slices 091 to 093), CLI R-PT21). |
| Q239 | G-C33: a release with no `<hash>-<target>` asset. Does a new `url` get `bin = {}` or no `bin` key (CLI R-PT19, R-PT20 step 1)? | `bin = {}`: step 1 ran and found no binary, so the lock shows that Talon checked. No `bin` key only when the check did not run or stopped at step 2: a `url` not on `github.com` (Q234), no usable `gh` with an asset there, or `tool-binaries = false`. Both forms let the next `tln update` check again (R-PT19). Accepts the proposal. Also checked: in `direct` mode step 1 uses the R-DI2 list that the command already read, with no `releases/tags/<tag>` call; only the registry mode makes that call (decided 2026-11-02, gaps of slice 092, CLI R-PT19, R-PT20). |
| Q200 | G-C1: the layout of registry answers and toolchain rows in `talon/index/`, and the copies after a change of the registry setting (Registry R-AP24, R-AP47, CLI R-RG5, R-CV1)? | One dir for each registry: `talon/index/registry/<origin>/` from the base URL form (CLI R-IX1). Each 200 answer of a `GET` is `get/<key>.json` and `get/<key>.meta`, the form of the GitHub pages, with `<key>` the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of the path and query as hex (R-IX2). No copy of a `no-store` answer, so no closure copy (R-IX3). The toolchain rows of all targets are one file `toolchains.json` with `format` 1 (R-IX4). A command uses only the dir of its registry; GC removes the pages older than the GC age and the dirs of other registries (R-IX5, R-GC15). The R-RG5 answer is `talon/index/github/<owner>/<repo>/repo.json` (R-RG4 note) (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-IX1 to R-IX5, R-GC15). |
| Q201 | G-C2: the forms of `share.json`, of the tables `share_queue` and `smoke`, and the `user_version` step of `compat.db` (CLI R-SH4 to R-SH6, R-SM4, R-TF1)? | `share.json` format 1 with `id`, `sent`, `counted`, `first`, `first_sent_at`, `last_sent_at` (RFC 3339), `token`, `token_expires_at`, `account` (CLI R-SH13). `share_queue`: `seq` INTEGER PRIMARY KEY is the order key, then `hash`, `nightly`, `target`, `result`, `talon_version`, `queued_at`, unique on the key and the version; a row of another `tln` version leaves with no send. `smoke`: (`hash`, `nightly`, `target`) and `outcome`; a machine error writes no row. No `user_version` step: new tables are new fields (R-TF2), so `user_version` stays 1 and an older Talon still reads the file (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-SH13, R-TF7). |
| Q202 | G-C6: how does `tln publish` find the asset URL, also for a repository with 2 or more packages and a release with no asset yet (CLI R-CM13, Registry R-AP27, R-TP2, R-PU39)? | No argument. `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` and the tag of `GITHUB_REF` (a branch ref: exit 2), then `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/releases/tags/{tag}`, R-DI3 and R-DI4 on that release, and R-DI5 for the URL. No release, a draft or prerelease, no asset or 2 assets: exit 2 before the token, and the message names the case. A repository with 2 packages publishes each from its own tag (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PUB1, R-PUB2). |
| Q203 | G-C7: what does `tln publish` send when `compat.db` has no facts for the hash? | The body with no `compat` member, a warning that names `tln probe --range`, and no change of the exit code. Reason: as R-PU39, the asset is public and is indexed anyway, so a stop loses only the provenance (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PUB3). |
| Q204 | G-C8 and G-C30: a command over the request limits (300 groups, 1000 hashes, 256 KB, 50 short values): split or stop (Registry R-AP46, R-AP62)? | Stop before the request, no split. Groups, hashes, body: exit 1 with the limit (R-AP46 already says so). More than 50 short names: exit 2, use full names or 2 commands. Hint rows: requests of at most 500 rows (writes can split). Reason: a split closure gives 2 states of the log (R-AP18) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP78). |
| Q205 | G-C9: `--offline` with a registry, when the closure is a `POST` with `no-store` (CLI R-AD37, R-DI12)? | The rule of R-DI12 on the copies that exist: the `GET` copies at any age (R-AP13 with no `series`, else R-AP1 of the repository), the toolchain rows, the archives for the facts, and the local `compat.db` facts as the only evidence. No closure copy. A group with no copy is an anchor; a new group or a short name with no copy: exit 15. No GitHub page for a registry command (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q206 | G-C10: a test hook for the device flow URLs, and the poll rules (CLI R-SH7, Registry R-CT8 row 1)? | `TALON_GITHUB_WEB`, a base URL in place of `https://github.com` for the 2 device flow requests and the printed URL only (CLI R-EV27, R-EV28, TESTING.md TQ5). The poll follows RFC 8628: the `interval` of the answer (5 s when absent, 0 is allowed), `slow_down` takes the new `interval` or adds 5 s, `access_denied` and `expired_token` or the end of `expires_in` give exit 13, another `error` gives exit 1, no answer gives exit 15 (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-SH12). |
| Q207 | G-C11: the one text form of the registry base URL, and the join with `/v1/...` (CLI R-RG2, R-TP2, Registry R-TP3 row 2)? | The ASCII serialization of the origin (RFC 6454): lower-case scheme and host, the port only when it is not the default, no path, no trailing `/` (`https://registry.example.org`). Talon makes this form from the setting (a trailing `/` and upper case are allowed in the setting) and uses it as the `audience`, the `AUDIENCE` text, the name in messages and the index dir. A request URL is the form and the route path. A path, a query, a fragment or user info in the setting is not valid (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG7 rows 2 to 5, R-RG8). |
| Q209 | G-C12: the form of the compat bot script, the source of its probe results and its test layer (Registry R-MX15)? | A Roc app `bot/main.roc` on basic-cli with the code shape of the CLI and the API types of `records`. Its input is the `data` of `tln probe --json`, not `compat.db`. Tests: L0 and L2 with a fake `Fx`; the Worker side stays W2 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-MX17). |
| Q210 | G-C13: the URL schemes of a registry setting, and a setting that is not a URL (CLI R-RG1, R-EV25, Design 8)? | `https` to any host, `http` only to `127.0.0.1`, `[::1]` and `localhost`, the set of R-FE8. A text that is not `direct` and not such a URL: exit 2 from `--registry` or `TALON_REGISTRY`, exit 19 from `config.toml` (R-TF4). Reason: the OIDC token, the JWT and the contributor id go in headers (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG7 rows 1 and 6). |
| Q211 | G-C14: the Windows roots: the order of the cache root, the XDG variables, and a missing `APPDATA` or `LOCALAPPDATA` (CLI R-LY5 to R-LY7, slice 088)? | Cache: the order of roc, `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `HOME`. Config: `APPDATA`, else `<HOME>\.config\talon`. Data: `LOCALAPPDATA`, else `<HOME>\.local\share\talon`. Talon does not read `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` or `XDG_DATA_HOME` on Windows. An empty variable is not set, and a trailing `\` is dropped. With no variable of a root: kind `Environment` (exit 18), `APPDATA is not set` or `LOCALAPPDATA is not set`. Reason: one order for the 3 roots (a Windows variable, then `HOME`); Windows sets both variables in each session, so `HOME` is used only where they are missing (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-LY5 to R-LY7, R-LY10). |
| Q208 | G-C11: the host of the default registry URL (CLI R-RG2, R-RG8)? | A domain of the org `roc-talon`, not a `workers.dev` name: the default is `https://registry.roc-talon.org`, in the form of Q207. Reasons: a `workers.dev` name holds the name of the Cloudflare account, and a domain allows a zone later (Registry R-AP43, the Cache-Tag purge). The user chose it. The domain is `roc-talon.org` (the user, 2026-11-03). The root name `roc-talon.org` is for the Talon website: the registry and other services use a subdomain, never the root. The URL goes in the `tln` release that changes the default (R-RG2) and in the Worker setting `AUDIENCE` at the first deploy (slice 121) (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG2, R-RG7 row 7). |
| Q212 | G-C31: how does Talon make a cache path absolute and canonical for the proxy when the dir does not exist yet, also on Windows (CLI R-PX6, R-LY5)? | A dir that exists: the canonical path of the system. A dir that does not exist: the lexical form, with no file system call: the separator of the system (on Windows a `/` becomes `\`), each `.` part removed, and each `..` part removes the part before it. No symlink is resolved. The proposal "the text as it is" was not taken, because a `..` in `XDG_CACHE_HOME` makes roc print "No errors found" for any app (Q15). The roots are absolute by R-LY10, so no current dir is joined (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PX6). |
| Q213 | G-C32: `tln share` with no word when `share.json` does not exist (CLI R-SH5, R-SH8, R-SH13)? | Option (b): it prints `id: none` and the totals as 0, and makes no file. A status command writes nothing. The file is made when sharing turns on (R-SH5), or when a send, `tln probe --share` or a login needs the id. The 0600 mode (R-SH5, Design R-SE15) is set with `chmod 0600` on the temp file of R-CC1, before the `rename`, as R-HF2 does (no call on Windows), so the file is never readable by others under its own name (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-SH13). |
| Q214 | G-C34: the text cleanup of CLI R-PX6 for a dir that does not exist: a `..` at the root, empty parts, a trailing separator, UNC, `C:x`, a relative path, the form of a drive root? | The CLI proposals, with one change. A `..` at the root is dropped, as the system resolves it (`/..` is `/`), so the text names the dir that roc opens for the raw value (R-LY5, Q15). Empty parts and a trailing separator are removed. The Windows root is `C:\` or the UNC prefix `\\server\share`, and a `..` never removes a part of it. A path that is not absolute is kept after the `.` and `..` steps, but on Windows a root variable that is not absolute (`C:x`, `x\y`) is not set (R-LY10), as `XDG_CACHE_HOME` on Linux, so the proxy never gets one (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PX6, R-LY10). |
| Q215 | G-C35: does the first-run prompt R-NI16 show again in later `tln run` steps after end of input, also in a child `tln` process (CLI R-SH11)? | No. At most once in one process, and at most once in a process tree: the `tln` that asked sets `TALON_SHARE_ASKED=1` in the env of each child, and a `tln` with it does not ask (CLI R-EV29). A later command with no such parent asks again. The readings of the CLI manager agree with the design: Enter is the default answer No and writes `share = false` (only end of input writes nothing, R-NM12 is the non-interactive case); `--offline` is not a skip (R-SH11 lists only conditions 2, 4 and 5); Yes makes `share.json` (R-SH5); a bad `share` value is exit 19 (R-TF4), and a damaged `share.json` is exit 19 (R-TF6: Talon cannot make the id again) (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-SH11, R-EV29). |
| Q216 | G-C36: the Windows details of the cleanup of R-PX6 and of R-LY10: a UNC root with no part after it, a relative path that starts with `..`, a relative `ROC_CACHE_DIR`, `//srv/sh`, and the absolute check of `C:\` (CLI R-PX6, R-LY10, Q214)? | The proposals of the test agent: a UNC root is `\\srv\sh\`, as `C:\`; a `..` at the start of a relative path stays; `ROC_CACHE_DIR` is a root variable, and a relative value is not set; `//srv/sh` is UNC; the absolute check comes before the drop of a `\` at the end. The readings of 088e agree: absolute is a drive letter of any case with `:` and `\` or `/`, or a start with `\\` (or `//`); the root variables are `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, `APPDATA`, `HOME` (and `ROC_CACHE_DIR`); a kept value is used as it is (`d:/cache\roc`); `HOME=/home/u` is not set on Windows. roc uses such a `HOME` as a path on the current drive, so the 2 differ only when `XDG_CACHE_HOME` and `LOCALAPPDATA` are both missing, and a proxy run still agrees, because the proxy gives roc the cache paths (R-PX6) (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-PX6, R-LY10). |
| Q217 | G-C37: a value of `TALON_SHARE_ASKED` other than `1`, and the 213b proposals for its use and for `id` in `tln share --json` (CLI R-EV29, R-SH13)? | Only the exact text `1` counts. Any other value, also empty, `0` or `true`, is not set. The use of 213b agrees with R-SH11: Talon sets it for each child (a `tln run` step, the proxy compiler, a tool run) when it showed the prompt, also at end of input, or saw the variable. The `--json` `id` of a machine with no `share.json` is `null`, not the text `none`: the catalog writes `null` for a value that does not exist (CLI 3, for example `alias` in `audit`), and a text would be read as an id. The human text stays `id: none` (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-EV29, R-SH13). |
| Q218 | G-C38: the max-age of a registry copy, a 200 with no ETag or no max-age, a 3xx, and a 304 with no `If-None-Match` (CLI R-IX2)? | A third meta line `max-age <n>` from the `Cache-Control` of the 200 answer. No ETag: kept, and read again with no `If-None-Match`. No `max-age`: never fresh. Talon reads only `max-age` and `no-store`, the 2 directives that the Worker sends (`worker/lib/Reads.roc`, `CompatReads.roc`, `Shorts.roc`, `Toolchains.roc`, `ClosureQuery.roc`); `s-maxage` and `stale-while-revalidate` are not read. A 3xx, or a 304 to a request with no `If-None-Match`: exit 15, nothing kept (R-IX7). The readings of slice 202 agree with R-IX2, R-IX3 and Registry R-AP43 (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-IX2, R-IX7). |
| Q219 | G-C39: which `github.com` texts name a repository for the privacy guard, an unsafe part, a body that does not decode, and the case of the name (CLI R-RG4)? | Each `github.com` text of a request: a typed name, a URL with the host `github.com`, and a group or URL form with no scheme. The repository is the first 2 path parts. One check for each lower-case name, with the lower-case file key, as R-AP69. An unsafe part: exit 2 for a typed name, else no request carries it (R-AP28 case 3). A 200 with no boolean `private`, or not JSON: exit 15, nothing kept. The R-RG6 texts depend on whether the request had a token (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG4 note rows 1 to 4). |
| Q220 | G-C40: a public repository, then a private one in one command: is the public answer kept? | Yes. The command stops with exit 2 (R-RG6), and `repo.json` of the public one stays: it is true and holds no private data (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG4 note row 5). |
| Q221 | G-C41: a typed name that does not have exactly 2 parts (`acme`, `acme/lib/x`) at the privacy guard (CLI R-RG4 note row 3)? | No error of the guard, and the skip of slice 202b is right. The grammar of CLI 2, R-SL1 reads the name before the guard: `acme` is an alias or a short name (R-SC8; `?short=` needs no guard, R-RG4), and `acme/lib/x` is a `host/path` name on the host `acme` (R-SC7), so it is not a `github.com` repository. The errors of those forms are the errors of their own rules (for example R-AD5, R-DI11). A second, different error from the guard would differ from them (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-RG4 note row 3). |
| Q222 | G-C43: the toolchain rows of a registry: a `get/` copy, freshness, a newer `format`, unknown members, two rows of one tag and target (CLI R-IX4)? | No `get/` copy and no freshness rule: one read `after=<largest>` in each command that needs the list. A newer `format`: the file is not written and not read, and Talon reads the list from `after=0` into memory (R-TF3, a file that it can make again; the proposal "use no rows" would leave the command with no compiler list). Unknown members are kept (R-TF2). Two rows of one tag and target: the smaller log index is the record (Registry R-PU10), not the later one, and another sha256 in the later row is the mismatch of R-CV6 (exit 16). The readings of slice 204 agree: no write with no new row, all rows kept and `log:N` applied at use, pins only for used rows, a page with `more` true that does not advance is exit 15 (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-IX4). |
| Q223 | G-C44: several short names that fail in one command, a short name with a selector, a failed `?q=` read (CLI R-AD34, Design R-I8)? | One stop with exit 2 that lists each failing name with its matches, as R-I8 already says, for `tln add` too. The selector stays with the matched name (`parser@^2` is `<owner>/roc-parser@^2`). A failed `?q=` read gives no suggestion, and the stop stays exit 2 (suggestions are display only, Registry R-SN5). The readings of slice 211 agree: `?q=` only for a name with no match and with no guard (it carries only the typed text, R-RG4), `log:N` hides its hits; a wrong count of `matches` entries is exit 15 (Registry R-AP51); `tln tool` and `tln x` never select from the registry, also for one match (R-AD7); the Project scope (R-SC1) needs no registry lookup (decided 2026-11-02, CLI R-AD34). |
| Q224 | G-C45: may `http_all!` run `curl` children with headers and a body, or do the ingests of R-AD35 run one after the other (CLI R-DA1, R-DA3, R-DA5, R-AD35 step 3)? | `curl` children, so the ingests run at the same time, up to 4, as R-AD35 and Registry R-AP28 case 2 say. Only `curl` (no `curl`: exit 18). A request of the list carries no credential (`Authorization`, `Talon-Contributor`), because other users can read a command line; such a request uses `Http.send!`. Other headers go as `-H`, the body as a temp file. No `--fail`, no `--location`. R-DA3 "no token, no header" stays true for downloads. No test changes: `hw2` keeps `maxconn` 4 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DA1, R-DA8). |
| Q225 | G-C46: is the timeout of `http_send!` the time to the first byte or of the whole answer? | The whole answer, to the last byte of the body: the one value of basic-cli `Http.send!` and `curl --max-time`. So a request ends in its timeout, and R-CC12 holds. The units of the proposal stay: the timeout in seconds, `sleep!` in milliseconds; there is no reason against it, and a `Retry-After` in seconds (Registry R-AP34) is multiplied by 1000 for `sleep!` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DA9). |
| Q226 | G-C47: an `http_all!` limit of 0? | It runs 1 request at a time, as the real `spawn_all!`. Reason: a limit of 0 that runs nothing would drop the requests with no error. A test of it is optional (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DA8). |
| Q227 | G-C48: must the CLI fake HTTP and the W2 fake GitHub have one route form (`@` against `%2F`, `.meta` against `.meta.json`, 404 against 599 for no file)? | They may differ in these 3 points. The 2 harnesses share no code and no fixtures, and a change costs about 100 CLI cases or the Worker runner with no gain in what a test checks. The CLI keeps 404 for no file, because a 404 is an answer that commands handle (R-AD35 starts with one). The method in the file name, the n-th file and `require` / `require-none` are the same idea in both. TESTING.md 3.1 "Fake HTTP routes" names the 3 differences (decided 2026-11-03, TESTING.md 3.1). |
| Q228 | G-C49: what does `http_all!` do with a request that has `Authorization` or `Talon-Contributor` (CLI R-DA8)? | It does not send it, and the result of that request is `Failed("credential")`. The name is compared in any case. The other requests of the list run. So no secret goes into a `curl` command line, also after a fault of a caller (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DA8). |
| Q229 | G-C50: where does "no `curl`" give exit 18 for `http_all!` (CLI R-DA8, R-EC18)? | In the command, not in the `Fx`: the command (the ingest of slice 212) asks `programs!(["curl"])` first and stops with exit 18 that names `curl`. The `Fx` call has no exit code, and with no `curl` each result is `Failed`. The `tmp_dir` that the caller gives to `http_all!` for the body files is not against a rule: the `Fx` has no roots, and the real `Fx` keeps no Talon logic (TESTING.md TW4) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DA8). |
| Q390 | G-C52: a release in a registry answer with `log_index` greater than `log_size` (Registry R-AP18, reader side)? | The answer is not used, and the command stops with exit 15, as an answer that does not decode (CLI R-E6, last row). Not a drop of the release: the answer mixes 2 states, so `log:N` and the printed log size would be wrong, and a drop hides a fault of the Worker. Test: one L2 case with such a closure answer (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP18). |
| Q391 | G-C53: `--offline`, a name with 2 groups, one with a copy and one with none (CLI R-IX6)? | Per group, as R-IX6 says ("a group with no copy"): the group with a copy uses it, and only the other acts as in R-DI11. The slice 206 mark of the whole name is not correct, and `ResolveBase.Candidates` needs a flag for each group. Test: one L2 case with the 2 groups (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q392 | G-C54: `--offline`, a kept copy that does not decode (CLI R-IX6, R-TF6)? | As no copy, not exit 15: the copy is a file that Talon can make again, so R-TF6 applies: Talon deletes it and prints a warning that names the file (a proposal, R-OUT2: `warning: the kept answer <path> of the registry <origin> does not decode (<reason>); removed`). Then R-IX6 goes on: the group acts as in R-DI11, and a new group fails with exit 15. Online, the same copy is deleted and read again. Test: one L3 case with `--offline` and a damaged copy (the warning, the tree diff, and the note of R-DI11). The other proposals of slice 206 agree with the design: the closure query timeout 20 s (R-DA9, R-CC12), and the move of the query step to a slice 206b (a plan change) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q393 | G-C63: the failing modules of a rank 2 `fail`, which has only an `error_digest` (Resolution R-P4, R-EP6, R-EP7, R-UM1)? | An unknown set. It touches each module that the app uses, and an inferred fail from it has an unknown set too. Such a fail does not decide alone, as an inferred fact (R-EP8): when the solver needs the pair, Talon runs the local unit probe, and the local fact gives the set. Reason: the error digest names no module, and a guess of "none" would skip a probe that the app needs, which is the fault that R-EP8 prevents. A rank 2 `pass` still decides. A later `/v1` member with the modules (Registry R-AP44 row 1) can make the fail decide (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-EF3). |
| Q394 | G-C64: do 2 `fail` rows with 2 error digests count as different results for the note of R-EF3? | No. Only a different `result` value. The digest is a text of the first error line, and it changes with the machine (paths) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-EF3). |
| Q395 | G-C65: the probe order of a pair with a rank 6 `fail` hint (Resolution R-AL4)? | After each pair with no hint: the rank 4 group, then the rank 6 `pass` group, then the pairs with no hint, then the rank 6 `fail` group. Reason: the hint says that the probe is likely to fail, so the other probes come first. The output proposals of slice 209 agree with the design: the source words `bot`, `author`, `shared` (rank 6 is shared results, R-EF1), the rank 6 line with the 3 counts, the note of R-EF3, and the `--json` members `error_digest` (text or null, R-AP58) and `pass_count`, `fail_count`, `crash_count`, which are the members of the closure answer (Registry R-AP17) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-AL4). |
| Q396 | G-C73: `--snapshot log:N` and a 404 for a full name: is the GitHub release list read before the stop (CLI R-AD36)? | No. Talon stops at the 404 with exit 2. The message needs no GitHub data, and a read costs a GitHub call for nothing (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD36). |
| Q397 | G-C74: the selector forms on a full name in the path of R-AD35 (CLI R-SL3 to R-SL11)? | A version, a range, a series and `latest` keep the candidates that they match. A nightly tag and a relative step are for a compiler only: exit 2 before any request. A git ref (`--git`) and a sha that does not read as a version, a range or a series are a source build: no registry path and no ingest. A sha of digits only reads as a series first (R-SL9 rule 1). Tests: L0 cases of `FullName.select` for the 4 forms (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35 step 1). |
| Q398 | G-C75: an ingest answer of R-AD35 that the rule does not name (5xx, 3xx, 202, no answer, another 4xx)? | The verdicts of Q421: a 5xx, a 3xx, a 202 or no answer is a skip with a warning that names the release and the status or `no answer`; another 4xx is a skip with no warning; a 429 is exit 15. One rule for both paths (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35 step 3). |
| Q399 | G-C76: is R-AP1 read again when no ingest answered 200 or 201 (CLI R-AD35 step 4)? | No, as R-AP65 and Q423. With only 409 and 422 skips: exit 2 with the text of no candidate (each skip has its warning). With one or more other answers (5xx, 3xx, 202, no answer, another 4xx): exit 15, with a text that says that the registry did not index the name now and to run the command again (a proposal, R-OUT2). The "still 404" text is only for a second read that gives 404. Tests: 2 L2 cases (all 409, and one 5xx) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35 step 4). |
| Q430 | G-C77: the text when the selector keeps no candidate: the text of slice 212, or the text of each command (CLI R-AD35 step 2)? | The text of each command, so one case has one text with a registry and with `direct`: `tln add` lists the close versions (R-SL12), `tln tool install` and `tln x` use their own text. `FullName.no_candidate` stays for a name with no usable release at all and no selector (R-DI3, R-DI4). The change is in slice 206b, and the L0 and L2 tests of the selector case change there. The other output proposals of slice 212 agree with the design: the 409 and 422 warning, the snapshot text, the still-404 text (now true, because it follows a 200 or 201) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35). |
| Q431 | G-C78: does filter 3 of CLI R-SH3 allow an official compiler release `X.Y.Z`? | No, only a nightly tag. The Worker takes only a tag of `toolchains` (Registry R-CT2), and R-CV1 and R-AP57 list only nightlies, so a release row is skipped there. A design change adds releases when roc publishes them (R-CV1) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filter 3). |
| Q432 | G-C79: which members of the registry answers give the hash set of CLI R-SH3 filter 4, and what the Worker refuses? | The `hash` of each artifact record and of each release in R-AP1, R-AP2, R-AP13, R-AP14 and the closure answer (`artifacts`, `releases`). Not a dep hash, `platform_hash` or `unknown` (they may name a hash that the registry does not index), and `runs`, `nearest` and `hints` add nothing (they name hashes of the records). New filter 7: the target that the Worker takes for the kind (Registry R-CT2): a package `any`, a platform `any` or a name of its `target_names`, an app any target. Reason: such a row makes the whole hint request 400 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filters 4 and 7). |
| Q433 | G-C80: does each write of `compat.db` make `share_queue` (CLI R-TF7)? | No. Each P2 table is made before the first write to it. A read of a missing table gives no rows. So `fx/db1` stays (only `facts` and `pinned`) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-TF7). |
| Q434 | G-C81: does `seq` start again after the queue is empty? | It can: a plain `INTEGER PRIMARY KEY`, not `AUTOINCREMENT`. Only the order of the rows that are in the table counts (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-TF7). |
| Q435 | G-C82: a queue write that fails? | The proposal: one warning on stderr, the rows of that write are lost, the command goes on, the exit code and `--json` do not change. A newer `compat.db` format is exit 19, as each write of the file (R-TF3) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4). |
| Q436 | G-C83: one write with more new rows than the cap? | The proposal: the last 10,000 rows stay, because they are the newest, as "drops the oldest rows" (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4). |
| Q437 | G-C84: the `compilers` window of `tln update` with no compiler id (no `roc:` pin, no compiler on `PATH`, `--no-check`) (Registry R-AP16, R-AP74 row 4)? | No `compilers` member, as a command that only reports. The Worker reads a missing member as the whole domain (`worker/lib/ClosureQuery.roc`, R-AP60), so the answer holds more facts, and the client uses no fact on a compiler for its choice. Not an empty window (R-AP61 is "no compiler is allowed", exit 10) and not a stop. Test: one L2 case with no pin and `--no-check`: the body has no `compilers` (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP74 row 4). |
| Q438 | G-C85: the R-DT5 line with a registry and `--snapshot <time>`? | Both: `resolved at <time> (log:<log_size>), <n> probes`. `log:<log_size>` alone would show the releases that the time hid, and the time alone does not name the state of the log. `--json`: `snapshot` is the text of the snapshot as given, and the log size is a member of its own (a proposal, R-OUT2, for slice 207). Test: one L2 case (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-DT5). |
| Q439 | G-C86: a valid `--registry` and a bad `TALON_REGISTRY` (CLI R-RG7 row 6)? | The variable is not read, so it does not stop the command (R-RG1: the flag wins). A bad `config.toml` value is exit 19 also when the flag wins (R-TF4: each known key of the file). The TW24 proposals of slice 206b agree with the design: the 3 error texts, the setting read after the project load, and the guard order (the kept answers, then GitHub, then the one `POST`, R-RG4, R-RG5). Test: one L3 case with a valid flag and a bad variable: exit 0 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-RG7 row 6). |
| Q450 | G-C87: the kind for filter 7 of CLI R-SH3 when the hash came only from a release row, with no artifact record in the answer? | From the header of `packages/<hash>/main.roc` (R-DI7): the first keyword gives the kind, and the platform header gives the targets, as the Worker reads them (Registry R-RE20). The probe of the row had the archive, so the file is there. With no such file, the row fails the filter and is not queued: a guess could make the whole hint request 400 (Registry R-CT2) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filter 7). |
| Q451 | G-C88: does emptying the queue (`ClearQueue`) on an old `compat.db` make `share_queue` (CLI R-TF7, Q433)? | Yes. Each write statement to a P2 table, an insert or a delete, makes the table first. One rule, and the L3 case `sq1` step 10 stays (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-TF7). |
| Q452 | G-C90: `share = true` and no `share.json` at the send (CLI R-SH4)? | Make the file with a new id, then send, as R-SH13 says ("when a send needs the id"). A hand edit of `config.toml` is a way to turn sharing on (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 1). |
| Q453 | G-C91: the faults of the send, and a newer `compat.db` (CLI R-SH4, R-TF3)? | R-SH4 wins for the send: no send, no message, no change of the exit code, also for a newer format and a `share.json` that does not parse. The send runs after the output. `tln share` shows the fault of a file (R-SH8). A queue write during the command keeps Q435 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 2). |
| Q454 | G-C92: the 5 s budget with a clock of whole seconds? | It is the meaning: the budget is a first guess, and the real bound is under 6 s. The timeout of each request is the time left, at least 1 s (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 3). |
| Q455 | G-C93: a status that R-SH4 does not name (1xx, 202, 204, 3xx)? | The rows stay, as for no answer: only a 200, a 201 or another 4xx says what the registry did with them (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 4). |
| Q456 | G-C94: a 200 or 201 whose body does not decode? | The rows leave, `sent` counts them, `counted` and `first` add 0. The registry took them, and a row sent again counts once (Registry R-CT4), so a fault of the body would only fill the queue (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 5). |
| Q457 | G-C95: the clock of the R-SH6 times, and a run with only 4xx answers? | The clock of Talon (`TALON_NOW`, R-EV22). Only a request with a 200 or 201 answer is a send for the times (R-SH13), so such a run writes no `share.json` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 6). |
| Q458 | G-C96: the second request after a fault of the first? | It goes only after a 200, a 201 or another 4xx. After a 429, a 5xx (also 503 `busy`, Registry R-AP82), a timeout or no answer, the send stops: the registry is busy or slow, and the 5 s budget is for the command (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 7). |
| Q459 | G-C97: `Unqueue` fails after a 200? | No totals write. The rows go again, the registry gives `written` 0 for them (R-CT4), and the next good send counts them once. The proposals agree with the design: the header order `User-Agent`, `Content-Type`, `Talon-Contributor` (the order is not part of the contract, Q422), and the `share.json` write of 213b (temp, `chmod 0600`, `rename`, Q213) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 8). |
| Q464 | G-C100: after a failed `Unqueue` that follows a 200, does the second request still go (CLI R-SH4, Q458, Q459)? | No. The send stops, and the totals cover only the requests whose `Unqueue` worked. The next write of the file is likely to fail too, and the rows of a second request would stay in the queue with no gain: the registry counts a row once (Registry R-CT4), so they go again later. This narrows Q458: the second request goes after a 200 or 201 only when its `Unqueue` worked (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 9). |
| Q465 | `tln update --offline` with a registry and a header value of another host (not `github.com`): which line (CLI plan G-C101, slice 206d)? | `<alias>: fixed: not in the registry index`, the line of a registry online. The registry indexes `github.com` release assets only (Registry R-AP28 case 3, R-PU40), so no copy of that group can exist: `no cached index` would say that a copy could help, and nothing would hide that the entry is an anchor. The cause is the same offline and online, so the text is the same. The direct line of R-DI11 stays for `direct` only (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q466 | After a ladder drop the search runs again: is the R-SP24 second query sent for each search, or once per command (Registry R-AP3, R-AP16; Resolution R-SP24; CLI plan G-C102)? | Once. The second answer is kept and each later search of the command uses it. Reasons: one command uses one index (R-DI14); the movable groups, their ranges (R-SP23) and the window of compilers are the same in every search of a command, because a ladder drop learns a fact and does not widen the window; and a query for each search adds a round trip to a step that must end in under 25 s (R-CC12). The closure route has no IP limit (the `RL_*` bindings are for ingests, hints and login). Rejected: one query for each search (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP3). |
| Q467 | `--offline` with a registry and a `[toolchain] roc` range (CLI R-IX4, R-IX6; CLI plan G-C103)? | Confirmed: the compiler list is the kept rows of `toolchains.json` (R-IX4), at any age. With no copy the list is empty, and the rule of R-AP74 row 4 gives its first cause (the compiler list is empty): exit 10 for a command that must choose a compiler. A held `roc:` pin stays (R-C9). The TW24 proposal that `data.log_size` is left out when no registry answer exists (offline, `direct`) agrees with R-DT5 and Q438 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q468 | A registry run where the compiler list is not empty but no compiler fits the `[toolchain] roc` range: the text of `Toolchain.range_failed` or the R-AP74 row 4 text (CLI plan G-C104)? | The text of `Toolchain.range_failed` (CLI R-SO13), exit 10 `no_tuple`, as on a `direct` run: one cause, one text. It already names the range and, when the switch is off, the switch and its source. R-AP74 row 4 gives its texts only to the causes that `range_failed` does not cover: the compiler list is empty, `min-release-age` and the snapshot (Design 5, `ClosureRequest.no_compiler`). The first-cause order stays; the switch and the range share one text (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP74 row 4). |
| Q469 | `tln install` with no names, with a registry, when no variable is free: the closure query or none (CLI R-DI1; CLI plan G-C105, slice 206e)? | The query, with no `groups` and the `hashes` of the header. The command still checks the header with the ladder (Resolution R-AL1), and one answer gives the header facts (R-AL8), the shared matrix (rank 2 and 6 evidence, so fewer probes), the index notes (R-AP30) and the `log_size` of the R-DT5 line; with no query the facts would come from archive reads and the line would have no log. It is one round trip for one resolve (R-AP3). `--frozen`, `list` and the proxy resolve nothing and send none (R-DI1). A header with no group and no hash sends none and prints the time. The TW24 proposal for `verify --json` (`data.snapshot` = `log:<n>`, then `data.log_size`, no human R-DT5 line) agrees with R-OUT2, R-OUT4 and Q438 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DI1). |
| Q470 | `tln share login` with no `share.json` (CLI plan G-C106, slice 219)? | Make the file with a new id under its lock, before the intro text, and send that id in `Talon-Contributor`. This is R-SH13 as written ("a login needs the id", Q213), as for the send (Q452). Reason: the hints that this machine sends later carry this id, and the Worker links the machine to the account only for the id that the login sent (R-CT8 row 3), so a login with no id would leave later hints outside the account. A file with a corrupt format stops the command before the person types a code (R-TF4) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH7, R-SH13). |
| Q471 | Do the `github.com` device flow requests carry a `User-Agent` (G-C107)? | Yes, `tln/<version>`, the text of every request of `tln` (Registry R-AP45). GitHub requires a `User-Agent` on its API, the device flow endpoints are not documented to refuse a request without one, and one text for all requests is simpler than a rule for each host. It gives GitHub no fact beyond what the same header gives it for `api.github.com`. Test change: `code_request`, `poll_request` and the `calls.log` lines of the device flow get the header after `Accept` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH12). |
| Q472 | The form body of the device flow: URL-encoded values (G-C108)? | Yes. The media type `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` percent-encodes a `:` as `%3A`, and a server decodes both forms. The tests already pin the encoded form, and the device code is encoded the same way. No test change (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH12). |
| Q473 | The timeout and the log of the device flow (G-C110 list; slice 219 Q8)? | 20 s for each request (CLI R-DA9), no limit of the whole flow except `expires_in`. A token, a device code or a user code never goes to a command line or a log (Q224). `sleep 0` in the log for `interval` 0 is allowed: R-SH12 step 2 uses 0 as it is (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH12). |
| Q474 | `expires_at` in ms against whole seconds of `token_expires_at` (G-C109)? | Round down (floor). A client then treats the token as expired no later than the Worker does, and never sends a token that is past its `exp` by a rounded half second. The Worker `exp` has 3 decimals (Q301), `expires_at` is whole ms, and `tln` reads only `expires_at`. Test change: add an `expect` with `...600500` ms that gives `...600` s (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH13). |
| Q475 | A 2xx answer that is JSON with missing members, from GitHub or from `POST /v1/tokens` (G-C110)? | Exit 15, as a 2xx answer that does not decode (R-E6, Registry R-AP51). The message names the step (the code request, the poll, or the registry login). Test change: L0 `expect`s for each missing member of step 1, an object with neither `error` nor `access_token`, and a token answer with no `token` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH12 step 11). |
| Q476 | `tln share login --offline` (G-C111), and the other TW24 proposals of slice 219? | `--offline` gives exit 15 with no request and the text that the login needs the network, as the other commands that need a network and have none (R-PTH2, R-PT15, R-IX6). The proposals agree with the design: the intro and the code go to stderr in both modes (prompt rule R-NR1, R-J2), and `--quiet` does not hide them because the person needs the code; `--json` `data` is `{account: {id, login}, token_expires_at}` (R-OUT4, `token_expires_at` as in R-SH13); 20 s for each request; a 401 of `POST /v1/tokens` is exit 20 (a write, R-AP45, R-E6) with its own text that says that the registry refused the GitHub login, not the text for a release; `share.json` is read (and made) before the intro. The intro, code and error texts are proposals (R-OUT2) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH7). |
| Q477 | `tln share` after a 401 or with a JWT past `token_expires_at` (G-C112): the exit code and the stream of `run tln share login`? | Exit 0, on stdout after the local lines. The status is a report, and R-E7 says that a report is not a failure; R-E6 does not apply because R-SH8 handles the 401. An expired JWT sends no `GET` and is removed, as a 401 does (R-SH13). `--json`: `data.login_needed` is `true`, left out otherwise (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH8). |
| Q478 | `tln share` with a JWT when the registry is `direct` or `--offline` is set (G-C113)? | The `GET` is not sent. The local lines only, exit 0, the JWT stays. The status already shows sharing off for both (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH8). |
| Q479 | `tln share` with a JWT when the `GET` fails (G-C114)? | The local lines, a warning on stderr that names the cause (with the `Retry-After` time for a 429, Q424), exit 0, the JWT stays. A status is a read of local state with an extra part, and R-E7 applies (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH8). |
| Q480 | `tln share logout` with no JWT or no `share.json` (G-C115)? | No request, no file, a message that there is no login, exit 0. The goal (no token) is reached, and a repeated command must not fail (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q481 | `logout` or `forget` when the registry answers 401 (G-C116)? | `logout`: remove the JWT and exit 0 (the token is already of no use). `forget`: change nothing (the JWT, the id, the totals stay), exit 20, and say to run `tln share login` and then `tln share forget`. Reason: the account data is still at the registry, and removing the JWT would make the next `forget` the machine form, which would not delete it. A login can always make a new JWT for the same GitHub account, so no local state takes the way to delete the data away (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q482 | `logout` or `forget` when the `DELETE` fails (no answer, 429, 5xx) (G-C117)? | The local state stays, exit 15, and a later run can try again. For a 429 the message gives the `Retry-After` time (Q424) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q483 | `logout` or `forget` with a JWT past `token_expires_at` (G-C118)? | `logout`: remove the JWT, no request, exit 0. `forget`: stop with exit 2 (a precondition), no request, no change, and say to run `tln share login` first. Not the machine form: it would delete only the rows from before the login, then wipe the id and the totals, and leave the account data at the registry with no proof (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q484 | `forget` with no `share.json` (G-C119)? | No request, no file, a message that there is nothing to forget, exit 0. A new id would delete nothing, and the registry would answer 400 with no id (R-CT16 row 7). This is not the login case (Q470): a login needs an id to link, and `forget` removes what an id and a login made (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q485 | `logout` and `forget` with `--offline` (G-C120)? | Exit 15, no request, no change, as `login` (Q476) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH9). |
| Q486 | `forget` with a JWT: only `Authorization`, or also `Talon-Contributor` (G-C121)? | Only `Authorization`: the Worker ignores the id when a token is there (R-CT16 row 7, R-CT1). R-SH5 now lists R-AP36 for the machine form only (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH5, R-SH9). |
| Q487 | `forget` and the members of `share.json` that Talon does not know (G-C122)? | They stay. "The other members" of R-SH9 and R-SH13 are the known members, because R-TF2 keeps a member that Talon does not know when it writes the file (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH13). |
| Q488 | The TW24 proposals of slice 220 (G-C112 note)? | `--json` of the status with no JWT: `"account": null`, not a missing member, because the catalog writes `null` for a value that does not exist (section 3, Q217) and R-OUT4 wants one shape; this changes the 3 `--json` lines of `sh3` (R-OUT5: before the first release a catalog row can change). A JWT whose `token_expires_at` equals now is expired: the Worker accepts a token only while `exp` is after now (R-CT9), so the client does not send one at the edge (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH8). |
| Q489 | `tln upgrade` with no names and a registry when the compiler list cannot be read: hold the nightly like `direct`, or exit 10 (CLI R-CV1, Registry R-AP74 row 4, Q467; CLI plan G-C123)? | Hold it, as `direct`: the nightly stays H, the note `the compiler list could not be read; roc stays <H>`, a `Held` window, and the packages upgrade. `upgrade roc`, and a command with no held compiler, fail with the failure of the list (exit 15; `Toolchain.offline_no_list` offline). One cause, one behaviour on both runs, as Q468. Q467 is changed in one point: a list that cannot be read is not an empty list. R-AP74 row 4 ("the compiler list is empty", exit 10) is for a list that was read and has no compiler left for a command that must choose, and `upgrade` with H does not need to choose (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP74 row 4). |
| Q490 | `--offline` with a registry and a kept `toolchains.json`: does the line `index: cached at <time>` count it as a copy (CLI R-IX6, R-DI12, R-IX4; CLI plan G-C124)? | No. The line shows the oldest index page copy that the run used (R-IX2). `toolchains.json` is a store of rows that never change, and its file time is the last write of a new row, not the last read: with no new nightly nothing is written (R-IX4), so a complete file can look old. A time from it would not tell the user how old the facts are. A run that used only the compiler list prints no `index:` line. The user who needs a newer list runs the command online (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IX6). |
| Q491 | The registry reads of `probe --share` for the filters 4, 5 and 7 (G-C125)? | One `POST /v1/closure` with `groups` empty, `hashes` the one hash, and `compilers` from the oldest to the newest nightly of the facts to send. `artifacts` and `releases` give the filters 4 and 7 (kind, `target_names`, the hash in an answer), and `runs` and `nearest` give filter 5 (`bot` rows). It is one round trip (R-AP3) before `POST /v1/compat`, with the same body builder as `tln install` with no free variable (Q469). `GET /v1/artifacts/{hash}` has no runs. A hash that no record has shares nothing, and `--share` sends no ingest (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q492 | What "recorded" means for the compiler of a fact in filter 3 (G-C126)? | The row (the tag of the fact, the asset target of this machine) is in `pinned`. The probe ran with the compiler of this machine, so that asset is the compiler of the fact. A row of the tag for another target says nothing about this compiler (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filter 3). |
| Q493 | `tln probe <alias> --share`: the order and the exit code (G-C127)? | Probe first, then send, as R-PB5 does for `probe --range` then `publish`. Probe lines, then share lines. The code of the probe stays when it is not 0 (for example 12). With a probe of 0 and a send that fails (429, 5xx, no answer), the code is 15: the person asked for the send, so it is not silent as the queue send is (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q494 | `--share` with `--offline` (G-C128)? | Exit 15 before any work, no request, as `login` (Q476) and `logout` (Q485). `direct` stays exit 2 (R-PB6) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q495 | No fact passes the filters (G-C129)? | No request, exit 0, the line `nothing to share` and the lines of the facts kept back. A report with bad facts is exit 0 (R-E7) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q496 | Does `--share` send the smoke rows of slice 218 (G-C130)? | No. R-PB6 sends the facts of `compat.db`. The smoke table is a separate store (R-SM4) that exists only when sharing is on and goes through the queue (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q497 | A failed write after the send of `--share`, and a bad file before it (G-C131)? | Before the request: a `share.json` that does not parse or a newer `compat.db` is exit 19 (R-TF3, R-SH13). After a 200: one warning on stderr, no change of the exit code. The silent rule of R-SH4 detail 2 is for the queue send, which runs after the output of another command and must not change it. `--share` is the work of its command and has its own lines (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q498 | The TW24 proposals of slice 217? | The two output lines (`shared <n> fact(s) of <label>: <counted> counted, <skipped> skipped by the registry`, `  not shared: <nightly> <target>: <reason>`), the `data` fields and the sort by nightly, target and closure agree with the design (R-OUT2). The 20 s timeout with no 5 s budget agrees: the 5 s is the budget of the end-of-command queue send (R-SH4). Batches of 500 rows, one after another, at most 20 requests (10,000 rows, the queue cap of R-SH4), and a stop at the first 429, 5xx or no answer. The user chose the cap on 2026-11-03: with no cap one hash with many facts could pass the 30 requests a minute of `RL_HINTS` (Registry R-CT11) (Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q499 | Where does `tln why` get the rank 2 and rank 6 evidence with a registry (Resolution R-EF1, R-EF3; CLI R-CM7, R-DI1, R-DI8; CLI plan G-C132)? | (a): `why` sends the closure query of R-DI1 with no `groups`, the `hashes` of the header and the held nightly as the window. The answer has `runs`, `nearest` and `hints` for these hashes and their closure (R-AP17), so the blocks of ranks 2 and 6 and the two rows of R-EF3 come from one round trip. R-CM7 and slice 209 promise the evidence, and (c) would show it in no command. (b) R-AP4 for each hash is many round trips and breaks the note that only the compat bot reads it. R-DI8 stays: rank 4 is from the files. The privacy guard runs as for any closure query (R-RG4). `why` sends none with `direct` or `--offline`, and shows ranks 1, 3, 4 then. `why --registry <url>` is accepted, as the registry is the source of the index, not a resolve flag (R-FL7) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DI1). |
| Q500 | Which pairs get the R-EF3 note in the resolve commands, and where does it go (CLI plan G-C133)? | (c): the pairs of the chosen tuple on the compiler of the run, one note for each pair where the `bot` and the `author` row differ. (a) would print one note for each compiler of E, many and not about the result; (b) would note pairs that the search tried and dropped. The note goes to stdout with the other notes of the resolve (R-AL7) and to `data.notes` as text; `data.index_notes` is for the freshness of the index (Q407). The accepted text stays: `note: <name> <version>: the bot and the author rows differ on <compiler>: bot <r>, author <r>`. `tln why` shows both rows in the block and in `--json` (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-EF3). |
| Q501 | Does `tln verify` with a registry print the R-EF3 note of Q500 (CLI plan G-C134)? | Yes. `verify` checks the tuple of the header with the rank 2 evidence of the same answer, so a differing `bot` and `author` row of a pair of that tuple is a fact the person wants, as in `install`, `update` and `upgrade`. Same text, stdout and `data.notes`. Test change: the shared finish step prints it for `verify`; one L2 run (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-EF3). |
| Q502 | The window of the `why` query in a workspace whose members have different `roc:` pins (G-C135)? | None. The nightly of the workspace (Pin.decide) when the members agree. With different pins R-WS4 gives no nightly to hold, but R-WS4 stops only the commands that write; `why` writes nothing, so it sends the query with no `compilers` member (Q437: the whole domain, more facts), and each member block shows the facts of its own pin. Not the pin of the first member (it would show the wrong compiler for the other members), and not one query for each pin (more round trips than R-AP3 allows) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DI1). |
| Q503 | When does `probe --share` send the closure query, and which reasons are listed (G-C136)? | When at least one stored fact of the hash passes the local filters 2, 3 and 6, with the window of their nightlies. With no stored fact, or with none that passes, there is no request (the CLI reading sent a query with no `compilers` for the second case; it is not needed, because nothing can go). The reasons of `not shared` stay the filter reasons (a hang, an unrecorded compiler); "not in an answer" appears only for a hash that is in no `artifacts` or `releases` of an answer that was read (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q504 | The `groups` of the `probe --share` query, and a hash that is in a release row only (G-C137)? | `groups` is empty (the slice text says `headers`). An indexed hash has an artifact record in `artifacts` (R-AP17), so filter 7 reads its kind and `target_names` from it. A hash in no `artifacts` shares nothing (filter 4); the file `packages/<hash>/main.roc` of filter 7 is only for the answer of another route in the same command, which `probe --share` does not read (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q505 | Which failed `POST /v1/compat` stops the send of `probe --share` (G-C138)? | Any failed request: a 429, a 5xx, no answer, and also a 400 or a 422 or another 4xx. A client fault would repeat on each later batch. The exit code is the one of that request in R-E6 (a 400 is 1, a 422 is 20, 429/5xx/no answer 15), after a probe of 0. The batches not sent are listed as not shared. This extends Q498 and keeps the cap of 20 requests (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q506 | `probe <alias> --share` after a probe of exit code 12 (budget) (G-C139)? | The send runs: a budget stop is not a fault, and the facts that exist are real facts of the hash. It runs after exit 0 and 12, and after no other code (1, 2, 15, a fault). The exit code stays 12 when the send is good, and also when it fails (Q493: the probe's non-zero code wins) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB6). |
| Q507 | The order of the exit stops of `tln publish`, and the OIDC variables against the release read (G-C140)? | `direct`, `--offline`, operand, the 2 job variables, the form of the repository, the OIDC variables, the release read, the facts, the token request, the publish. The OIDC variables come before the release read because they cost no request and a missing `id-token: write` is the common fault; R-PUB2 ("before the OIDC token is read") still holds, as the token is fetched later (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 1). |
| Q508 | `--offline`, an operand and `--file` with `publish` (G-C141)? | `--offline` is exit 15 with no request (Q476). An operand is exit 2. `--file` is accepted as in R-PD14; since Q524 it names the new package of the API check, and has no other effect (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 items 1, 2). |
| Q509 | The `error_digest` of each fact (G-C142)? | `null`. `compat.db` keeps no digest, and a digest names a local path (Registry R-CT2). The member is required, so `null` is written (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 3). |
| Q510 | More than 500 facts for one hash (G-C143)? | The 500 facts of the newest nightlies, with one warning, exit code unchanged. Not a stop: the registry takes at most 500 (R-AP71) and the publish is worth more than the older facts (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 4). |
| Q511 | The timeout of `POST /v1/releases` (G-C144)? | 20 s, R-DA9 and R-CC12 (a step that is not a wait ends in under 25 s). A cut request is sent again (R-AP34) and is idempotent (R-PU14). The last message says that the publish may be done (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 6). |
| Q512 | A `GITHUB_REPOSITORY` that is not `owner/repo` (G-C145)? | Exit 2 before any request, the message names the variable. Form: 2 non-empty parts, one `/`, ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 5). |
| Q513 | The `audience` in the token URL: as it is or percent-encoded (G-C146)? | Percent-encoded, as Registry R-TP17 already says (the function of R-AP69). The CLI reading "as it is" is changed. Test change: L0 `token_request` and the `http_send GET` lines get `audience=https%3A%2F%2F...` (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 7). |
| Q514 | A tag with `/` or a space in the release path (G-C147)? | Exit 2 before any request: the tag must be one safe path part (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `+`, `~`), because the asset URL has it as one segment (R-DI5) and the Worker puts the tag in the path as it is. A safe tag is used as it is (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 8). |
| Q515 | The token answer and the exit code of a failed token request (G-C148)? | The member `value`. No answer, 5xx, 429, a body that is not JSON or has no text `value`: exit 15. A 401 or 403: exit 2 and the message names `permissions: id-token: write`, because a retry will not fix it. The token is never in a command line or a log (Q224) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 7). |
| Q516 | The texts of a 429, a 503 `busy` and a 401 after the retries (G-C149)? | 429 and 503: exit 15, the text of `Registry.refuse` (Q424, Q445) with `after 4 requests`. 401 after the new token: exit 20, the `detail` and `the registry refused the new token too`. Proposals (R-OUT2) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 9). |
| Q517 | A busy or failed read of `compat.db` (G-C150)? | One warning, then the publish with no `compat` member (the asset is public already, R-PUB3). A newer format is exit 19 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 10). |
| Q518 | Does `tln publish` run the queue send (G-C151)? | No. It runs in a job where `CI` turns sharing off, so the queue is empty. R-SH4 names the exception (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4, R-PUB4 item 11). |
| Q519 | A `Retry-After` that is a date, above 60 s, or 0 (G-C152)? | A date is ignored (the schedule 2, 8, 30 s). Above 60 is 60. 0 is 0. The CLI readings agree (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 12). |
| Q520 | Does `tln publish` read the registry setting, so `--registry <url>` is not refused (G-C153)? | Yes. `publish` is in `Opts.reads_registry`. The registry is the target of the request (R-FL5, R-RG1, R-RG7); `direct` is exit 2 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 2). |
| Q521 | `why` in a workspace where some members have a `roc:` pin and some have none: do the unpinned members count as "the pins do not agree" (G-C154, Q502)? | No. A member with no pin gets the workspace value (R-WS4 last sentence, `Pin.decide`: one pin text means every member holds it), so it uses the same compiler as the pinned members. The pinned members decide the window: equal pins give that nightly, different pins give no `compilers` member (Q502). With different pins an unpinned member has no workspace value to get: its block shows the facts of the compiler that the command selects (`Pin.decide` `compiler`, R-SP1, as when no member has a pin). No pin in any member: no window, as before. The code reading is right (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-DI1). |
| Q522 | The second answer of a held move: are its `epochs` and `hints` used in the search after the move, as its `runs` and `nearest` are (G-C155, Q460, Q466)? | Yes, all four. The second answer is the answer that the later searches of the command use (Q466) and its window is the window of the command (Q460), so its rows are the rows of the same view. The moved groups, and the hashes of their releases, are only in the second answer: without its `hints` the order of R-AL4 has no hint for a moved pair, and without its `epochs` R-EP4 gives each moved release an epoch of its own, so R-EP2 to R-EP3 cover fewer pairs and the search probes more. Merge: `epochs`: for a target that the second answer lists, the epochs of the second answer replace those of the first (it asks for more hashes, so its epochs are the larger; the epochs of one target never overlap, R-AP17); a target that only the first lists keeps its epochs. `hints`: the rows of both, and for the same (hash, nightly, target) the row of the second answer. `runs` and `nearest` as now (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SP24, R-EP15). |
| Q523 | Where does the compiler of "the `roc:` pin of the release header" come from in `tln publish`, which never downloads the new archive (G-C156)? | The new package is the project file of the job (R-PD14, `--file`): the job runs on the tag (R-PUB1), so its tree is the tree of the asset, and its header holds the pin (R-DI7 reading, no archive download). The compiler is the one with the id of the pin when it is installed (`roc` on `PATH` counts when its id is the pin). A header with no pin: the compiler that the command selects (Compiler.select; `PATH` counts). A pin that is not installed: the download is optional (R-DL2: `publish` finishes without it), so it starts only with `--probe-downloads` or `--yes` (R-DL4, R-NI4), and else the check is skipped with a note. `publish` never prompts and never exits 13 for it. No compiler at all, no project file, or a project file that is not a package: a note, no check. Rejected: download the new archive (a download in each job, to read a header that the checkout has) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q524 | How is `roc bump` started (G-C157)? | One `Fx.probes!` spec (it has a timeout and gives stdout and stderr; `exec_in!` has neither): `program` the compiler file (Launch.program), `args` `bump --old <URL> --expect <version> <file>` where `<file>` is the name of the project file, `dir` its directory, `env` as the other compiler calls (R-PX2, R-PX6), weight 1. The 5 words of R-PU39 stay, and the file name is the 6th: the facts of `research/roc-bump` show that `roc bump` reads the new package from `ROC_FILE` (default `main.roc` of the directory), so a repository with 2 packages names the package by `--file`. This changes Q508: `--file` names the new package. `roc bump` prints to its own stdout and stderr only; Talon prints none of it (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q525 | How does Talon read the result of `roc bump` (G-C158)? | `roc` exits only 0 or 1 (research/roc-bump), so the text tells the causes. Exit 0: a pass, no line. Exit 1 with the stderr headline `insufficient version bump`, the words `must be at least` (the white space of the stderr joined, because the nightly of 2026-10-31 wraps the sentence), and in the stdout the lines `This is a <PATCH or MINOR or MAJOR> change.` and `<old> -> <next>`: the warning, and `<next>` is the bump that roc wants. The stdout is the same in all 5 nightlies tried. Any other exit 1 (`package does not compile`, `download failed`, `missing old version`, `invalid expected version`, a headline that Talon does not know): a note with the headline. A warning that lacks a part is a note, never a guess. A signal, a timeout or no start: a note (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q526 | The time limit of the check (G-C159)? | `probe.timeout` (R-P5, default 30 s, R-RC3), as the limit of the `Spec`. A run of 10 to 35 ms was measured for local packages, 1 s with a package that has one dependency and an empty cache; the time is the download of the old bundle and of the dependencies. At the limit the child is killed and the note says `roc bump did not end in <n> s`. The publish goes on. The check comes before the token request (Q528), so its time cannot make the token old (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q527 | The read of the group: `newest` or the cache, and a failed read (G-C160)? | `newest` (`Cache-Control: no-cache`, no token, R-RG3): another job may have published the previous release a moment ago. One request, no retry. A read that fails (no answer, 429, 5xx, a status other than 200, a body that does not decode): a note `note: the releases of <form> could not be read (<cause>): roc bump did not run`, no `roc bump`, the exit code does not change (R-PU39: the check never stops) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q528 | The place of the check in the order of R-PUB4 item 1 (G-C161)? | After the facts of `compat.db` (R-PUB3) and before the token request. The token lives a few minutes, and the check can take up to the limit of Q526 (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 1). |
| Q529 | Can a pre-release version or a withdrawn row be the previous release (G-C162)? | No for both. A release version has no pre-release (Registry R-IK5; a pre-release tag is another group, CLI R-DI5), and `roc bump` reads only `X.Y.Z` (a pre-release gives `invalid old version`). A row with `withdrawn` set is not a candidate (R-C6, CLI R-AU2). So the previous release is the row with the largest version below the new one, with `yanked` false and `withdrawn` null (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-PU39). |
| Q530 | Check of the TW24 proposals of slice 222 (G-C156 to G-C162)? | Accepted: `note: no earlier release of <form> before <version>: roc bump did not run`; a passing check prints nothing; `--json` gets no new member (the notes and the warning are on stderr, as R-PUB3); the group is the URL form with no `series`, so 1.9.0 is the previous release of 2.0.0 (R-AP13). New proposals (R-OUT2), in R-PUB4 item 13: the warning, the notes of a failed check, a skipped check and a failed read (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q531 | The result of `roc bump` is `Signaled` (G-C163, R-PUB4 item 13)? | Its own note, not the timeout note and not the "other cause" note: `note: roc bump ended with signal <n>: no API check. The publish goes on.` `Fx.probes!` gives a kill at the limit as `TimedOut`, a kind of its own (Probe.Output), so `Signaled` is a signal that nobody at Talon sent: a crash of the compiler or a kill from outside, and the number is the one fact that Talon has. A different text for each kind lets a reader tell a slow check from a broken compiler (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q532 | The "other cause" note when stderr has no headline, and when the exit code is neither 0 nor 1 (G-C164, Q525)? | The cause is the name in the last line of the form `── ✗ <name> ──` of stderr (a compile error prints its error headline first and `package does not compile` last; `research/roc-bump` runs 7, 8, 12 show it). Exit 1 with no such line (`Error: no value was supplied for --old`, run 17): the note has no cause words: `note: roc bump did not compare <old> and <version>. The publish goes on.` An exit code that is not 0 or 1: the stderr is not read (roc gives only 0 and 1, so another code is not a roc result) and the cause is `exit code <n>`. Exit 0 is always a pass. A text that Talon does not match is a note, never a guess (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q533 | A command that ends with an error: does it run the send and print the index notes (G-C165)? | The facts of a round are queued when the round ends (R-SH4 detail 12), so an error later in the command loses none. The send runs after an error exit too, when the command got an answer from the registry, except for exit 15 (no network or a busy registry: a send would only wait), exit 19 (a newer `compat.db`, Q453) and the end by a signal such as Ctrl-C (the user wants to stop). The index notes are printed after the error text on stderr (a stale index often explains a missing release); the `--json` error object gets no new member. The exit code does not change (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 10, Registry R-AP30). |
| Q534 | Which commands send the queue (G-C166, R-SH4)? | A closed list, not "each command that sent a request": `update`, `install`, `upgrade` with names, `add`, `verify`, `outdated`, `why`, `audit` (the closure and release reads), and the commands that build a tool app or a smoke build (`tool install`, `tool update`, `tool rebuild`, `x`, `run` with a tool build, R-T21, R-SM1), each wired in the slice that makes its facts. Not: `init`, `roc install`, `roc use`, `roc default`, `roc update`, `upgrade roc` with only the compiler list, the `roc` proxy, `list`, `share *` (`share forget` would send what it must delete), `publish` (Q518) and `probe --share` (it has its own send, R-PB6). Reason: a compiler list request makes no fact and no new hash; a send there only adds a round trip to a command that does not need one. Slice 206e4 wires the 6 closure commands of its scope and `audit` is wired with the rest (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 11). |
| Q535 | Q450 header read: read `packages/<hash>/main.roc` again for the queue, or reuse the R-DI7 text (G-C167)? | Reuse. A command reads a header file once (R-DI7) and keeps the text for the command; the queue step uses the kept text and calls `read_files!` only for a hash that has no kept text. So `update` shows one `read_files` of those headers (the fixture `sh52-update-share-header`) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filter 7). |
| Q536 | `machine_error` of a queued fact before slice 218 (G-C168)? | `false` for each `fail` and `crash` of a unit check. A unit check is `roc check`: it has no link and no SDK step, so no error of the list of R-SM7 can come from it, and a hang is its own result (R-RC3, filter 6). A build (a smoke build, slice 218; a tool build, R-T21) sets `machine_error` from its error text and the list of R-SM7; until a slice wires such a build, none is queued (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH3 filter 6). |
| Q537 | Do `install`, `upgrade` and `verify` print the index notes (G-C169)? | `install` and `verify`: no. They check the header or the lock and choose no newest release, so a stale index does not change what they do, and a note would be noise in each CI run. `upgrade` with names: yes, as `update`: it looks for the newest releases, which a stale index hides. This adds `upgrade` to the list of R-AP30. `why` and `list`: no (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP30). |
| Q538 | Does `--dry-run` queue the facts and run the send (G-C170)? | Yes. R-LF4 forbids project files only, and the facts of a probe go in `compat.db` in a dry run, so they are queued; the send writes `compat.db` and `share.json`, no project file (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-LF4, CLI R-SH4 detail 12). |
| Q539 | The pinned readings of the queue wiring in slice 206e4 (R5 to R9)? | Confirmed. The enqueue follows the facts write of each round: `db_query` `Pinned` (one query for the distinct (tag, target) pairs of the round, so `pinned=<n>`), one `clock` (`queued_at`), one `db_write` `Queue`; a round with no fact that can pass filters 1 and 2 makes no queue call; the rows are in the order of the probe results (R-PP3). The send runs after the output. `outdated` and `why` send too (no exception in R-SH4) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 12). |
| Q540 | The output proposals of the index notes (TW24, slice 206e4)? | Accepted: `note: index: <owner>/<repo> checked <age> ago` and `note: index: <owner>/<repo> not checked since its first ingest` (the text is R-AP30, the group is not in it); in `update` after the other `note:` lines and before `check:`; in `outdated` after the table; `--quiet` hides the lines (R-FL4). The conflict with Q407 is none: both stand. `data.notes` has the texts (as every note), and `data.index_notes` has the objects; `data.index_notes` is the last member of `data` in `update` and follows `members` in `outdated`, which has no `notes` list, so there only `index_notes` is added. `--json` has `index_notes` also under `--quiet` (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP30, Q407). |
| Q541 | Does `upgrade roc` or a bare `upgrade` send the queue (G-C171, Q534)? | Yes, when the run sent a closure query or a release read (R-DI1): it probes, so it makes facts from registry evidence and must share them. The rule is the work of the run, not the name: a command sends when it sent a closure query or a release read of a group, or built a tool app or a smoke build. A command that read only the compiler list or a toolchain page (`roc install`, `roc use`, `roc default`, `roc update`, `init`, the proxy) does not. So each form of `upgrade` that sends the closure query sends the queue, and also prints the index notes (R-AP30, as Q537; Q537 said "with names" by the same reason) (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 11). |
| Q542 | The note when the compiler of the check is not installed, whatever its source, and when the compiler step fails (G-C172, Q523)? | One shape: `note: no API check: <reason>`. A compiler that the command selects (a `roc:` pin, `ROC_TOOLCHAIN`, `default-roc`, `.roc-version`, a range) and that is not installed: `the compiler <id> is not installed (pass --probe-downloads to install it)`, the same words for each source; for a range with no installed compiler in it the id is not known and the words are `no installed compiler is in the range <range> (pass --probe-downloads to install it)`. `roc` on `PATH` is installed by definition: with none, the reason is `no compiler`. The download is optional (Q523), so `publish` never calls the required-download consent and never prints its texts (`TALON_AUTO_INSTALL=no refuses ...` is false when the variable is not set); `--probe-downloads` or `--yes` starts it, with no prompt. A failure of a step before the run (the project read `prepared!`, a bad pin, a bad `ROC_TOOLCHAIN`, a download that fails): the reason is the message of that failure, as the other commands print it, the first line only; its exit code is not used. Anything else not matched is a note, the publish goes on and the exit code does not change (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q543 | Are the unit facts of the ladder (`verify` step 1; the ladder after `install` and `update`) queued, and where (G-C173, R-SH3, Q539)? | Yes. They are results of probes that the command ran (R-SH3 filter 1) and the unit checks of a package or a platform pass filter 2; the baseline check and the whole-app check of the ladder do not (filter 2). The point is the one of Q539: right after the facts write of each probe round, in the search and in the ladder alike (each ladder round that probed, not once at the end, so a later fault of the command loses none). The filters decide: a pair that a `bot` row covers is not queued (filter 5), and a ladder pair with a rank 2 `fail` is probed locally (Q393) and is stopped by filter 5 when the row is a `bot` row; `machine_error` is false for a unit check (Q536). So the `Ladder` input carries the same sharing facts as the search: the answers of the command, the recorded compilers and the registry (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH4 detail 12). |
| Q544 | `publish --probe-downloads` with a pin that is not installed: may it write `default-roc` to `config.toml`? And the range with no installed compiler and no flag (G-C174, Q523, Q542)? | (a) Yes, and no exception for CI. R-SO15 and R-SO24 say that an install that a command makes for its own work sets `default-roc` only when none is set, and never changes one that is set: the first compiler of a machine becomes its default, for each command. `config.toml` is not a project file, so R-LF4 does not apply. The `downloading <id> (<size>)` line on stderr is the line of every download. The case `pu25-pin-download` is right. (b) Confirmed. The note is `no API check: no installed compiler is in the range <range> (pass --probe-downloads to install it)`, `<range>` is the text of `[toolchain] roc` as written in `talon.toml`, and Talon sends no request: with no flag the download cannot start, so no list read helps.
