With the flag, Talon reads the compiler list once (R-CV1) and downloads the newest compiler in the range that the switch allows (R-SO13), as an optional download with no prompt (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PUB4 item 13). |
| Q545 | `tln add <full name>` with a registry, after R-AP1: what does the closure query carry for "the group of `tln add`", and do the candidates and facts come from the closure answer or from R-AP1 plus the archive (G-C175)? | The R-AP1 answer finds the group: the `group` of the releases that the selector keeps (a repository that the registry knows, or that the ingest of R-AD35 made known). The closure query then has one item `{group}` for it: with a series selector (`@2`, `@1.4`, R-SL5) the item has `series`; with a version or a range selector (R-SL3, R-SL4) it has `range` with the selector text; with no selector or `latest` it has neither (all series: the search picks the newest that the ladder allows, as for a free direct dep). The candidates, the kinds, the deps and the facts of the new name come from the closure answer (Registry R-AP17: `releases`, `artifacts`, `runs`), not from R-AP1 plus the archive: R-AP3 allows one round trip for one resolve, the answer carries the shared matrix (Q469) and the notes, and the archive is read for the pick only after R-DI7 as for any dep. The R-AP1 read is the 1 extra request that R-AD35 already names; a second group in the same repository follows the rule of `direct` (no new rule). A short name with one match goes on as a full name (R-AD3) and has the same query (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35 step 5). |
| Q546 | Which commands that make no index call read `config.toml`, so that the check of R-RG7 row 6 reaches them (G-C176)? | Only the commands that read the file for a key of their own (R-TF4: "each known key" of a file that the command reads). The registry key adds no read. `remove`, the project commands and `share *` read it and stop with exit 19 for a bad `registry`. A command that reads no setting (`list`, `cache`, `tool list`, `path`) does not read the file and does not stop: it must work when the config is broken, so that a person can use it to look. The test for `remove` is the right sample (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-RG7 row 6). |
| Q547 | `tool install` and `x` with a registry: the kind of a candidate from the archive header or from the closure `artifacts` (G-C177)? | The archive header (R-T10, R-DI7), as `direct`. The R-AP1 record has no kind. The commands fetch the bundle for the build anyway, so the kind costs no request, and a closure query would add a round trip for a fact that the bundle gives. So they send no closure query (R-AP3). They do not send the share queue in slice 206f: Q541 lists a command that sent a closure query or a release read of a group, and the tool builds that make facts (R-T21); the queue of a tool build is wired with the slice of its facts, and until then `tool install` and `x` queue and send none (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SC3, R-SC4). |
| Q548 | G-C178: how does Talon find a machine error of R-SM7 in a build result? | A line of stderr, with no white space at its end, that is exactly `Error: <name>` for a name of the list, with any exit code. Not stdout, not a substring. Measured in `research/smoke-errors/` (2026-11-03): nightly 10-06 prints `Error: WindowsSDKNotFound` as a line of its own on stderr, exit 1, stdout empty, also after another report. The `ld64.lld` and `lld-link` errors are platform faults. Not measured: the pinned nightly 10-14 (no platform in the cache needs the SDK with it), a missing system linker (roc links with its own `lld`) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM7). |
| Q549 | G-C179: the platform URL of the smoke app when the built platform is not the platform of the project header (`verify`, `--dry-run`)? | The URL of the built tuple, whose hash is the hash of the key. A URL of another hash would build another platform, or roc would refuse the hash. R-SM2 is changed (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM2). |
| Q550 | G-C180: do tool builds run smoke builds in slice 218? | The rule includes them (R-SM3, R-SM8 row 1), and the slice that wires a tool command to the queue (Q534) adds them. Out of scope of slice 218 is a plan choice, not a design change (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM8). |
| Q551 | G-C181: a key that is in `smoke` already at a write? | The first row stays (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), as the queue and the Worker (R-CT4). The key fixes the result (R-SM2), so the 2 rows mean the same (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM4). |
| Q552 | G-C182: does a command that ends with an error run the smoke builds? | Yes, when the send runs (Q533): not after exit 15 or 19. A failed build is the case where a smoke result tells most (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM8 row 2). |
| Q553 | G-C183: does `--no-probe` stop the smoke builds? | No. It stops new unit probes only (R-LF2), and a smoke build is no probe of the solver (R-SM4) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM8 row 3). |
| Q554 | G-C184: several keys in one command? | One `probes!` round for all keys, with the slot pool of R-PP1 (a smoke build takes the slots of a build) and `probe.timeout` for each build. The pinned call order is confirmed with this change for 2 or more keys: the calls before the build for each key in key order (`db_query` Smoke, the platform, `db_query` Pinned, random, `make_dirs`, `write_atomic` of `main.roc`), then one `probes!` with all builds, then `db_write` Smoke and the delete for each key, then the queue calls. Each build passes `--target=<key target>` (R-SM3 row 1) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM8 row 4). |
| Q557 | G-C187: how does a `bot` smoke `fail` become a learned interval (Resolution R-SM6, R-EP7)? | Option (b). The oracle cannot read smoke rows before the pick, because the build target is known only at step 3 (R-TG1), so (c) does not work. (a) is correct but drops one tuple at a time. At step 3, a smoke `fail` of the platform on the build target drops the tuple with no build, and the ladder gives the next search a `Fail` check of the platform release on each compiler of its smoke runs. The check touches each module and decides alone (a real fact about the platform as a whole, not Q393), and it holds over a unit `pass` of the same key. R-EP7 learns the interval. Test: an L2 case where 2 compilers have the smoke `fail`: one drop, then the search skips the second compiler with no step 3 (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM6, R-EP7). |
| Q558 | G-C188: a `bot` smoke `hang` or `crash`? | A `crash` is a step 3 fail with no build, with the interval [r, r] of a fault (R-EP7). A `hang` is not used: it can come from a slow machine (R-SH3 filter 6), so step 3 builds the app. Test: L0 rows of `Matrix.smoke_fail` (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM6). |
| Q559 | G-C189: a local build row against a `bot` smoke `fail` of one (platform hash, nightly, target)? | The local build `pass` wins, with any digest: the real app built with that platform on this machine, and a real app needs each part that the smoke app needs. They are 2 facts, but this one decides the key. A local build `fail` does not change the use of the smoke row. The TW24 texts are accepted: `no tuple works: the build for the host target failed on the platform <name> <version>: <digest> (bot smoke build)`, and `bot smoke build failed` for a null digest. Test: an L2 case with a local build pass and a smoke fail: step 3 runs (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM6). |
| Q562 | G-C192: after a step 3 drop for a smoke `fail`, are the `Fail` checks added when the command read no compiler list (a held compiler), and does such a failure start the held move (Resolution R-SM6, R-SP16, R-SP24)? | (b). The checks are always added: a held compiler is a compiler of the domain, and a real fact must not depend on which list the command read. The held move of R-SP16 starts for each first search that finds no tuple with the held variables fixed, whatever the cause, not only "do not fit together". So `update json` with a held compiler H moves the held platform to a release that builds (the case `sm6-bot-fail-update`), and a held compiler still moves only by R-SP24. Test change: `PickMoves.second_wanted` reads each no-tuple failure; `sm6-bot-fail-update` expects the move with a check on H. The note on `sm6-interval` of the first text was wrong: Q563 corrects it (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SM6, R-SP16). |
| Q563 | G-C193: Q562 and the cases `sm6-interval`, `tg3-constraint`, `ex3-no-tuple-yaml` (Resolution R-SP16, R-SP23, R-SP24, R-SM6)? | (a), Q562 as written, with its wrong note removed. In `tln upgrade roc` the packages are held (R-SP8) and movable (R-SP23), so a first search with no tuple starts the held move, whatever the cause, as R-SP16 says. That is what the user wants: the newest nightly that the project can use, with the consent of R-NI3 for each held package that moves. The expected results: `sm6-interval`: the first search still learns the smoke interval of pf 0.1.0 (that is what the case tests), then the second search finds pf 0.1.1 on N; with `--allow-moves` or `--yes` exit 0 and `~ pf: 0.1.0 -> 0.1.1 (held)`, without them in non-interactive mode exit 13 with the same report (R-SP17). `tg3-constraint` and `ex3-no-tuple-yaml`: the second search runs, with its index read (`index/github/acme/pf`, the extra GET and `1.json`), and when it finds no tuple the case ends with exit 10 as before (R-SP16, last sentence). Rejected: (b), a limit of the trigger by command or by cause: R-SP16 and R-SP24 give no such limit, and a smoke fail is a real fact as a unit fail is (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-SP16). |
| Q564 | G-C194: does `tln init package` with a registry send a closure query, and so the queue (CLI R-IN1, Q541)? | No. The template has no platform and no dependency, so `groups` and `hashes` would be empty and the answer would hold no fact. It is a command that reads only the compiler list: no closure query, no queue send. Its probe is of the project file, which is never shared (R-SH3 filter 2). Requests: `GET /v1/toolchains?after=<largest>` (and the next pages while `more` is true, R-IX4), nothing else to the registry (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IN1). |
| Q565 | G-C195: the closure item of `tln init --platform owner/repo@<version>` with a registry (CLI R-IN9, R-IN10, Q545, Q555, Q560)? | `groups` `[]` and `hashes` `[<hash of the named release>]`, as R-IN10 (2) and `upgrade roc`: the platform is held and not movable, so a group item would only add candidates that the search cannot use. Q545 and Q555 are for a free entry of `add`; Q560 "the group of the newest release" is for `init` with no version. Requests, in order: the privacy guard read of R-RG4 when no `repo.json` is kept; `GET /v1/packages/{owner}/{repo}/releases` (R-AP1; the ingest of R-AD35 on a 404), which gives the hash and the release state for R-IN9 (2); `GET /v1/toolchains?after=<largest>`; `POST /v1/closure` with `{"groups":[],"hashes":["<hash>"],"compilers":{...}}` (the window of a free nightly, R-AP16); then the queue send (Q541). `in1-init-version` stays (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IN1, R-IN10). |
| Q555 | The group of the closure item of `tln add` when R-AP1 has more than one group, and the `range` of a version selector (G-C185, Q545)? | Group: the group of the newest kept release (the largest `published_at`, then the larger `log_index`) that is not yanked and not withdrawn (R-C6): the newest release is the line that the repository publishes now. `strategy` does not choose a group, only a version in it. The other groups are not offered. Range: the range of the new entry (R-AD26, `AddTest`): `=1.2.3` for a version selector, the typed range for a range selector, and `^1`, `^1.2`, `^0.25` for a series selector; none for no selector or `latest`. The `=` form is sent because it is exact in the roc-semver of the Worker (0.2.0) and in the CLI range text (roc-semver 0.1.0), so the meaning does not depend on the reader. Both versions read a bare 3-part `1.2.3` as that version only too (the doc table of `VersionReq.roc` is the same in 0.1.0 and 0.2.0; 0.2.0 only adds `key_bounds`), and the CLI selector reads a bare 3-part version as `Exact` (R-SL3), so the two parsers do not differ. A bare 1 or 2 part version is a caret in both (`2` is `^2`). The Worker reads the text with `VersionReq.parse` (R-AP62: a text that it refuses is 400). This corrects Q545: a series selector gives `range`, not `series`, because the header gets that range (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-AD35 step 5). |
| Q556 | The first-run prompt (R-SH11) calls `Share.setting`, which checks the `registry` key: does a bad `registry` in `config.toml` stop the first interactive run of each command (G-C186, Q546)? | No. The prompt reads the `share` key only, and checks that one (a value that is not a boolean is exit 19). The `registry` key is checked by the command that reads the registry setting, as Q546 says, so `list` runs with a bad `registry` also on the first run. A `config.toml` that is not valid TOML stops as R-TF4 says; this is not new (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-SH11, R-RG7 row 6). |
| Q560 | With a registry, does `tln init` read the platform releases from the registry, not from the GitHub list (G-C190)? | Yes, in a later CLI slice (a new plan row after 206g). The platform of `init` (`roc-lang/basic-cli`, or the name of `--platform`) is a full name: with a registry Talon reads it as `add` does (R-AP1, the ingest on a 404, R-AD35, the closure query with the group of the newest release, Q545, Q555), so the ladder of R-IN2 gets the shared matrix and fewer probes, and the GitHub rate limit is not used. Until that slice, `init` keeps the GitHub list of the platform and reads only the compiler list from the registry (slice 206g); `sh62-no-send-init` stays as written. When the later slice makes `init` send a closure query, `init` joins the list of R-SH4 row 11 by Q541 (a command that sent a closure query sends the queue); until then it sends none (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-IN1, R-SH4 row 11). |
| Q561 | The proxy and a bad `registry` in `config.toml` (G-C191, Q546)? | The proxy reads the `registry` key only when it needs the compiler list: rank 4 has a range and no installed compiler is in it, or R-SO14 picks a compiler to download. A plain `roc` call that has an installed compiler never reads or checks the key, so a bad value never breaks it (R-PX2: dispatch, cost target 5 ms). When the proxy needs the list and the value is bad, it stops with exit 19 and the message of R-RG7 row 6: Talon cannot pick the source of the list, and a silent fallback to GitHub would hide the error. A bad value of another key that the proxy reads (`default-roc`, `min-release-age`) stays as R-TF4 says. This is the same excuse as the first-run prompt (Q556): a read for another purpose does not check the key (decided 2026-11-03, CLI R-PX2, R-RG7 row 6). |
| Q566 | How does the bot run one unit probe of a hash on a nightly (G-C196, R-MX15)? | Keep the reading. (a) The archive: `GET /v1/artifacts/<hash>` (R-AP2), the `url` of the first row of `releases` whose `withdrawn` is `null` (any `yanked` value), then `curl -fsSL --output <RUNNER_TEMP>/<hash>.tar.zst <url>` as a job of `spawn_all!`; `tln probe` checks the hash of the file (R-PB1). No such release, a 404, or a failed `curl`: no row, a warning, and the hash counts as a probe with no fact. (b) The compiler: `env ROC_TOOLCHAIN=<tag> tln probe --json --yes <path>` (R-SO2 rank 2 with a full id): `env` is a program of the one Linux runner (R-MX10), so `Fx.spawn_all!` needs no `env` member. (c) The consent: the compiler of the probe is a required download (R-DL2), and the job has `TALON_AUTO_INSTALL=yes` (R-DL5) and the flag `--yes`, so no prompt and no exit 13. Batches of 4 for the downloads and 4 for the probes (first guess, the 4 vCPU of the runner) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 1). |
| Q567 | Which nightlies does a run sweep, and what is a run with no operand (G-C197, R-MX1, R-MX7)? | An operand is a nightly tag and wins (the input `tags` of `workflow_dispatch` fills them). With no operand (the `schedule` trigger has no input) the bot reads the newest 7 releases of `roc-lang/nightlies` from GitHub (`GET https://api.github.com/repos/roc-lang/nightlies/releases?per_page=7`, no token, one request), keeps the tags that read as a nightly (R-NS1, R-IK4; the draft and prerelease flags are not read, as R-CV1), and sweeps each in the order of R-NS1, oldest first. The bot keeps no state: a tag whose sweep is done (Q572) costs one `PUT` and one read, so a missed day is caught by the next run. A failed list (no answer, 5xx, 429) is exit 15; a list with no nightly tag is exit 15 with the text `no nightly in the list`; neither is a usage fault. A tag that is not a nightly tag in an operand is exit 2. The `PUT` answer 422 `not-a-candidate` skips that tag with a warning (a nightly with no asset of R-CV1). R-MX7 (the range search of new releases) is Q574 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 2). |
| Q568 | How does the bot run the smoke builds of R-MX10 and the audit pairs of a platform target (G-C198)? | It does not, in P2. `tln` has no command that runs one smoke build for a (platform hash, nightly, target): R-SM3 runs it only as an after-step of a command with sharing on, and `tln probe` has no smoke result in its `data`. This is a CLI question (R-PB, R-SM; route to the CLI design agent): the form is probably a `tln probe <hash> --smoke` that prints a `data.smoke` list of `{target, result}`. Until it exists, the bot runs unit checks only: `Bot.audit_groups` leaves out each pair whose target is not `any`, with no message, and R-MX10 and the platform part of R-MX13 are the plan, not the state (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 3). |
| Q569 | The `error_digest` of a bot row (G-C199)? | Keep `null`, as Q509. The `data` of `tln probe --json` has `modules`, no error text (R-AP58, Q394), and a digest of another source would name a local path (R-CT2). When `data` gets an error line, the bot sends it, and no test changes until then (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 4). |
| Q570 | A probe whose `data.compiler` is not the tag or whose `data.hash` is not the hash (G-C200)? | No row, a warning, and the hash counts as a probe with no fact. One change to the reading: the compiler is compared by its nightly id (Registry R-IK4, `Id.nightly_id`), not as text, so the 2 forms of a tag (`nightly-2026-07-31-123c5d7` and `nightly-2026-July-31-123c5d7`) are equal. Also no row: `kind` is not `unit` (a `--range` result), `ok` is false, a body that is not JSON (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 5). |
| Q571 | One OIDC token for each write, or one for the run (G-C201)? | One for each write, fetched just before the `PUT` or the `POST` (a token lives a few minutes, and a run takes up to 35 minutes, R-MX10). A 401 answer to a write: one new token and the same request, once (R-AP34 `Renew`); a second 401 is exit 20. The token is never in a command line or a log (Design Q224) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 6). |
| Q572 | After a 422 `sweep-incomplete`: how many rounds, what after a yank, and a work list with nothing to do (G-C202, R-MX9)? | The request wrote nothing, so the bot reads R-AP39 again once, probes the hashes of the new `missing` that it has no row for, and sends all its rows again (a row that is there is ignored, R-RE13) with the new `log_size` as the bound. A yank makes the set smaller (R-MX11), so the second `missing` can be shorter. At most 2 sweep requests for a tag; a second 422 is exit 1 at the end of the run with `no sweep for <tag>`, and the other tags go on. A work list with a `sweep` row whose bound is at least `log_size` and a `missing` that is empty is done: no token and no request, the line `sweep <tag>: done`. A `sweep` row with a smaller bound and no `missing`: the sweep alone is sent (b2). More than 500 rows (R-AP46): chunks of 500 in order, and the sweep only in the last chunk, because its check needs each member row to be in the matrix. A 404 answer (a row with a hash that is not indexed) is exit 20 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 7). |
| Q573 | The exit codes and the texts of a bot failure (G-C203)? | Keep the reading. 0: all done. 1: a probe had no fact, or a sweep was not completed after the second try; the run goes on with the other tags and the code is given at the end. 2: usage (a missing variable, Q576, a tag that is not a nightly): stop at once, no request. 15: no answer, a 5xx, a 429 or a timeout after the retries of R-AP34 (2, 8, 30 s, `Retry-After` as Q519), and the failed GitHub list (Q567): stop at once. 20: a refusal (401 after the new token, 403, 400, 404 of a row): stop at once. The failures are the `Fail` values of `tln publish` (`Registry.refuse`, `Registry.no_answer`) with the line `tln: error[<kind>]: <text>` (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 8). |
| Q574 | The range search of each new release (G-C204, R-MX1, R-MX7, R-PB3) | Not in slice 223; a later slice. The 2 missing parts are decided here. (1) The output: `tln probe --range --json` gets `data.range.facts`, the real facts that this run probed (not inferred, not from `compat.db`) as `{nightly, target, result}` in the probe order, a proposal for the output review (R-OUT2); the bot posts them as rows with no sweep (CLI side, route to the CLI design agent). (2) The state: the bot keeps none. `after` of R-MX7 is the `upto_log_index` of the `sweep` row of the newest swept nightly before this run (R-AP39), the log size of the last run; the new releases are `GET /v1/releases?after=<that>` (R-AP14) in pages. R-MX17 stands: the bot never reads `compat.db` (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 9). |
| Q575 | The types of R-AP39 and R-AP40: `records` or `bot/lib/Bot.roc` (G-C205)? | `packages/records`: the Worker builds both answers, and a type in `records` makes its encoder and the bot decoder agree (R-PK7, R-AP73). New records slice 104k: a module `Work` (the answer of R-AP39: `sweep`, `log_size`, `members`, `missing`) and a module `Audit` (R-AP40: `pairs` of `{hash, nightly, target, pass_count, fail_count, crash_count}`), each with a type, a decoder and an encoder, the Worker calling the encoder, as R-AP73 says. The `sweep` row uses `Compat.Sweep` when its members are the same. The bot imports them; `Bot.work` and `Bot.pairs` go, and L0 expects 1 and 2 of slice 223 move to the records tests of 104k. R-AP73 text changes (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP73, plan row 104k). |
| Q576 | The registry of the bot: only `TALON_REGISTRY` (G-C206)? | Yes: no `config.toml`, no flag, no default. The text is read with the rules of CLI R-RG7 (base URL form, rows 1 to 6) by the shared function of the CLI, not used as it is. `direct`, an empty value and a bad form are exit 2 with no request. The audience of the token is the origin form of that URL (R-TP17, R-RG7 row 3). Reason: `tln publish` does the same, and the Worker compares the audience as text (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 10). |
| Q577 | The workflow details (G-C207)? | `schedule` `43 4 * * *` (an odd minute: GitHub delays the load of the hour); `workflow_dispatch` with an input `tags` (text, empty by default, passed as the operands); one `ubuntu-latest` job with `if: github.repository == 'roc-talon/talon'`, `permissions: contents: read, id-token: write` on the job, `timeout-minutes: 120`; `concurrency: { group: compat-bot, cancel-in-progress: false }` (a run waits for the earlier one); `TALON_REGISTRY: ${{ vars.TALON_REGISTRY }}` (a variable, not a secret) and `TALON_AUTO_INSTALL: yes` in the job env; `tln` and `bot` built from the checked-out commit with the pinned compiler (TW28), never a release; no secret and no `GITHUB_TOKEN` (the GitHub list of Q567 is read with no token). No `probe.max-toolchains` setting: the compilers of the probes are required downloads, which that cap does not limit (R-DL6). The workflow runs only from the default branch (R-TP7, `BOT_WORKFLOW_REF`); a dispatch from another branch gets 403 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX15 rows, R-MX18 item 11). |
| Q578 | Wiring in the harness files for `bot/` (G-C208)? | Consistent with TESTING.md. `scripts/test.sh`: `bot/main.roc` and `bot/lib/main.roc` go in `APPS` of `check`; `step_fmt` formats `bot` as it formats `cli`, `tests` and `packages`; the L0 drivers are found by name (`tests/unit/l0-*.roc`), so `l0-9.roc` needs no list. `tests/selftest.sh`: a row for `ciscan.py compatbot`, and the count of drivers is 9. `bot/lib` has the dep `lib` (`cli/lib`) and `records`, so the check order needs `cli/lib` first. The workflow text check stays a text check (TW18: the bot code agent does not edit it) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 12). |
| Q582 | The GitHub list of Q567: one request or the retries of R-AP34 (G-C209 a)? | One request, no retry. R-AP34 is the schedule for the registry. The GitHub list is public and has a low limit without a token, the bot keeps no state, and the next run reads the newest 7 tags again, so a retry buys nothing. No answer, a 5xx, a 429 or a body that does not decode is exit 15 and stops the run (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 2). |
| Q583 | A nightly with no row and no sweep to send (G-C209 b)? | No post and no token. The bot posts only when it has a row or a sweep: a done nightly (Q572), and a nightly whose probes all gave no fact (it has no row, and no sweep by R-MX4). `Bot.chunks` of no rows and no sweep is `[]`. The second case is exit 1 at the end of the run, as Q573 says (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 7). |
| Q584 | An empty batch of jobs (G-C209 c)? | No `spawn_all!` call: no `curl` job and no probe job means no call, for the download batch and the probe batch alike (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 1). |
| Q585 | The failure texts of the bot: reuse `Registry.refuse` ("Fix the release or the workflow") or its own text (G-C209 d, TW24)? | Its own text. The words of `Registry.refuse` are for a person who publishes a release; the bot is a scheduled job. One pure function `Bot.failure` gives the texts of R-MX18 item 13 (proposals, R-OUT2). The kinds and exit codes are those of Q573: `rejected` 20 for each 4xx answer except 429, `network` 15 for no answer, 429, 5xx and 503 `busy` after the retries of R-AP34, `usage` 2. The OIDC token is never in a text (Design Q224). The other new lines (the Output table of slice 223) are TW24 proposals for the human review (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 13). |
| Q586 | G-C215: the order of the exit-2 checks of `tln probe --smoke` (Resolution R-PB10)? | The order of the table of the slice: the flags, `--target` with no `--smoke`, `--range`, `--share`, 2 operands, no operand, a bundle file, a bad URL; then, after the fetch, not a platform, no target to build, a target that is not of the platform. The first one that fails gives the message (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 1). |
| Q587 | G-C216: a platform with `targets: {}`, or with no `targets` section? | Both are exit 2, with a message that the platform has no target to build. The header keyword is `platform`, so it is not "not a platform"; but no smoke build exists for it (R-SM2) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 1). |
| Q588 | G-C217: the same `--target` 2 times? | One build, at the place of its first time in `data.smoke`. No exit 2: the bot can repeat a target with no harm (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 2). |
| Q589 | G-C218: the key is in `smoke` with another result? | The first row stays (R-SM4), and `data.smoke` shows the new result. R-PB10 now says "when the key is not there". The table only stops a build again, so its row needs no update, and one write rule stays (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10). |
| Q590 | G-C219: the exit codes of a machine error and of a build with no fact? | A `machine-error` item is a result that is printed: alone it gives exit 0. A build with no fact (R-P3) shows `no-fact`; one or more `no-fact` items give exit 1 (R-PB8), `data` `null` (R-J4), and `error.details.smoke` with the whole list, so the bot still reads the other targets (as Q581 for `facts`) (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 6). |
| Q591 | G-C220: does a URL operand need a project file? | No. A release URL operand uses no project (as R-PD16): no search, no `projects.json` record, the compiler of section 13 with no project (`ROC_TOOLCHAIN` for the bot). `--file` with a URL operand is exit 2 (R-FL6). An alias operand uses the project. Test change: the URL cases drop `--file p1/main.roc`, and one case runs with no project (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 3). |
| Q592 | G-C221: does a `--smoke` result go in the share queue? | No. The bot posts its own rows (R-MX10), and the share path of a user is R-SM3 (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 4). |
| Q593 | G-C222: a compiler id that is not a nightly: is the key written to `smoke`? | Yes, for each compiler id. The table only stops a build again (R-SM3 row 3); the queue has its own filter (R-SH3 filter 3). The TW24 texts and the `data.smoke` shape have no conflict with the design (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 5). |
| Q594 | Do the bot reads (R-AP39, R-AP40, R-AP2) get the retries of R-AP34, and which texts (G-C223)? | Yes, the same schedule: after a 429, a 5xx, a 503 `busy` or no answer, at most 3 more tries after 2 s, 8 s, 30 s (the `Retry-After` of the answer when it has one, at most 60 s). This is an exception to the sentence of R-AP34 that other commands do not send a read again: those commands have a person who waits, and a bot run is long, so one blip on a read would lose the sweep of a whole day. A GET is idempotent. After the last try the failure is exit 15 (a 4xx other than 429 is exit 20), with the texts of `Bot.failure` (Q585) and the new steps `get sweeps <tag>`, `get audit` and `get artifact <hash>`; `Registry.refuse` is not used by the bot. The GitHub list stays one request (Q582) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 14, R-AP34). |
| Q595 | A failed read of R-AP2 for one hash (G-C224)? | Only a 4xx answer other than 429 (a hash that is not indexed, 404) is a fault of that hash: a warning `warning: no fact for <hash> on <tag>: the registry did not give the artifact (<status>)`, no row, no sweep for the tag, the run goes on, exit 1 at the end. No answer, a 429, a 5xx or a 503 `busy` after the retries of Q594 says that the registry is down or busy: exit 15 at once (Q573), because 1000 hashes with 40 s of retries each would use the 120 minutes of the job for nothing (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 14). |
| Q596 | The GitHub list answers 200 and has no nightly tag (G-C225)? | Exit 15, kind `network`, as Q567 says: the bot stops, because a list with no nightly means that the source changed or is not the list that the bot expects, and a silent exit 0 would hide it for each later day. The line: `the list of nightlies at api.github.com has no nightly tag (<n> releases read). The bot stops, and the next run starts again.` This holds for a run with no operand only (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-MX18 item 14). |
| Q597 | G-C226: `tln probe --smoke <URL>` with no project and no compiler selected (Resolution R-PB10, R-SO14)? | Not exit 18 (that is the P0 rule of R-SO14). R-SO14 case (1) applies, with the platform of the operand as the package (R-SO23): Talon picks the newest compiler in the channel with a recorded pass for the platform hash, else the latest, and downloads it with the consent rules R-DL1 to R-DL6 (prompt R-NI5; `TALON_AUTO_INSTALL=yes` or `--yes` accept it, a refusal or no prompt is exit 13), then sets it as `default-roc`. The bot sets `ROC_TOOLCHAIN`, so it does not reach this case. Test change: the case of C1 expects the download prompt or exit 13 in non-interactive mode with no flag (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 7). |
| Q598 | G-C227: a platform whose `requires` gives no smoke app (Resolution R-SM2, R-PB10)? | Not exit 2. No build, no temp dir, no `smoke` row; `data.smoke` has each target of the run with the result `no-smoke-app`, and the exit code is 0. It is a known state of the platform, so the bot records nothing for it, posts no row, and goes on; an exit 2 would stop the run of the bot (Q573), and a `no-fact` would give exit 1 each night. `tln init --platform` keeps its exit 2 (R-IN8): it must write a file. Test change: the C2 case expects exit 0 and the `no-smoke-app` items (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10 detail 8). |
| Q599 | G-C228: the design example `tln add parser@2.0.0` (CLI 1) and the `add` row of the output catalog (CLI 3) do not match the text of the code (TESTING.md TW16, R-OUT1)? | The design changes. A design example is normative (R-OUT1, TW16), so it must match the rules, and the rules already give the text of the code: `matched "parser": <name>` (R-AD3) and `resolved at log:<n>, <probes> probes` (Resolution R-DT5). The old example had a text of its own (`resolved 4 packages in 41ms`, `probed 3 times`, `matched ... to`) that no rule gives. The new example is the command of the L2 case `registry/rg2-example-add-short` with its output, and the catalog row lists the `data` fields of the code. The JSON failure example of CLI 3 stays: its form (`data` `null`, `error`) is the envelope of R-J3 to R-J5. No test change: `rg2-example-add-short` stays (decided 2026-11-06, CLI 1 example, CLI 3 catalog). |
| Q600 | G-C230: the default registry host does not answer (no DNS, an outage) (CLI R-RG1, R-RG2)? | Changed by the user on 2026-11-06 (Q601): the first answer (exit 15 for each registry, no fall back) holds only for a registry that a person set. When the built-in default does not answer, `tln` goes on in `direct` mode ("a no-host mode"; the design allows each operation without a registry). The release that sets the default waits for the DNS zone move (G-C231, the user) (decided 2026-11-06, CLI R-RG9). |
| Q601 | The details of the fall back of the built-in default to `direct` (the rule of the user, 2026-11-06; CLI R-RG9)? | (1) Only the built-in default falls back; a set registry (`--registry`, `TALON_REGISTRY`, `config.toml`) gives exit 15 with the message of R-RG9. (2) No answer means no DNS, no connection, TLS, a timeout and a 5xx (also 503 `busy`); a 429 or another 4xx follows R-E6. (3) One warning on stderr, also with `--quiet`, and `data.notes[]` kind `registry-direct` with `registry` and `cause` (a proposal, R-OUT2). (4) Only at the first registry request of the run, before any registry answer is used; a later failure is exit 15 (R-DI14, one index). A command that needs a registry (`share login`, `logout`, `forget`, `probe --share`, `publish`, `--snapshot log:N`) does not fall back: exit 15. (5) Yes: the run is `direct` in each rule, also the compiler list from GitHub (R-CV1) with the GitHub `digest`, no guard, no share send, no smoke builds. (6) No memory: each run tries again. This is a trade-off for the user: a host that takes the connection and does not answer costs 20 s (R-DA9) in each run; the recommendation is no memory now (no state to go stale, and today the name does not resolve, so the failure is fast), and a memory of a few minutes only if the outage case occurs. Tests: an L2 case with no answer from the default (exit 0, the warning, GitHub calls, the note in `--json`), one with a set registry (exit 15), one with a 5xx from the default, one with a failure after the first answer (exit 15), one with `share login` (exit 15) (decided 2026-11-06, CLI R-RG9). |
| Q602 | G-C233: the stop texts of `tln publish`, `yank` and `move` for a set registry with no answer (CLI R-RG9, R-PUB4 item 9)? | They get the URL and the source, not the fix: `direct` cannot do their work. `publish` keeps its own form, with the source added (`... after 4 requests`, then the source). The same holds for `share login`, `logout`, `forget` and `probe --share` (decided 2026-11-06, CLI R-RG9). |
| Q603 | G-C234: the ingest warning, and an ingest with no answer after the first registry answer (CLI R-RG9 row 2, R-AD35, Registry R-AP28, Q421)? | No change of the warning: the URL and the status, no source. It is an exception to row 2 of R-RG9: the rule "a later request with no answer is exit 15" is for a request whose answer the command needs. An ingest is not one (Q398, Q421), and the share send is silent (R-SH4). Row 2 now says so (decided 2026-11-06, CLI R-RG9 row 2). |
| Q604 | G-C235: the `registry-direct` note in `tln outdated --json` (CLI R-RG9 row 5)? | A new member `notes`, after `members`, as the case `rg9-outdated-note` has. Not an item of `index_notes`: those are the notes of R-AP30, with `name` and `checked_at`. `tln why` makes no registry request now, so it has no fall back and no note: confirmed (decided 2026-11-06, CLI R-RG9 row 5). |
| Q605 | G-C232: a bundle file operand (`tln probe <hash>.tar.zst`) when a project file is found: does it use the project (its `roc:` pin and its config), or no project, as the URL operand of Q591 (Resolution R-PB1)? | It uses the project (decided by the user, 2026-11-07). The compiler of section 13 with the project, and the config of the project. No project file found: the no-project path of Q591 (`ROC_TOOLCHAIN` for the bot, no `projects.json` record), slice 226. This is the current CLI behaviour: no code change. The `label` of a bundle probe in `--json` is the bare hash (accepted output text, R-OUT2). |
| Q606 | G-C236: does the compat bot keep the time and the resources of its probes, so that a person can see the trend and predict when the compat builds get too large (Registry R-MX18)? | Yes (decided by the user, 2026-11-08). (1) Each probe of the bot runs under GNU `time`, which gives the wall time in ms, the user plus system CPU time in ms and the peak RSS in KB. No change to the platform `spawn_all!`. (2) The data is files in the repository, not registry data: no new column, no new rule of the Worker. (3) Files: `stats/compat/<YYYY-MM>.csv`, one row for each probe (run id, time, nightly, hash, repo, target, result, wall ms, CPU ms, RSS KB); `stats/compat/runs.csv`, one row for each run; `stats/compat/README.md` with a mermaid chart of the run totals. (4) The files are on the branch `compat-stats`, not on `main`. The bot job (it has the OIDC token) stays read-only and gives the files as an Actions artifact. A second job with `contents: write` and no OIDC token commits them. Plan: slice 229. |
| Q607 | G-C237: how do the files of `tln` stay usable between releases N and N+1 and back (the audit `research/cli-format-audit/REPORT.md`, F1 to F15; CLI R-TF2, R-TF3, R-TF7)? | Decided by the user, 2026-11-08. The user rejected two methods: freeze `format` at 1 for ever, and a new file name as the general method. The rules: (1) R-TF2 (changed): a release never changes the meaning of a field, column or key in place; a changed meaning gets a new name; a new enum word is a new field; no `format` or `user_version` change unless the design shows that the UX is worse without it. (2) R-TF3 (added): a `.talon.json` with a newer `format` reads as no fields (GC keeps it); only the `link-*` target gives exit 19; one reader function. (3) R-TF7 (added): a changed column meaning is a new column; a row with an unknown word is not used and a write can replace it. (4) R-TF8 (new): an unknown TOML key is a warning and stays; an unknown value form gives exit 19 plus the hint `A newer tln may know this value. Update tln: tln tool update talon`; an item with an unknown enum word is not used, is kept, is listed as "needs a newer tln", and only a command that targets it gives exit 19; `tln tool update talon` reads `config.toml` with no stop and ignores bad `tools.json` entries; `tln setup` replaces an unreadable `talon` entry. (5) R-TF9 (new): the project record step never stops on a `projects.json` input failure; it warns; GC still gives exit 19. (6) R-TF10 (new): the optional gate `[toolchain] tln = "<range>"` in the root `talon.toml` (the model is `engines` of Bun), exit 19 with `this project needs tln <range>, this is tln <v>. Run: tln tool update talon`; a later release can drop the key. (7) R-PT18 (changed), R-PT3, R-PT19: an unknown `bin` target is a warning and stays; an entry write keeps unknown subkeys. (8) R-CC3, R-CC12: the dir lock is a contract between releases; a later mechanism is added in addition. (9) R-CC2: the temp name forms are fixed (adds `.stale` and `.locks`). (10) R-GC5: an unknown `toolchains/` dir name is not listed, not selected, never removed. (11) R-EC19 lists the new exit 19 cases. No rule change for F9 (one format constant for each file kind, no migrate function) and F12 (the code keeps unknown row fields of `path-roc.json`). Plan: CLI slices 230 to 236 (talon-ca). |
| Q608 | G-C238: the probe time of the compat stats (Q606) includes the download of the compiler on the first probes of a nightly. Must it? | No (decided by the user, 2026-11-08). When the stats are on (`COMPAT_STATS_DIR`), the bot runs `tln roc install <tag>` once before the first probe batch of each nightly (sweep and audit), with no GNU `time`. The probe rows then have no download time. A failed install gives one warning on stderr, and the probes go on (they download the compiler, as before). With the stats off, the bot does not change. Plan: slice 229b. |
| Q609 | G-C239: which exit 19 faults of `talon.toml` and `config.toml` get the hint line of R-TF8 (CLI slice 231, Q607)? | Each fault of the value of a known key: an unknown word, a wrong type, a range or `snapshot` that does not parse, `min-release-age` `"48h"`, a bad `registry` text. Only text that is not valid TOML gets no hint. Reason: one simple rule, and `tln` cannot know which values a newer `tln` takes (decided by the user, 2026-11-08, relayed by talon-ca). Also: the read of `config.toml` by `tln tool update talon` goes on when the file is not valid TOML (a warning, an empty config), so the fix of the hint never stops (the reading of R-TF8 "no stop" by talon-ca, accepted by TalonRegistry; not a separate user decision) The hint text is `A newer tln may know this value. Update tln: tln tool update talon` ("may", not "can": the user, 2026-11-08) (CLI R-TF4, R-TF8). |
| Q610 | G-C240: which commands check the `[toolchain] tln` gate of R-TF10, and how does it compare a prerelease `tln` (CLI slice 236)? | (1) Each command and each `roc` proxy call that reads the `talon.toml` of the project. A command or proxy call that reads no `talon.toml` (`roc version`, a project with no `talon.toml`) is not checked. `tln tool update talon` is never checked, because it is the fix that the message names. (2) Only major.minor.patch of this `tln`: `0.4.0-rc.1` counts as `0.4.0` (as Cargo `rust-version`) (decided by the user, 2026-11-08, relayed by talon-ca; CLI R-TF10). |
| Q579 | Q568, CLI side: a command that runs one smoke build of a platform on a nightly and gives the result as JSON, for the bot (Resolution R-SM1, R-SM2, R-MX10)? | The design did not cover it. New flag `tln probe --smoke <alias or release URL> [--target <t>]...` (Resolution R-PB10): it fetches the platform by its URL, builds the smoke app for each target (all targets of the header with no `--target`) on the selected compiler (`ROC_TOOLCHAIN` for the bot), always builds, and writes `smoke`. `--json` `data.smoke` is a list of `{target, result}`, with `result` `pass`, `fail`, `hang`, `crash` or `machine-error` (R-SM7: the bot posts no row for it). A URL operand, not a bundle file, because the smoke app header must name a URL with that hash (Q549); the bot has the URL from R-AP2 (Q566). Exit codes: R-PB8, and 2 for a bad target, an operand that is not a platform, or a mix with `--range` or `--share`. Plan: CLI slice 218b, then the bot uses it for R-MX10 and the platform pairs of R-MX13 (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB10, CLI R-FL12). |
| Q580 | Q574, CLI side: `data.range.facts` of `tln probe --range --json` (Resolution R-PB3)? | It fits R-PB3 and R-PB7: the range search probes real facts, and `--json` shows them. New rule R-PB9: the real facts that this run probed, as `{nightly, target, result}` in the probe order, not the inferred facts and not the facts known before. A new `data` member: no new `schema` (R-J6). Plan: CLI slice 217b (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB9). |
| Q581 | G-C210 to G-C213: the details of the range facts of slice 217b (Resolution R-PB9)? | (1) Always present: `[]` when the run probed nothing. (2) For an exit-12 run, `data` is `null` (R-J4), and `error.details.facts` has the facts that the run probed: the bot needs them most there. (3) A run with no pass has `range` `null`, so the facts are not in `range`: the place is `data.facts`, a member next to `range`, for each result. This changes the path of Q580 (`data.range.facts`) before any code: one place for each case is simpler for the bot. (4) `target` is the target of the key of the fact (R-P4): `any` for a unit check, which is what a range of a hash probes. The TW24 proposals agree with the design, with the path change: `facts` is the last key of `data` (and of `error.details`), and a fact has `nightly`, `target`, `result` in that order (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-PB9, Registry R-MX18 item 9). |
| Q460 | After the second query of R-AP28 case 2, which `log_size` does the R-DT5 line print, and which answer is "the first answer" of a held move (R-SP24; CLI plan G-C98)? | The `log_size` of the answer that the command uses: the second answer (51 in the test), because the ingested releases have a log index up to that size, and `--snapshot log:51` repeats the resolve while `log:50` would hide them. "The first answer" of R-AP16 is the answer that the first search used, so after case 2 it is the second answer. Q404 stands with that meaning: the second query of a held move is hidden above the smaller of N and this `log_size`. Q438 stands (decided 2026-11-03, Resolution R-DT5, Registry R-AP16). |
| Q461 | A `Label` value (R-AP28 case 1): must the pick drop the registry's row of the same version with another hash (CLI plan G-C99)? | Yes. The pick drops the registry's row of the same group and version with another hash and keeps the header URL (R-C9). This is the warning text ("Talon does not use the registry's release for this value"), and it avoids two candidates of one version with two URLs. Other versions of the group are not changed (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q462 | The text of the `fixed:` lines of a registry (CLI plan G-C98, slice 206d)? | `<alias>: fixed: not in the registry index` and `<alias>: fixed: offline, no cached index`, one line for each variable, with the alias of its header entry. Three equal lines would not tell which entry is fixed, and the alias prefix is the form of the design's own notes (R-PU28: `json: moved to ...`). R-OUT4 holds: the alias is a fact that the text prints. The direct text `fixed: <host> has no index without a registry` (R-DI11) is unchanged and is not printed with a registry. `--offline` with a registry prints `resolved at <time>` as direct does: there is no closure answer, so there is no log size. All three agree with R-OUT1 to R-OUT2 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q463 | The titles of the trap answers (Registry R-AP82; slice 123d)? | The `title` depends on the type only. `/errors/internal` keeps `Internal error` (the `Problem.title` of the Worker), and `/errors/busy` is `Busy`. The trap bodies go through the same code as other answers. R-AP82 had `Internal Server Error`, which is changed (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP82). |
| Q260 | An ingest of the URL of an old name after a rename (GitHub follows the 301, the final answer has the recorded ids and another `full_name`): what does it write, and under which name is the release (Registry R-TP10)? | The release is under the name of the URL. R-TP9 checks the ids against the row of the URL name. No row of another name changes. A URL name with no row gets a row with `bind`, and the reconcile or the webhook sets it `moved` later. Reason: the ingest does not know the row of the current name, and a mark `moved` with the old name as successor would reverse the rename (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP18 rows 5, 6). |
| Q261 | Rename chains and a rename back to an earlier name: which rows become `moved` or `active` (Registry R-TP10)? | At a new name, each row with the same `repository_id` that is not `moved` becomes `moved` with the new name as successor. A `moved` row stays. When the current name has a `moved` row with the same ids, that row becomes `active` with no successor and the event `rename`, and the other rows that are not `moved` become `moved` with it as successor. The old proposal (a `moved` row stays as it is) would keep the current name `moved` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP18 rows 1, 3). |
| Q262 | The re-check of R-AP41 row 3 for the ids of an ingest: the 403 uses the row of call 1, and the row can change before the write batch (Registry R-AP41)? | No new check in the batch. Each row change is guarded with the old ids and each event has `changes() > 0`. Reason: a row changes between the 2 calls only by a change that R-TP9 accepts, so a request that shares one id with the old row shares one with the new row, except after 2 changes in about 100 ms. The release is public data with a checked hash (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-AP41 row 3). |
| Q263 | A name bound to other ids whose repository is not public now: 422 or 403 (Registry R-AP68, R-PU32)? | 422 `not-a-candidate`. The visibility (row 6) comes before the ids, so the answer for a private repository is one answer for any ids (R-PU32). The ids check is before rows 7 and 8 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-AP68 row 6). |
| Q264 | Does an ingest that sets a `missing` row `active` also clear `withdrawn` = `repo-missing` (Registry R-TP11, R-PU24)? | No. It sets the state and the event `found`, and sets `repo_checks.next_check_at` to the smaller of its value and now. The reconcile clears the releases after the asset check (R-PU20) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP19). |
| Q265 | `went_missing` of a `moved` row (Registry R-TP11)? | No change and no event. The row keeps its successor. The state `missing` is for the row of the current name, and R-PU24 withdraws the releases of every name of the repository (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP19). |
| Q266 | The order of the events of one change and the first `seq` of a name (Registry R-TP13, R-RE15)? | The first `seq` is 1, then the largest plus 1. In one row: `transfer` or `recreate`, then `found`. An event is added only by a statement that changed a row. `claims` is NULL for an ingest (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP19). |
| Q267 | A publish whose token ids both differ from the `repos` row: 403 `forbidden` (R-TP3 row 5) or 403 `repository-changed` (R-TP9 row 4) (Registry R-TP3, R-TP9)? | `repository-changed` with `issues_url`: it is the same check, and the publisher needs the issues URL. The other failed claims stay `forbidden` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 113, Registry R-TP3 row 5, R-TP12). Slice 108 Q108-3 had the same answer. |
| Q250 | Slice 104g, Q9: a `title`, `detail`, `issues_url`, `field` or `limit` of a problem body (R-AP21) that is `null` or of a wrong JSON type. Is the body still problem details? | Yes. Each optional member of a problem body is read as missing when its value is `null` or of a wrong type, as `minimum` is (R-AP74 row 2). Only `type` and `status` are strict: a missing or wrong-type `type` or `status` is `BadJson`. Reasons: RFC 9457, 3.1 says that a member whose value type does not match must be ignored; the client must not exit 15 when a later Worker changes an optional member (R-AP44). The registry still writes the members in the form of R-AP21 and R-AP51 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 104g, R-AP21, R-AP52, R-AP74). |
| Q251 | The platform edge of a record: a new member, or a derived rule (CLI plan G-C51, slice 206; Registry R-PU38 row 13, Q128)? | A new member: each dep has `is_platform` (boolean), stored as `deps.platform` (default 0) from the header parse the Worker already does. `Release.pf` of a package or platform record is the `url` of its marked dep. An app record gives no `pf` (R-C3 is about packages). Q128 stands: the platform of an app is `platform_hash` only. Rejected: a derived rule (the record has no mark, so no rule can tell the platform dep from another dep), a read of the archive for each record (it defeats the closure answer), the URL of the platform on an app record (nothing needs it). Wire: a member is added to each dep, so the bytes of answers with deps change once, before any deploy. Slices: records 104i, Worker 125 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP81). |
| Q400 | Does `Artifact.decode` refuse 2 deps with `is_platform` true, or a mark in an app record (Registry R-AP81; records slice 104i)? | No. The decoder is a reader (R-AP51) used by the CLI and the Worker: it keeps the marks as sent. The Worker enforces the rule at the write: roc refuses a header with 2 platform entries (`multiple_platforms`), so ingest and publish give 422 `not-a-candidate` (R-PU38 row 13), and an app never gets a marked dep (its platform is `platform_hash` only, Q128). The client takes the first mark in the answer order and reads no mark of an app record. Rejected: a decoder that refuses (one bad record would stop a whole resolve for a fact the client can handle) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP81). |
| Q401 | An app record with a marked dep: is the client entry flag `is_platform` of that dep true or false (Registry R-AP81; CLI plan G-C55, slice 206c)? | False. The client finds the platform dep with one function (a package or platform record: the first marked dep; an app record: none), and `pf` and the entry flag both come from it. The flag is the platform edge of the resolve and the lock (CLI R-C3), so it must not be true when `pf` is none. A registry never sends this case (Q400) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP81). |
| Q402 | Two marked deps: do both entries keep `is_platform` true, or only the first (Registry R-AP81; CLI plan G-C56, slice 206c)? | Only the first, the dep that gives `pf` (Q400). A second platform edge would make the closure walk see two platforms (R-AL9) and a `pf` that names only one of them. The same function as Q401 gives both. Rejected: the mark as sent (the flag could name a dep that `pf` does not) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP81). |
| Q403 | `--snapshot log:N`: is the release of a header hash with `log_index` above N hidden or kept (Registry R-AP19, R-AP63; Resolution 2, R-C9; CLI plan G-C57)? | Kept, with the toolchain row of the held `roc:` pin. R-C9 says a snapshot never hides the value that the header has now. The client does the hide on the answer and keeps each release whose hash is in the `hashes` of its request. The Worker has no snapshot parameter, so no wire or ETag change. Rejected: hide all (a held value would leave its own domain, and `tln update` with a snapshot would break) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP19). |
| Q404 | A user `--snapshot log:N` and the second query of a held move: hidden by N, by the first `log_size`, or by the smaller (Registry R-AP16, R-AP19; Resolution 9, R-SP24; CLI plan G-C58)? | By the smaller of the two. The first answer is hidden above N, so the second answer must be hidden above N too, and above the first `log_size` because the log grows between the two queries (R-AP18, one state). The keep list of Q403 applies to both (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP19). |
| Q405 | What does a snapshot do to `artifacts`, `unknown`, `checked`, `repos` and `log_size` of the answer (Registry R-AP19; CLI plan G-C59)? | Nothing: the client changes none of them. An artifact of a hidden release is never read (R-AL9). `unknown`, `checked` and `repos` are facts of the index at the answer time. `log_size` stays the value of the answer. The printed number is N of the flag, else the first `log_size` (R-DT5). The hide is only on the release rows and the toolchain rows (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP19). |
| Q406 | Does CLI slice 210 show a `repos` state (Registry R-AP32; CLI plan G-C60)? | No. The rules that show a state or a successor are R-PU28 to R-PU30, and their client part is P3. The client decodes `repos` and reads nothing from it in P2 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP30). |
| Q407 | `--json` `notes`: are all notes objects, or do index notes go in a second list (Registry R-AP30; CLI 3, R-J6; CLI plan G-C61)? | A second list. `data.notes` stays a list of texts, because a changed type of a member is a new `schema` (R-J6) and it would change every pinned output of every command. Each index note text is in `notes` too. The structured form is `data.index_notes[]` `{kind, name, checked_at, text}`, kind `index-stale`, `checked_at` in ms or `null`, and the member is left out when there is no index note, so no existing output changes. The age text `3d`, `5h`, `12m`, `0m` is accepted as a proposal (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP30). |
| Q408 | Which of the 2 answers of a held move gives the index note of a repository (Registry R-AP16, R-AP30; CLI plan G-C62)? | One note for each repository, from the stale item with the older `checked_at` of the two answers (`null` is older than any time). A fresh item and a stale item give the stale one. Sorted by `name`. Reason: the older time is the true age of the index that part of the resolve used (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP30). |
| Q409 | Can 2 epochs of one target in a closure answer overlap, and what does the client do if they do (Registry R-AP17; CLI plan G-C66, slice 209)? | The Worker cannot send it: the epochs are maximal intervals of one epoch number for each compiler and target. Epochs of different targets may share a compiler, and the client reads target `any` only. If one answer has an overlap in one target, the client takes the first epoch in the order of the answer and does not stop. Rejected: exit 15 (it needs the compiler order in the reader for a case the Worker cannot produce; the facts are evidence of lower rank, not a lock) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP17). |
| Q420 | The timeout of one ingest POST in R-AP28 case 2 (CLI plan G-C67)? | 20 s for the whole answer, the value of every small request (CLI R-DA9, R-CC12: each step that is not a wait ends in under 25 s). The Worker needs a few seconds for an ingest (3 GitHub reads, an archive of at most 100 MB, 2 D1 calls), and an ingest is idempotent, so a cut ingest is not a loss: the second query shows if it was indexed (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q421 | An ingest of R-AP28 case 2 with no answer, a 5xx, a 3xx or a 202 (CLI plan G-C68)? | It is case 3 for that URL (the archive), with one warning that names the URL and the status or `no answer`. The command goes on and the exit code does not change: the closure query worked, and the archive gives the right facts. A 429 is exit 15. Another 4xx is case 3 with no warning. No retry (R-AP34). Rejected: exit 15 (one failed ingest would stop a command that has the means to go on) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q422 | How the client finds the largest `Talon-Bookmark`, and the order of the headers of the second query (CLI plan G-C69)? | The bookmark is opaque except its order: the longer text is larger, and of one length the byte order decides. D1 bookmarks are fixed-width lower-case hex parts, so this is the order of the numbers, as in the tests. The order of the headers is not part of the contract (RFC 9110, 5.3); the proposal `User-Agent`, `Content-Type`, `Cache-Control`, `Talon-Bookmark` is fine for the tests (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q423 | A hash still in `unknown` after the second query, and ingests (200 or 201) with no bookmark (CLI plan G-C71)? | The hash is case 3, with no more ingest and no third query. With no bookmark the second query has `Cache-Control: no-cache` only (R-AP55), and it is sent when at least one ingest answered 200 or 201 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q424 | Does the 429 message give the `Retry-After` time (Registry R-AP25; CLI plan G-C70)? | Yes. `Registry.refuse` gets the `Retry-After` value (whole seconds), and the message gives it; with no valid value it has no time. This is R-AP25 as written, so slice 201 gets a header argument (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q425 | The facts of a `Label` value (case 1, R-AP65) and the `fixed` note (CLI plan G-C72)? | The facts come from the archive, as case 3: the label in the registry has another hash, so its record is another artifact. The `fixed: not in the registry index` note follows the case 3 rule: when the answer has no release of the group of the value. The warning of case 1 is also printed (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP28). |
| Q440 | The answer of a trap (`on_trap`, platform 0.0.3) and its problem type (slice 123d)? | A new type `/errors/busy`, 503, in the table of R-AP21. `oom`, `shed`, `cpu`, `deadline`, `alarm` and `cancelled` use it, with a fixed `detail` for each. `crashed`, `event`, `failed` and `default` use `/errors/internal`, 500. All are `application/problem+json` with `cache-control: no-store`. `Retry-After: 1` only on `oom` and `shed` (the shim adds it, TR7): a `cpu` or `deadline` request would use the same budget again. The crash text is never in the body. `tln` reads a 5xx as exit 15. Records gets `Problem.Kind` `Busy` in slice 104j (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP82). |
| Q441 | The limit on decoded bytes after the tar end with `feed!` (Registry R-PU38 row 5; slice 123c)? | All decoded bytes count (members, headers, padding, zeros after the end). The limit is 256 MiB plus 16 MiB, 285,212,672 bytes: 10,000 members need at most about 10 MB of headers and padding. Over it: 422 `not-a-candidate`, the decoding stops at once, the hash of the packed stream still reads to the end (at most 100 MB), and the order of refusals is row 9. CPU: at the measured speed the limit is about 18 s, inside 30 s (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-PU38 row 5). |
| Q442 | Invalid UTF-8 in a query value with `query_all` (Registry R-SN6, R-SN7, R-AP56; slice 123b)? | The platform decode stands: `%FF` becomes U+FFFD, the answer repeats it, 200, no match. No 400 and no new rule of the check order. `tln` builds each value from text, so it never sends this. The plan row named R-AP23 (media types); the rules are R-SN6, R-SN7 and R-AP56 (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-SN6). |
| Q443 | `hints/ct13-hint-quota`: 1 or 2 reads of the tag list when the case clock jumps 12 h (Registry R-CT12; slice 123a)? | 2 reads. R-CT12 reads the list at most once an hour on the clock of the shim, and the case clock is now the shim clock, so request 3 is 12 h after request 2 and the `Memo` has expired. The expected `github.log` of the case changes to 2 lines (the file `research/slice-123-revert/proposed/ct13-hint-quota-github.log`). The code keeps the `Memo` (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-CT12). |
| Q444 | Do slice 123e (the W2 runner on the platform test helper) now? | No. Drop it for now and record it as a G row (G19 in the registry plan). Reasons: its gain is not measured (the probe could not start the Worker: `startApp` refuses the 3 `ratelimit` bindings, reported to the platform), it changes the whole runner (493 lines) and 11 case files, and it is not on the path of 123a to 123d. The W2 run stays at about 69 s against the 45 s of `TESTING.md` TW20 until the platform accepts the bindings and the probe gives a number. The overshoot is a known item of the G row, not a failure of a slice (decided 2026-11-03, Registry plan G19). |
| Q445 | A 503 `/errors/busy` on a read (the closure POST, R-AP1, R-AP2, R-AP13, the toolchain list, short names): one wait and one more try, or exit 15 at once; and the message (Registry R-AP82, R-AP34; CLI plan G-C89)? | Exit 15 at once, no retry. R-AP34 limits retries to the writes that need them (publish, yank, move): a read is safe to run again, a retry inside a read adds a wait to a step that must end in under 25 s (R-CC12, with the 20 s timeout of Q420), and the read cache and the next command already give the second try. The message names the cause when the type is `/errors/busy`: `the registry is busy (503). Try again in <n> s.` with the `Retry-After` value, else `Try again later.` Another 5xx keeps the general text. `--json` `error.details` has `status` 503 and `type` `/errors/busy` (R-J7). Rejected: one retry for a read (it hides a registry that is down and doubles the worst time) (decided 2026-11-03, Registry R-AP34, R-AP82). |
| Q280 | Slice 119a, Q1: where does the installation id of the token request come from (Registry R-PU25, R-PU41)? | The setting `GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID`. No lookup at first use (`GET /orgs/roc-talon/installation`). Reasons: no extra call, no second `Memo`, no org name in the code, and the value is fixed (177657685). `API.md` RN18 already says "the App id and the installation id" (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU41, R-WH14). |
| Q281 | Slice 119a, Q2: the form of the secret `GITHUB_APP_KEY` (Registry R-PU41, R-WH14)? | A PKCS#8 PEM. GitHub gives PKCS#1 and WebCrypto `importKey` takes PKCS#8 or JWK only. The operator converts the key once (`openssl pkcs8 -topk8 -nocrypt`), a deploy note for slice 121 in R-WH14. A PKCS#1 PEM is a bad setting. Rejected: a Worker that wraps PKCS#1 (about 25 lines of code for a step that runs once) and a JWK text (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU41, R-WH14). |
| Q282 | Slice 119a, Q3: which hosts get the installation token (Registry R-PU42)? | Only a URL that starts with `https://api.github.com/`. The raw read of `.roc-version` and the asset download get the user agent only: a public repository needs no token there, and the 5,000 requests an hour are for the REST API (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU42). |
| Q283 | Slice 119a, Q4: the answer for a missing setting, a sign fault and a failed token request (Registry R-PU43, R-AP21, R-AP48)? | A setting that is missing or not valid, or a sign fault: 500 `internal` (fixed detail, R-AP48). A failed token request (status other than 201, no answer, bad body): 502 `upstream`. The hint path gives the key 0 for every kind (Q178) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU43). |
| Q284 | Slice 119a, Q5: a held token that GitHub refuses with 401, and a token request that fails each time (Registry R-PU44)? | No special handling now, and no negative cache. A token dies early only when the installation is removed, and then no read can pass. A failed request is asked again by the next read, and the rate limits bound the rate. Later slice if the edge shows a need (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU44). |
| Q285 | Slice 119a, Q6: the `iss` of the App JWT (Registry R-PU41)? | The app id as a JSON number. The GitHub docs accept the client id or the app id (and recommend the client id), and the real check passed with the app id (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU41). |
| Q290 | Slice 114, Q114-1: do the URL rules of a publish come before the token check (Registry R-AP75, R-AP71)? | Yes: after the body checks and the fact values, before the token. The token check needs `owner/repo` and the tag of the URL, the rules cost no D1 call, no GitHub call and no JWKS read, and the ingest puts them right after the body (R-AP68 row 2). A bad URL with a bad token is 422 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP75 row 6). |
| Q291 | Slice 114, Q114-2: the order of the faults inside one fact (Registry R-AP71)? | `nightly`, then `target`, then `result`, the order of the members of R-AP71 and of the bot row (R-AP66 check 6, R-AP70 row 8). Facts in the order of the body. The first fault is the answer (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP71 "Order of the values"). |
| Q292 | Slice 114, Q114-3: R-TP3 row 4 says a host other than `github.com` is 403, R-PU40 says no path indexes it (422)? | 422 `not-a-candidate` at the URL rules, before the token check. The host test of the claim check stays as a guard that no request reaches. Reason: one answer for one URL on the ingest and the publish, and the token is not read for a URL that is no candidate (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP75 row 6, R-TP3 row 4, R-PU40). |
| Q293 | Slice 114, Q114-4: the answer for a `repository_id` or `repository_owner_id` claim that is missing or not a number (Registry R-TP3 row 5)? | 403 `forbidden`, the `detail` names the claim, with no D1 call. Both ids must be numbers (digits, at most 2^64 - 1), also when the other one is equal to the row, because the `repos` row keeps both. 403 is the status of the claim rows 4 to 8, and 401 is for a token that is not valid (rows 1 to 3). The form check runs with rows 4, 6, 7, 8, so a token with a bad `ref` gets the 403 of `ref` (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP75 row 8, R-TP3 row 5). |
| Q294 | Slice 114, Q114-5: 403 `repository-changed` or 409 `label-conflict` first (Registry R-TP3 row 5, R-PU19)? | 403 first, at D1 call 1 (one call, no GitHub read). R-TP3 row 5 is a check of the token, so an account that does not own the name learns nothing about the labels of the name. The ingest has 409 first (R-AP68 rows 4, 6) because it has no ids before its GitHub read. A publish has them from the claims (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP75 rows 10, 11). |
| Q295 | Slice 114, Q114-6: the whole order of the publish path (Registry R-AP71 "Order")? | The 16 rows of R-AP75: route and client version and media type, size, body decode, fact limit, fact values, URL rules, token, claim rows 4, 6, 7, 8 and the id form, D1 call 1, ids against the row, label, release read, archive, `.roc-version`, write batch, cache drop. Rows 1 to 3 are those of R-AP70, the body rows are those of R-AP66, and the rows after the token are those of R-AP68 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP75). |
| Q296 | Slice 114, Q114-7: does a publish have an IP limit or an owner limit (Registry R-PU34)? | No limit in P2. A token is signed by GitHub for this registry, is of a public repository, and fits one tag of the URL, so one token publishes one release of its own repository, and a repeat is idempotent and bounded. An ingest gives the same reach with less proof and has the limits. An IP limit would hit a CI job that publishes a monorepo from a shared address. The gap is names without the owner count. Later option: `limit!` keyed by the claim `repository_id` (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU34 row 5). |
| Q297 | Slice 114, Q114-8: how does a draft show when the release read gives 404 (Registry R-PU16, R-AP68 row 8)? | 422 `not-a-candidate` in both cases. A draft that the Worker sees (the App is on the repository) says `draft`. A 404 says that the repository has no published release with the tag and that a draft is not published. The text names the cause because the caller is the author (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-PU16, R-AP75 row 12). |
| Q298 | Slice 114, Q114-9: is `repo_events.claims` the same text as the `claims` of the provenance row (Registry R-TP6, R-TP13)? | Yes, one text for one request: the 8 claims of R-TP6 in that order, `null` for a missing claim. A repeated publish adds no provenance row, and its events carry its own claims. The JSON form is a proposal (TW24) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-TP6, R-TP13). |
| Q300 | Slice 117, Q117-1: the form of the secret `TOKEN_KEY`, and where the `kid` comes from (Registry R-CT9, R-WH14)? | The text of a private Ed25519 JWK (RFC 8037: `kty` `OKP`, `crv` `Ed25519`, `x`, `d`, `kid`). The `kid` of the JWT is its member `kid`, which must not be empty. The Worker takes the public key from the same text. A text that is not such a JWK is 500 `internal`. Rejected: PKCS#8 in base64 (the `kid` would need a second setting) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-WH14, R-CT9). |
| Q301 | Slice 117, Q117-2: `iat` and `exp` are NumericDate in seconds and `tokens_after` is in ms. A login and a revoke in one second (Registry R-CT9)? | `iat` and `exp` are seconds with 3 decimals (`1767225601.234`). RFC 7519, 2 allows a fraction, `roc-jwt` 0.0.1 reads them as `Dec`, and `tln` does not read the token (it uses `expires_at`). The compare with `tokens_after` is in ms, and equal is good. Change to the proposal: `exp` and an `iat` in the future have a leeway of 60 s (as R-TP3 row 3), because two machines of the edge can have clocks that differ, and only the Worker signs, so an `iat` in the future proves nothing. Rejected: whole seconds (in the second of a revoke, a new token is refused or an old token is not stopped) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT9). |
| Q302 | Slice 117, Q117-3: `contributor_totals` has no `last_time`. What is `last_time` of the tracker after a trim (Registry R-CT10, R-CT15, R-RE25)? | Change to the proposal: `contributor_totals` gets the column `last_time` (INTEGER NOT NULL ms), the largest `time` of the trimmed rows. The trim sets it to the larger value, the login move of R-CT8 row 3 takes the larger, and the tracker takes the larger of the rows and the totals. So R-CT15 holds: the tracker is the same before and after a trim. The schema is not deployed, so the column goes in `sql/0001_init.sql` (no new migration). Rejected: `last_time` from the rows only (it falls after a trim), and `contributors.last_time` (that is the last login, and a machine has no row) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-RE25, R-CT10, R-CT15). |
| Q303 | Slice 117, Q117-4: the form of `github_token` (Registry R-AP37, R-AP51)? | A JSON text, not empty, at most 512 bytes, each byte `!` to `~`. Else 400. A GitHub token today has 40 (`gho_`) to 93 (`github_pat_`) characters, so 512 leaves space for a longer form. The id of `Talon-Contributor` is checked before the body decode (as R-AP70 rows 5 and 6) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT16 rows 1 and 2). |
| Q304 | Slice 117, Q117-5: which GitHub answers are "GitHub refuses the token" (Registry R-CT8 row 4)? | Only status 401: 401 `unauthenticated`. Each other status (also 403 and 429), no answer, and a 200 with no integer `id` above 0 or no `login`: 502 `upstream`. Reason: a GitHub limit is not a refusal of the token, and `tln` must not drop a good login for it (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT8 row 4). |
| Q305 | Slice 117, Q117-6: a good token whose account has no `contributors` row (Registry R-CT9, R-RE17, R-AP36)? | `GET /v1/contributors/me` and `DELETE /v1/tokens`: 401 ("log in again", CLI R-SH8). `DELETE /v1/contributors/me`: 204, as R-AP36 says for a request when nothing was stored (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT16 row 4). |
| Q306 | Slice 117, Q117-7: `DELETE /v1/contributors/me` with both headers, or with neither (Registry R-AP36, R-CT7)? | A header `Authorization` is the account form and `Talon-Contributor` is ignored (a token wins, as R-CT1). A bad token is 401 and deletes nothing. Neither header: 400 `bad-request` (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT16 row 7). |
| Q307 | Slice 117, Q117-8: `tokens_after` of a new `contributors` row (Registry R-RE17, R-CT8, R-CT9, R-CT7)? | The time of the login, not 0. Else a delete followed by a login makes the tokens from before the delete good again. A login never changes `tokens_after` of a row that exists (`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, not `INSERT OR REPLACE`) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-RE17, R-CT16 row 5). |
| Q308 | Slice 117, Q117-9: the place of `RL_LOGIN` in the order, and is the budget shared (Registry R-CT11)? | One binding and one budget for `POST /v1/tokens` and both forms of `DELETE /v1/contributors/me`. After each check that needs no D1 call and no GitHub read (the body; the token proof of the account form; the id of the machine form), before the first of them, as Q168 and Q186. `Retry-After: 60`. The tracker and `DELETE /v1/tokens` have no limit: they need a valid token and do no GitHub read (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT11, R-CT16 row 9). |
| Q309 | Slice 117, Q117-10: a read session or the primary for the tracker and the token check (Registry R-AP41, R-AP35)? | The primary, for all 4 account routes, one batch, 1 D1 call (the login after its GitHub read). A revoke must stop a token at once, and `tln` reads the tracker just after a login. An exception to R-AP41 row 5 (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP41 row 5, R-CT16 row 10). |
| Q310 | Slice 117, Q117-11: how does the Worker keep the old public key after a key change (Registry R-CT9)? | Not in P2. The Worker has one key, and a token with another `kid` is 401. The operator changes `TOKEN_KEY` only after a leak, and then the old tokens must stop at once, so keeping the old key would be wrong for that case. A planned change with a set of old public keys selected by `kid` is a later item (`docs/registry/PLAN.md` G W7). The sentence of R-CT9 is changed (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT9). |
| Q311 | Slice 117, Q117-12: a login with no `Talon-Contributor` (Registry R-AP37, R-CT8)? | It upserts the `contributors` row only: no link and no move (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-CT8 row 3). |
| Q312 | Slice 117, Q117-13: R-AP35 says `no-store`, R-AP24 says each 200 of a `GET` in `/v1` has an `ETag` (Registry R-AP24, R-AP35)? | No `ETag` on the tracker: the answer is of one account and no cache keeps it. R-AP24 names the exception (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP24). |
| Q320 | Slice 118, Q118-1: does the signature of the webhook come before the body parse or after (Registry R-AP48 rows 6, 7, R-AP12)? | The signature first. The reason of R-AP48 for the parse before the token (no D1 call, no JWKS read, nothing shown) holds for a local HMAC too, and an HMAC is cheaper than the JSON parse. So bytes that nobody signed never reach the parser. A body that is not JSON with a bad signature gets 401, not 400. The order is in R-AP79 (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-AP79, R-AP48). |
| Q321 | Slice 118, Q118-2: a webhook request with a good signature and no `X-GitHub-Event` header (Registry R-AP12)? | 400 `bad-request`, also for an empty value. GitHub sends the header with every delivery, so the request is not a delivery of GitHub as it is. The check is after the signature (an unsigned request is 401) and before the body is read. A name of an event that the Worker does not use is 202 with no message (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-AP79 rows 6, 7). |
| Q322 | Slice 118, Q118-3: what do the `installation` actions `suspend`, `unsuspend` and `new_permissions_accepted` do (Registry R-PU11, R-PU26)? | `suspend` sets `app_installed` to 0 and `unsuspend` to 1, as `deleted` and `created`; `new_permissions_accepted` is ignored. The GitHub docs do not say that a suspended installation gets events. A wrong 0 costs a read every hour (R-PU22), a wrong 1 leaves the index old for up to a day. All five actions have `repositories` in the payload, so no new read is needed. R-PU26 "(added, removed)" is read as the actions of both events (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU11, R-PU26). |
| Q323 | Slice 118, Q118-4: are the retry delays 1, 5, 15 and 60 min one after another or total times since the event (Registry R-PU13, R-PU35)? | One after another, each from the failed attempt: the reads come at 0, 1, 6, 21 and 81 min after the event. The delay of `retry` is a delay from now, and a total time needs the event time in the message with no gain (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU45, R-PU13). |
| Q324 | Slice 118, Q118-5: which rows of a repository with a `moved` row do `publicized` and `installation` change (Registry R-TP19, R-PU26, R-PU33)? | `publicized`: `next_check_at` of each row of the repository id whose state is not `moved`, because the check of the current name clears the releases of every name (R-TP19). `installation`, `installation_repositories`: `app_installed` of every row of the repository id, also a `moved` row, because the App is on the repository and not on a name. `privatized` is as R-TP19 says (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-TP19, R-PU26, R-PU33). |
