| Q325 | Slice 118, Q118-6: does the job `privatized` read `GET /repositories/{id}` and ignore an old event with a public answer (Registry R-PU33, R-PU31, R-PU12)? | Yes. The action is a hint. A 404, `private` or `internal` withdraws; `public` changes nothing; a failed read is a failed job and is retried. Events can come again or in another order, and the read gives the state now (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU33). |
| Q326 | Slice 118, Q118-7: the limit of the webhook body (Registry R-AP46)? | 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes) for this route only. The 256 KB of R-AP46 is for one D1 statement, and the webhook body goes to none. Measured: a normal payload is 30 to 70 KB, but `edited` holds 2 release bodies of up to 125,000 characters (about 260 KB with ASCII text, about 760 KB with 3-byte characters), and a release can have many assets (about 1.6 KB each). GitHub sends no payload over 25 MB. A body over 1 MiB gets 413, a failed delivery in GitHub, and the reconcile finds the change (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-AP79, R-AP46). |
| Q327 | Slice 118, Q118-8: a missing or empty `GITHUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (Registry R-WH14, R-AP12)? | 500 `internal`, never the empty key. The check is after the static checks (path, method, size, media type) and before the signature header, so a deploy without the secret gives 500 for every request that gets past them (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-AP79 row 4). |
| Q328 | Slice 118, Q118-9: the endpoint of the release read of the job (Registry R-PU12)? | `GET /repos/{full_name}/releases/{release_id}`, with the `full_name` of the answer of `GET /repositories/{repository_id}` of the same job. The message has no name (R-PU32). A 404 stops the job (118b) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU12). |
| Q329 | Slice 118, Q118-10: is the consumer and `dead_letter_queue` in `wrangler.toml` enough for `INDEX_DLQ`? | Yes. The code has no producer for it, so `main.roc` has no binding. R-WH14 still lists it (a consumer only), because the operator makes the queue (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU45, R-WH14). |
| Q330 | Slice 118, Q118-11: is a `retry` at the last attempt a failed delivery that moves the message to the dead letter queue (Registry R-PU35)? | Yes (Cloudflare docs: a message goes to the dead letter queue when it reaches the retry limit). A local run cannot show it, so the first deploy checks it once with a throwaway pair of queues and `max_retries = 1`. If the check fails, the consumer at attempt 5 sends the message to `INDEX_DLQ` itself and acks it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU45). |
| Q331 | Slice 118: the queue names `talon-index` and `talon-index-dlq` (TW24), and must R-WH14 list them? | They stay a proposal for the review. R-WH14 lists them next to the binding names, because `wrangler queues create` and `wrangler.toml` use the names and the code uses the bindings (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-WH14, R-PU45). |
| Q332 | Slice 118b, Q118-9a: the `withdrawn` value of an `edited` event for a release that is now a prerelease (Registry R-PU17)? | `edited`. R-PU17 says the action, and the column allows `edited` (`sql/0001_init.sql`) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU17). |
| Q333 | Slice 118b, Q118-9b: the log line of a label conflict on the webhook path (Registry R-PU14)? | `Log.warn!("index label conflict", { repository_id, release_id, group, version })`. The repository is public (R-PU31), so the label is public data; a name is not in the line (R-PU32). The text is a proposal (CLI 3, R-OUT2). The path writes nothing (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU14). |
| Q334 | Slice 118b, Q118-9c: what ends the job when the fifth read of a release still has no package asset (Registry R-PU13, R-PU35)? | The consumer acks it and writes an info log line. It is not a fault: a repository with the App can release files that are not a Roc package. The dead letter queue then holds faults only. The reconcile can still find the asset (R-PU21) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU45, R-PU13). |
| Q335 | Slice 118b, Q118-9d: how does `app_installed` get set for a repository that had no row when its installation event came (Registry R-PU26, R-PU32)? | A job `Release` of a message with an installation id of 1 or more sets `app_installed` to 1: for the rows of that repository, and for the row that it adds with a new name. The event proves that the App is on the repository, and GitHub does not send a lost event again (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118, Registry R-PU26). |
| Q336 | Slice 118b, Q118b-1: the action `deleted`. The release read gives 404, and neither the `releases` table nor the message has the tag. How does the job find the label to withdraw (Registry R-PU17, R-PU12)? | It does not find it. After a public repository read (R-PU31), a job `deleted` does no release read (the read of a deleted release always gives 404). It sets `next_check_at` to now for each `repo_checks` row of the repository id whose state is not `moved` (as `publicized`, Q324), and acks. The next reconcile (at most 5 min later) does not find the release in the list and sets `withdrawn` = `deleted` (R-PU23). A repository with no row writes nothing (R-PU32). Rejected: the tag in the message (R-PU12 does not use the event body, the message of R-PU11 has ids only and slice 118a pins its form, and a lost event needs the reconcile anyway) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU12, R-PU17). |
| Q337 | Slice 118b, Q118b-2: `IngestRoutes.index!` reads the release by tag again, so a new package costs 5 GitHub reads. Accepted, or must the job pass its release into `index!` (Registry R-PU25, R-PU42)? | Accepted. Only the REST reads count against the 5,000 an hour (R-PU25): the repository, the release by id and the release by tag, so 3 for a new package. The asset download and `.roc-version` do not count (R-PU42). A new package is rare. To pass the release in changes `IngestRoutes.Flow`, which the ingest and the publish share (slices 112, 114), to save 1 read. When the 2 reads differ (a race), `index!` decides: a 404 by tag is a refusal (Q361), and the archive read checks the hash (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU13). |
| Q338 | Slice 118b, Q118b-3: an event that is older than the state on GitHub (Registry R-PU12, R-PU17)? | R-PU12 gives it: the state that the release read shows governs, and the action only names the `withdrawn` value. A `published` or `released` event for a release that is a draft or a prerelease now writes nothing (the newer event or the reconcile withdraws it). An `unpublished` or `prereleased` event for a release that is final now is the same as `published` (index, clear). The proposal is taken, and R-PU17 now says it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU17). |
| Q339 | Slice 118b, Q118b-4a: a withdraw of a row that already has a `withdrawn` value (Registry R-PU17, R-PU24)? | The row keeps its first value and its time, as R-PU24 does for `repo-missing` ("not withdrawn yet") and as the job `privatized` does. The value is the first reason, and a candidate needs no value at all. R-PU17 now says it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU17). |
| Q340 | Does a toolchain row (Registry R-RE4, R-AP5) get a `size` and a `url` (CLI plan G-C42, slice 204; CLI 5, R-DL1)? | `size`, yes: the byte size of the asset from the GitHub release, written once with the row, a whole number above 0 (an asset with no such `size` is not an asset). Not in the compiler leaf, so the log does not change. `url`, no: `tln` builds `https://github.com/roc-lang/nightlies/releases/download/<tag>/<asset_name>` (the `browser_download_url` form of GitHub). The column is in `sql/0001_init.sql` because no Worker is deployed. Slices 109b (Worker, schema, fixtures) and 104h (`records` `Toolchain.Row`). Rejected: a `url` member (repeated bytes, and the registry would choose the download host), a nullable `size` (every asset of GitHub has one) (decided 2026-11-02, Registry R-AP80). |
| Q341 | Slice 122, Q122-1: what does a read do for a release with no `provenance` row (Registry R-AP49, R-PU15)? | It gives `provenance: []`, not 500. Each write path adds the row in the release batch, so the state is a data fault only, and no trigger can guard it (the row follows the release in the batch). A 500 would hide the other releases of a page or of a closure for one bad row. A reader accepts `[]` (R-AP51 rejects only a missing member or `null`) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 122, Registry R-AP49). |
| Q342 | Slice 122, Q122-2: in what order do 2 unknown `via` texts go (Registry R-AP49)? | Ascending byte order of the UTF-8 text, after the known values. The key (release, via) makes the texts unique, so the order is total. Input order is not used: a query without `ORDER BY` has no stable order (decided 2026-11-02, slice 122, Registry R-AP49). |
| Q343 | Slice 120, Q343: is the `hint_days` row of `today - 2` deleted by the daily cron (Registry R-RE6, R-PU35)? | No. A day is old when `day < today - 2`, where `today = now / 86400000` (strict). The rows of today, yesterday and `today - 2` stay. The trigger `contributions_count` (R-CT13) reads and writes only the row of the day of `NEW.time`, and `time` is the clock of the request (`Hints.insert_sql` ?2), so a read needs only the row of today. The edge keeps one more day than a read needs, so a request that runs across the start of a day, or a clock that differs by some seconds, never loses its counter. The counter of a day that was deleted starts again at 0, so a day is never deleted while its counter can still grow (decided 2026-11-02, slice 120, Registry R-RE6, R-PU35). |
| Q344 | Slice 120, Q344: a DELETE of the daily cron fails, or the read of the due compilers fails: is the run `Err`, or a log line and `Ok` (Registry R-PU35, R-PU36)? | A log line and `Ok`. The 3 steps (hint cleanup, `hint_days` delete, trim) are independent: a failure of one step writes `Log.warn!` and the run goes on with the next step. A failed read of the due compilers skips the trim of that run only. Reasons: the work stays due by the state of D1 (R-PU35), so the next daily run does it, and each statement is safe to run again (R-PU36). A failed step must not stop the other steps (as R-PU37 row 2). Production does not run a failed cron again (R-PU36, `cloudflare-workers/API.md` AQ12), so an `Err` would give no retry, only a line in Past Events. The log lines are a proposal (CLI 3, R-OUT2): `hint cleanup failed`, `hint days cleanup failed`, `trim read failed`, each with `{ error }` and no name (R-PU32) (decided 2026-11-02, slice 120, Registry R-PU35). |
| Q345 | Slice 120, Q345: is the clock of a scheduled job `Time.now_ms!()` or `time_ms` of the `Scheduled` event (Registry R-PU35, R-PU36)? | `Time.now_ms!()`, read once at the start of the run, for each job (the daily cron and the reconcile). The Worker has one clock (the platform 0.0.3), and the tests set it with `x-test-now`. The planned time is the time that the run should have started: a run that is late, or runs again, would then use an old cutoff. R-PU35 selects work by the state of D1 and the time now, not by the planned time, and R-PU36 holds because a second run finds no rows. The `time_ms` of the event is for a log line only (decided 2026-11-02, slice 120, Registry R-PU35, R-PU36). |
| Q360 | Slice 118b, Q118b-4b: does the webhook job clear `repo-missing`, and does a release with 2 or more package assets (`TooMany`) clear a row whose recorded asset is there (Registry R-PU17, R-PU20, R-PU24)? | `repo-missing`: no. Only the reconcile clears it, after the visibility read and the binding check of the repository (R-PU24, R-PU33). `TooMany`: no clear, a change to the proposal. R-PU17 clears only for a candidate, and a release with 2 or more package assets is not a candidate (R-PU6, CLI 7.3, R-DI4). R-PU20 still applies: a row with no value whose recorded asset is not in the assets gets `asset-replaced`. R-PU20 now says that its clear needs a candidate (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU17, R-PU20). |
| Q361 | Slice 118b, Q118b-7: what does the job do with `Identity.Changed` and with a `Refuse` of `IngestRoutes.index!` (Registry R-TP9, R-PU45)? | `Changed`: R-TP9 row 4 gives it: the job writes nothing for that name (no identity, release, provenance or new `repo_checks` row). The writes of the job by repository id (R-PU17, R-PU20, R-PU26) go to rows bound to that id, so they stay. Each 4xx refusal (403 `repository-changed`, 404 of the release by tag, 409 `label-conflict`, 422 of the archive read) is final: one line `Log.warn!("index job refused", { repository_id, release_id, type })` (the problem type, no name, R-PU32; the text is a proposal, CLI 3, R-OUT2), and ack. A retry gives the same answer. A 5xx refusal or a `Fault` is a failed job: retry, then the dead letter queue. The proposal is taken. The rule is R-PU45 row "Refusals" (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU45, R-TP9). |
| Q362 | Slice 118b, Q118b-5: Q335 gives the 24 h only for a row that the job adds. Does a row that exists keep `next_check_at`, and which outcomes set `app_installed` (Registry R-PU26, R-PU22)? | A row that exists keeps `next_check_at`, as for the `installation` event (R-PU26). Its next check is at most 1 h away, and then the reconcile sets the interval of 24 h (R-PU22). `app_installed`: R-PU26 has no condition on the release, so each job `Release` whose repository read shows public sets it, also for a conflict, no asset, 2 package assets, a stale event, `deleted`, a 404 or a refusal. A repository that is not public gets no write from it (R-PU33). This changes the proposal for the outcomes that are not tested (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU26). |
| Q363 | Slice 118b, Q118b-6: a release with no package asset whose recorded asset is gone (Registry R-PU13, R-PU20)? | Both rules apply: the job writes `asset-replaced` (R-PU20), then the job is a failure, so the queue reads it again (R-PU13). The write is in its own batch before the failure, so it stays. A later read finds a row with a value and writes nothing; when the asset is there again, R-PU20 clears and the job indexes. At attempt 5 the end of Q334 applies. The proposal is taken (decided 2026-11-02, slice 118b, Registry R-PU20). |
| Q370 | Slice 119, Q370: R-PU23 says "oldest first", and the claim of R-PU37 row 1 is one `UPDATE` with `RETURNING`, which gives the rows in no promised order and with the new `next_check_at` only. In which order does the run handle the claimed rows (Registry R-PU23, R-PU25, R-PU37 row 1)? | The claim selects the oldest rows (the subquery: `next_check_at`, then `repo`, at most 500). The run handles the claimed rows in the order of `repo` as a signed 64-bit integer. "Oldest first" is a rule of the selection, not of the order in the run. The order matters only for the reserve of R-PU25: the rows that move are the rows after the stop in this order. A moved row is due at the reset time, so the next selection takes it before the rows that come due later. Rejected: a batch of a `SELECT ... ORDER BY` and the `UPDATE` (2 statements that must select the same rows, for no gain). The proposal is taken (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119b, Registry R-PU37 row 1, R-PU23). |
| Q371 | Slice 119, Q371: R-PU31 sends `If-None-Match` on `GET /repositories/{id}`, and a 304 keeps the last result. `repo_checks` has one `etag` column, the etag of the list (R-PU23). What holds the etag and the last result of the visibility read (Registry R-PU31, R-RE14, R-PU25)? | A new column `repo_checks.repo_etag` (TEXT, NULL) in `sql/0001_init.sql` (no Worker is deployed, as Q302). The pass batch of a row stores it only when the read is public and its `full_name` in lower case is the name of the row. Else the batch stores NULL. A failed row does not change it (as `etag`, R-PU37 row 5). So a 304 means: public, the name of the row and the same ids. That is the whole last result, and no other column is needed. A row with no `repo_etag` sends no `If-None-Match`. A `moved` row never stores one (its `full_name` is another name), so its read costs 1 counted request at each check (Q375). Cost: without the column, each check costs 1 counted request for the visibility read (1,000 an hour for 1,000 repositories with no App, under the 5,000 of R-PU25, but shared with the index jobs). With it, most visibility reads are free 304 answers (`research/github-app-check/RESULT.md`). Slice 119c. Until then the visibility read sends no `If-None-Match` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119, Registry R-PU31, R-RE14). |
| Q372 | Slice 119, Q372: CLI R-DI2 follows `Link: rel="next"`. R-PU23 stores one etag, and a 304 for page 1 does not show a change on page 2. How does the reconcile read a list of more than 100 releases (Registry R-PU23, CLI R-DI2)? | The Worker reads each page: while an answer has a `Link` header with `rel="next"`, it reads `list_url(full_name)` plus `&page=<n>`. It makes the URL itself and does not use the URL of the `Link` header. At most 10 pages (1,000 releases, first guess). More pages: the row fails with `the release list has more than 10 pages` (a proposal, CLI 3, R-OUT2). All pages are one list for the work, and a release id that comes 2 times is kept once. A row whose list has more than one page stores `etag` NULL, so each check reads all pages again (each page is a counted request). A row with one page keeps its etag: when a new release makes a second page, page 1 changes and the answer is 200. A release that is deleted between 2 page reads can move a release up one page before it is read, so the work sets `deleted` on it; the next check sees it as a candidate and clears `deleted` (R-PU17), so the error stays at most one interval. Rejected: the etag of page 1 for the row (a deleted old release on page 2 is never found while page 1 does not change). Slice 119c. Until then the row fails with `the release list has more than one page` (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119, Registry R-PU23). |
| Q373 | Slice 119, Q373: R-PU21 says "the work is the same as an ingest" and "the provenance is `poll`". Does the reconcile add a `poll` row to each listed release that is indexed already, or only to new candidates (Registry R-PU21, R-PU14, R-PU15)? | Only to new candidates, through the index job (R-PU13, via 2). For a tag that is indexed already, the reconcile does not run the index flow (no release read and no download, R-PU23), only the checks of R-PU17, R-PU19 and R-PU20, so it adds no provenance row. A `poll` row there adds no fact: the release came in by another path, and the log has its label already. It would also be one more statement for each release in each batch of a 200. A job of a candidate that another path indexed in the meantime adds its `poll` row, as R-PU14 says. The proposal is taken, and R-PU21 now says it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119b, Registry R-PU21). |
| Q374 | Slice 119, Q374: R-PU23 says a 304 "changes only `checked_at` and `next_check_at`", and R-PU37 row 3 says a row that passes gets `fail_count` = 0. Does a 304 set `fail_count` to 0 (Registry R-PU23, R-PU37 row 3)? | Yes. A 304 is a pass: `fail_count` = 0, `checked_at` = now, `next_check_at` = now + the interval (`next_check(now, interval, 0)`). It changes no `etag` and no release. R-PU23 now says it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119b, Registry R-PU23). |
| Q375 | Slice 119, Q375: a `moved` row (R-TP11) has its own `repo_checks` row and comes due. The check of the current name does the work of every name of the repository (Q324). What does the claim of a `moved` row do (Registry R-PU23, R-TP19)? | The same work as any row: the read by id, the list of the current name, and the work on the releases of every name of the repository id (the claim gives them all). The writes are guarded by state (R-PU36), so a second check of the same repository in one interval changes nothing. Reason: after a move of R-AP31 (P3), the old repository can have no other row, and then only the check of the `moved` row reads it. Cost: 1 counted request for each `moved` row and check (no `repo_etag`, Q371). Its list read is mostly a free 304. Rejected: no read for a `moved` row (it needs a second rule to know that a row of the current name exists). The proposal is taken (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119b, Registry R-PU23). |
| Q376 | Slice 119, Q376: after R-PU24 (`repo-missing`), the etag of the list stays. When the repository is public again with the same releases, the list gives 304, and `repo-missing` is never cleared (Registry R-PU24, R-PU33)? | A rule change: each write of `repo-missing` (the withdraw of the reconcile, R-PU24, and of the job `privatized`, R-PU33) also sets `etag` and `repo_etag` to NULL on each `repo_checks` row of the repository id, in the same batch. So the first check that reads the repository as public gets a 200 list, and R-PU24 can clear. Rejected: send no `If-None-Match` while a release has `repo-missing` (a release that keeps `repo-missing`, for example one deleted while the repository was private, Q339, would then cost 1 counted request at each check for ever). Slice 119c: 119b clears no `repo-missing`, so a kept etag is no fault yet, and the change is in the statements that the job `privatized` shares (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119, Registry R-PU24, R-PU33). |
| Q377 | Slice 119, Q377: R-PU25 moves `next_check_at` of "the rest of the batch". Does the reserve on the visibility read stop the list read of the same row (Registry R-PU25)? | No. The row that gets the answer under the reserve does all its work first (its list read too, and all its pages, Q372). Then the run moves the claimed rows that it did not start (in the order of Q370), writes the log line and stops. So a row never stops in the middle. Reason: the reserve is 10 % of the limit (500 requests), so some more reads are safe, and a row that stops in the middle needs a third kind of end (no pass, no failure). An answer that is a failure (for example a 403 at a limit of 0) is a failed row (R-PU37 row 3). This changes the proposal (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119b, Registry R-PU25). |
| Q378 | Slice 119, Q378: does the reconcile write `found` itself (the ingest does it in R-TP19 row "Found"), in the batch of the row (Registry R-TP11, R-TP19)? | Yes. R-TP11 gives it: a `missing` row gets `active` again (event `found`) when a read of the same `repository_id` succeeds, and a public read by id is such a read. The reconcile writes it in the batch of the row (R-PU37 row 5), on the row of the current name (Q380), with the guard `state` = `missing`, and the event only when the statement changed a row (R-TP19 "No change", R-PU36). Order: R-TP9 (`transfer`) first, then `found` (R-TP19 "Order"), then the clear of `repo-missing` (R-PU24). A failed list read or a failed batch writes no `found`, so the next check does it (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119c, Registry R-PU24, R-TP11). |
| Q379 | Slice 119, Q379: what is the binding check of R-PU24 ("the binding of section 7.1 allows it") for the reconcile, and is a transfer a pass (Registry R-PU24, R-TP9)? | R-TP9 with the ids of the read by id against the `repos` row of the claimed name. The read by id always has the recorded `repository_id`, so only R-TP9 rows 1 and 2 can apply, and both accept. Row 2 (a transfer): the row gets the new `repository_owner_id` and the event `transfer`, and the clear goes on. So for a read by id that is public, the binding always allows the clear. A name that another account took (R-TP9 row 4) is not seen by a read by id: GitHub gives the repository of the id. The ingest of that name finds it (403, R-TP12). R-PU24 now says that only the withdraw keeps the binding ids (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119c, Registry R-PU24, R-TP9). |
| Q380 | Slice 119, Q380: a rename found by the read by id (the `full_name` is not the name of the row). Does the new name get a `repos` row and a `repo_checks` row in the batch of the row, with the events `rename`, as R-TP18 row 1 (Registry R-TP10, R-TP18, R-PU26)? | Yes. The reconcile writes the identity rows of an ingest of `F` (R-TP18 rows 1, 3 and 4 with `U` = `F`), where `F` is the `full_name` of the read by id in lower case. Row 1 (`F` has no row): the `repos` row of `F` (`active`, the ids of the read, event `rename`, no `bind`), the `repo_checks` row of `F` (`app_installed` of the claimed row, `next_check_at` = now + the interval of R-PU22), and each row of the id that is not `moved` gets `moved` with the successor `F` (event `rename`). Row 3 (the row of `F` is `moved` with the same id): as R-TP18 row 3. Row 4: R-TP9 and `found` on the row of `F` (Q378). A row of `F` with another `repository_id` gets no identity write, and the list work of the repository id goes on. All in the batch of the row, before the pass update. The statements keep the SQL guards of slice 113 (`WHERE NOT EXISTS`, `state <> moved`), because a webhook or an ingest can write between the claim and the batch (R-PU36). Reason for the `repo_checks` row: `checked` of `F` (R-AP29) reads the row of `F`, and a later ingest of `F` finds a `repos` row and adds no `repo_checks` row (R-TP18 row 4). The code reuses the identity statements of `IngestRoutes` (slice 113). The proposal is taken (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119c, Registry R-TP18, R-TP10, R-PU26). |
| Q381 | Slice 119c, Q381: a read by id can give a rename and a transfer at the same time (`F` is not the row name, and the owner id is not that of the claimed row). Which row gets R-TP9 row 2, the new owner id and the event `transfer` (Registry R-TP9, R-TP18, R-PU30)? | Both rows that the rules name, a change to the proposal. The claimed row gets R-TP9 row 2 first (the new owner id, the event `transfer`), then `moved` with the successor `F` (the event `rename`), as Q379 says. A new row of `F` gets the ids of the read and the event `rename` only (R-TP18 row 1: its first event). A row of `F` that exists gets R-TP9 with its own ids (row 3 or 4). When the row of `F` has another `repository_id`, `F` gets no write and the claimed row does not become `moved`, but the claimed row still gets R-TP9 row 2: R-TP9 compares the read with the claimed row (Q379), and the reason of R-PU30 is the same (added 2026-11-03 after the review of slice 119c). Reasons: R-PU30 shows `transferred to <owner>` from the events of the row of the header entry, and a header has the old name, so the old row must have the `transfer` event. An ingest of the old URL (R-TP18 row 5) and the next check of the `moved` row (Q375) apply R-TP9 to that row anyway, so a row that keeps its old owner id gets the event later, at a time that shows nothing. All in one batch, with the SQL guards and `changes() > 0` for each event (R-TP19), so a second run adds no event (decided 2026-11-02, slice 119c, Registry R-TP18, R-TP19). |

Open: none.
